# Plan: stratagems joins template-commit-reveal

Written 2026-09-30. One step per fresh agent context: each step below starts with what to read, says what to do and what "done" means, and ends by appending an entry to the **Progress log** at the bottom of this file and ticking its box. Do ONE step per context. The prompt that starts a step is in the next section, and it is the same prompt every time.

## Branches

**`main` stays untouched until the whole join is good** (the user, 2026-10-01). The plan and every step's work live on the integration branch **`join-template-commit-reveal`**, pushed to origin, which no worktree checks out. Each step branches from it into its own worktree, and when its acceptance criteria hold, fast-forwards the integration branch to its step branch. So the live copy of this file is `git show join-template-commit-reveal:work/plans/join-template-commit-reveal.md`, and the copy on `main` is stale by design. Every step pushes the integration branch to origin when it lands (the user, 2026-10-01), so origin's copy is the authority and a session on another machine starts from it. Nothing else is pushed without asking, in any repo. Merging the integration branch into `main` is the user's call, at the end (step 4 and step 5 say where).

## The prompt for a step

Paste this into a fresh session as it is. It picks the next step itself. To run a different step (for example U2 in parallel with steps 1 to 3), add one line after it: `Do step U2.`

```
You are working in the stratagems repository, /home/wighawag/dev/github/wighawag/stratagems, on the plan in work/plans/join-template-commit-reveal.md. The live copy of the plan is on the integration branch `join-template-commit-reveal`, NOT on main. Fetch first (`git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/stratagems fetch origin --prune`), make sure the local branch is not behind `origin/join-template-commit-reveal` (fast-forward it with `git fetch origin join-template-commit-reveal:join-template-commit-reveal` if it is, or create it from there if it is missing), then read the plan in full with `git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/stratagems show join-template-commit-reveal:work/plans/join-template-commit-reveal.md`.

Which step: if my message after this prompt names a step, do that one. Otherwise do the first step whose box is unticked in the plan's Steps list. If that step's section has no "Start here" block (only a paragraph), stop: it has to be specified first, so measure what it needs, propose its specification in the shape of the specified steps instead of doing it, and bring me each of its decisions as a numbered recommendation (what you recommend, and the alternative you rejected), so I can answer "go" or correct only the ones I disagree with. Do ONE step and nothing else.

First:
1. Check that the main checkout is clean (`git status`), that `main` is up to date with origin, and that the integration branch exists and contains origin/main (`git merge-base --is-ancestor origin/main join-template-commit-reveal`). If not, stop and tell me.
2. Read the step's "Start here" block and re-derive reality: the numbers in the plan are measurements from the day they were written. If a premise of the step no longer holds (a file moved, a version changed, the template changed), stop and tell me the discrepancy rather than building on it.
3. Check that every step the chosen step needs is ticked: what its own text says it needs, and otherwise every step above it in the Steps list. If not, stop and tell me.

Rules:
- Work in a git worktree under ~/dev/worktrees/<repo>/<branch>, never beside a repo. In stratagems, branch from `join-template-commit-reveal`, on the branch the step names, or `step-<id>` if it names none. Never commit to main or to the integration branch directly.
- Commit in small steps. The only push you make without asking is the integration branch, at the end (below). Push nothing else, in any repo, and do not open a PR or touch main without asking me. A step that works in another repo (the template tree) follows that repo's own AGENTS.md and work protocol there.
- Ask before any destructive command. Never `git stash` inside a conflicted merge.
- Bound every exploratory shell command with `timeout` and cap its output with `head`. Never grep node_modules, dist, .git or minified bundles, and never run an unbounded regex over a generated or minified file.
- Before starting anything on a port, check it is free (`ss -ltn`) and what holds it; 8545 and 8546 belong to another project. Stop every proxy, node, dev server and browser you start, by its process group, before you finish.
- Never write an em dash character, and do not hard-wrap Markdown prose.

When the step's acceptance criteria all hold: append a dated entry to the Progress log (what was done, the measurements, the commits, anything that surprised you), tick the step's box, commit, then land it: fetch origin and bring the local integration branch up to `origin/join-template-commit-reveal` as in the first paragraph, fast-forward it to your step branch with `git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/stratagems fetch . <step-branch>:join-template-commit-reveal`, and push it with `git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/stratagems push origin join-template-commit-reveal`. Both only ever fast-forward. If either refuses because another step landed first, rebase your step branch onto the integration branch keeping both log entries, and try again; never force. If the criteria do not all hold, do not tick and do not fast-forward: say what is missing.

End with a report: what changed, the measurements against the step's acceptance criteria, anything you could not verify, a `## Decisions` block for every non-obvious choice, and which step comes next. If what you learned changes the order or the content of later steps, fix the Steps list and the step's text in the plan, and if it changes how a session must start or finish, fix this prompt there too (it must stay paste-as-is, with nothing to fill in), and say what you changed. The next session gets this same prompt, unchanged.
```

## Where things stand (2026-09-30)

- **stratagems** (`main` a7d0c5f, pushed): etherfold 0.11.0 indexer (`indexer/`, a worker in `web/`, verified against the alpha1 oracle), contracts on Hardhat 3.18 + hardhat-deploy 2.0.30 + rocketh 0.23.1, web on **Svelte 4** + SvelteKit 2.5 + Vite 5 + `web3-connection` + twgl.js, pnpm 9. Workspaces: `common`, `contracts`, `indexer`, `web`, `helper-services/{fuzd,missiv,secp256k1-db}`. Checks green: `svelte-check` 0 errors, contracts 69 tests pass (the ERC721 suite is skipped, as it always was), `pnpm format:check`.
- **template-commit-reveal** (`main` 2694b172, 2026-09-28): stems from jolly-roger `integration`; web on **Svelte 5** + SvelteKit 2.70 + Vite 8 + Tailwind 4 + TypeScript ~6.0 + vitest 4 + Playwright, `@etherplay/connect`; contracts on Hardhat ^3.13 + hardhat-deploy ^2.0.24 + rocketh ^0.19 (OLDER than stratagems'); pnpm 10.28.1 (`packageManager`); workspaces `web`, `contracts`. Its offshoot config: `main` (stemBranch `integration`), `with/pixi-js`, `with/nft-identity`, `with/all`; verify `pnpm install && pnpm --filter ./web check && pnpm --filter ./web run test:unit`.
- **The template's own plan already places stratagems** (template repo, `git show work:work/specs/proposed/games-on-this-foundation.md`, Decision 1): stratagems stems from `template-commit-reveal@main` as a **dormant, compile-only member** that supplies its own twgl render host, participates in `check` and not in e2e, and exists partly as a cheap test that the seams fit ("a cascade that breaks stratagems' build has found something"). The same document lists the port's real size: a Svelte 4 to 5 migration, a connection-library swap, a state-model swap, a renderer swap, each "about the size of the whole conquest graft".
- **Shared history: none.** stratagems was not cloned from the template. The way in is one merge with `--allow-unrelated-histories`, as mandalas did (`c96382d9`, 2026-08-02, "Merge jolly-roger template history into mandalas": no rewrite, every SHA stays), after which every template update is an ordinary three-way merge.

### A trial merge, measured 2026-09-30

In a throwaway clone (`git merge --no-commit --allow-unrelated-histories` of template `main` into stratagems `main`):

- **40 add/add conflicts**, every one a path both repos created independently: the root `package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `.gitignore`, `README.md`; `dev/zellij*.kdl`; in `contracts/`: `package.json`, `hardhat.config.ts`, `rocketh/{config,deploy,environment}.ts`, `tsconfig.json`, `scripts/tsconfig.json`, `.env`, `.gitignore`, `.prettier*`, `remappings.txt`, `zellij.kdl`, and three Solidity files the template inherited from stratagems long ago (`src/game/internal/UsingVirtualTime.sol`, `src/utils/{PositionUtils,StringUtils}.sol`); in `web/`: `package.json`, `svelte.config.js`, `vite.config.ts`, `tsconfig.json`, `.env`, `.gitignore`, `.prettier*`, `README.md`, `src/app.{d.ts,html}`, `src/web-config.json`, `src/routes/+layout.{svelte,ts}`, `src/routes/+page.svelte`, `static/preview.png`.
- **668 files arrive cleanly**: the framework (`web/src/lib/core` 134, `shadcn` 104, `ui` 43, `game` 40, `context` 11, `embedded` 9, `account` 7, `kit` 6, ...), its unit tests (`web/test`, ~170, including the boundary tests), the reference game (`web/src/lib/placement`, `view`, `onchain`, routes `explorer`, `contracts`, `transactions`, `offline`, `debug`, `web/e2e`), and the template's reference CONTRACTS (`contracts/src/game/{interfaces,internal,routes}/Game*`, `src/tokens`, `deploy/*`, `test/js/*`, `js/*`).
- **Two folder names collide in `web/src/lib`**: stratagems has `account/` and `ui/`, and so does the template. Clean adds would interleave two apps' files in the same folders.

### What a game drops from this template

reveal-or-die, the one game fully on the template, is the reference (`git -C ../reveal-or-die show main:.offshoot-omissions`): it drops the reference game's client (`web/src/lib/placement`, its tests, `game.e2e.ts`, `out-of-gas.e2e.ts`), the token and stake-sale contracts and deploy scripts, and a few tests, and keeps the framework. Its own game lives in `web/src/lib/world`. stratagems, whose contracts share nothing with the reference game, drops more: ALL of the template's reference contracts, deploy scripts and contract tests (the template's own rule: contracts are not inherited by games), and, until it adopts the app context, the routes that need it.

## The direction (the user, 2026-09-30)

**stratagems ends up as a small addition on the template, not as a second app beside it.** It uses the template's systems (app context, connection, clock, submission and recovery, render seam, contracts), and where stratagems needs something the template lacks, that feature is built IN THE TEMPLATE TREE, at its home, and arrives by cascade, rather than being kept in stratagems. So every port step (6 onwards) begins with the `reconcile-template-tree` skill's home test: would a sibling game want this? If yes, it lands upstream first (in the template repo, following that repo's own work protocol, with the user's approval), and the stratagems step then consumes it. What stays in stratagems is its game: the rules, its contracts' game logic, its renderer, its brand.

Candidates already visible, each to be judged when its step comes (not decided here):

- **The commit-reveal contracts as a reusable base** (decision 5 below): the template's `UsingGameInternal` mixes the mechanism (reserve, commitment, hash chain of chunks, cycle policy, advance, forfeit, delegation) with its reference game (`_place`, `Placement[]` in `reveal`), so no game can build on it yet.
- **An indexed state layer** (stratagems' etherfold indexer and `StateFollower`): the template's plan already says the layer a game needs tracks its cycle length, and that a 24-hour game wants an indexer while a fast one reads logs, which makes it a candidate layer rather than stratagems-only code.
- **Automatic reveal through fuzd**, and **missiv** messaging: jolly-roger's service layers (`with/*`) are where the template's plan puts such services.
- **A client-side simulation of the contract** (stratagems' 700-line TypeScript reimplementation in `common/`; catacombs does the same with a local EVM): the template's plan notes both, so the seam for it may belong upstream.

## Decisions (confirmed by the user, 2026-09-30)

1. **Where stratagems' current app lives after the merge: `web/src/lib/stratagems/`**, moved there BEFORE the merge (step 2), so the template's `account/` and `ui/` land without interleaving, and so the port later dissolves one folder rather than untangling two. (reveal-or-die's game lives in `web/src/lib/world`; that name is taken by meaning, not by path.)
2. **The merge keeps stratagems' app shell** (its `+layout`, `+page`, `(pages)/`, routes) and the template's FRAMEWORK code and its unit tests, and omits the reference game (client and contracts) plus the template routes that need the template's app context. Those routes come back when the port adopts the app context (step 6). The alternative, taking the template's shell in the merge and mounting the game inside it, IS the port, and does not fit in one merge. **Confirmed, as a transition only**: the port then moves stratagems onto the template's system (see "The direction").
3. **Contracts toolchain versions: bump the template upstream first** (step U), so the merge's `contracts/package.json` and `rocketh/*` conflicts are about the game and not about versions. Without it, the merge takes stratagems' newer versions and every later cascade conflicts on them until the template catches up.
4. **The node's verify is the template's**: `pnpm install && pnpm --filter ./web check && pnpm --filter ./web run test:unit`, per the template's plan (check, not e2e). stratagems' own heavier checks (contracts tests, the alpha1 browser verification) stay stratagems' to run.
5. **The merge keeps stratagems' contracts; later, stratagems' contracts are rebuilt on the template's commit-reveal system.** At the merge the template's reference contracts, their deploy scripts and tests are omitted (they are a reference GAME today, and would deploy a second game beside stratagems'). Step 10 then happens in two parts: first, upstream, the template separates its commit-reveal mechanism into a game-agnostic base that a game extends with its own action type and resolution hook (the reference game becomes its first user); then stratagems un-omits that base and rebuilds its game logic on it. That is a new deployment; alpha1 stays as history.

```sh

git -C ../template-commit-reveal log --oneline -1 main
git -C ../template-commit-reveal show main:AGENTS.md | head -120
git -C ../template-commit-reveal show main:CONTEXT.md | head -80

git -C ../reveal-or-die show main:.offshoot-omissions | grep -v '^#' | grep -v '^$'
git -C ../template-commit-reveal show work:work/tasks/done/port-bomber-world-onto-reveal-or-die.md | head -120   # the closest precedent: a port with a trial merge
```

**Do.** No commits to code. Record, in the Progress log:

- the green baseline of stratagems `main`, each as a number: `pnpm install` (with the pnpm it uses today), `pnpm --filter ./web check`, `pnpm --filter ./contracts test`, `pnpm format:check`, `MODE=alpha1 pnpm --filter ./web build`;
- the template's baseline the same way, from a worktree of `template-commit-reveal@main` under `~/dev/worktrees/template-commit-reveal/<branch>`, since that is what step 4's result is judged against: `pnpm install`, `pnpm --filter ./web check`, `pnpm --filter ./web run test:unit` (its verify);
- the trial merge again (the numbers above may have moved): in a clone under `/tmp`, count the add/add conflicts and the clean adds by area, as the table above does. Remove the clone afterwards;
- which template files under `web/src/lib/{core,game,context,kit,embedded,account,ui}` import the reference game (`placement`, `view`, `onchain`, the `Game` deployment) or `$lib/deployments`, because those decide what compiles once the reference game is omitted. A list, with the import that ties each to the reference game.

### Step 1: pnpm 10

Branch `pnpm-10`.

**Start here.** Step 0's log entry. The template's root `package.json` (`packageManager: pnpm@10.28.1`, `pnpm.onlyBuiltDependencies`, `pnpm.overrides`). stratagems' root `package.json` has a `pnpm` field that pnpm 10+ no longer reads (`supportedArchitectures`, `overrides`, `auditConfig`: pnpm warns on every command), and `.github/workflows/{ipfs,github-pages}.yml` pin pnpm 9.

**Do.** `packageManager: "pnpm@10.28.1"`; move what the `pnpm` field still needs to where pnpm 10 reads it (`pnpm-workspace.yaml` settings); `onlyBuiltDependencies` for every package that genuinely needs its install script (pnpm 10 runs none by default: check `contracts` (hardhat, solc), `esbuild`, `better-sqlite3`/libsql if present, `helper-services/*`); CI workflows use the `packageManager` field and do NOT also set the setup action's `version:` (both at once fails with `ERR_PNPM_BAD_PM_VERSION`). Regenerate the lockfile.

**Done when** every baseline from step 0 is unchanged, `pnpm install` prints no ignored-field warning and no skipped build script that matters, and the `indexer:index` path still works (`pnpm indexer:build alpha1` at least; a full fold is not needed).

### Step 2: stratagems' app moves to `web/src/lib/stratagems/`

Branch `lib-stratagems`. Needs decision 1 confirmed.

**Start here.** `web/src/lib/` today holds `account actions blockchain config.ts render state ui`; `web/svelte.config.js` aliases `$data`, `$external`, `$utils`, `web-config`; `web/src/utils/` and `web/src/data/` are outside `lib`.

**Do.** `git mv` the whole of `web/src/lib/*` into `web/src/lib/stratagems/` (pure moves, so git tracks them as renames), then fix imports (`$lib/...` becomes `$lib/stratagems/...`). Nothing else: no refactor, no rename of anything else. Keep the commit a move plus import fixes so `git log --follow` works and the merge in step 4 sees renames.

**Done when** `svelte-check` 0 errors, the alpha1 build succeeds, `git show --stat` of the move commit reports renames (similarity 100% for most files), and the app starts under `vite dev` and draws the board (a manual or scripted look is enough; the full browser verification runs after step 3).

### Step 3: the web toolchain the template runs on

Branch `web-toolchain`. The largest risk before the merge, so it gets its own step and the full browser verification.

**Start here.** Template `web/package.json` versions (step 0 recorded them). Svelte 5 runs Svelte 4 syntax components in legacy mode, so this step does NOT rewrite components into runes; it moves the toolchain so the template's framework code can later compile beside them. `web3-connection@0.1.39` declares a Svelte 4 peer: check what it actually does under Svelte 5 before assuming it works.

**Do.** Svelte 5, `@sveltejs/kit` and `@sveltejs/vite-plugin-svelte` at the template's versions, Vite 8, `svelte-check` 4, TypeScript at the template's version (~6.0), `@sveltejs/adapter-static` compatible. Fix what breaks (`svelte-migrate` can help with mechanical changes, but read every change: logic must not move). Tailwind is NOT added here: it arrives with the template's framework in step 4.

**Done when**, all measured:

- `svelte-check` 0 errors, `pnpm format:check` green, `MODE=alpha1 pnpm --filter ./web build` succeeds;
- `pnpm --filter ./indexer compare:alpha1` still answers every question the same (the indexer is untouched, so this is a guard, not a test of this step);
- the real-Chromium verification passes: `MODE=alpha1 pnpm --filter ./web build`, then `VERIFY_ETH_NODE=http://127.0.0.1:<port> pnpm --filter ./web verify:alpha1` through the caching proxy (`indexer/oracle/2026-09-29-alpha1/README.md` says how; start it with `--cache ~/dev/github/wighawag/stratagems/indexer/data/rpc-cache` so a worktree uses the main checkout's cache, about an hour of RPC otherwise). Both tests: publication versus self-indexed (about 40 min) and the two-tab election;
- the app starts from the publication under `vite dev` too.

### Step U (optional): the contracts toolchain at its home, in the template tree

Needs decision 3 confirmed. Uses the `reconcile-template-tree` skill; this is template-tree work, not stratagems work, and changes other repos: ask before each push.

**Start here.** `offshoot-fanout status` for the tree that holds template-commit-reveal (find the root with `offshoot-fanout discover ~/dev/github/wighawag --remote stem`). Where is the contracts toolchain's home: `template-ethereum-contracts`, jolly-roger, or template-commit-reveal? (Both templates were on rocketh ^0.19 on 2026-09-30.)

**Do.** Bump hardhat to ^3.18, hardhat-deploy to ^2.0.30, rocketh to ^0.23.1 and the `@rocketh/*` packages to their latest at that home; apply what stratagems' migration found (commit 09a8ae6 and its message: `-e` not `-n` on the rocketh CLIs, `ChainInfo` no longer exported by `@rocketh/export`, the export writes `chain` instead of `chainId`/`genesisHash`/`chainInfo`, rocketh 0.23 refuses a chain-id mismatch outside a fork, the gas-estimate helpers are gone); cascade with `offshoot-fanout`, verify every node.

**Done when** the template's `contracts/package.json` carries the same versions as stratagems', every node of its subtree is green, and the user has approved the pushes.

**Decided by the user, 2026-10-24** (before the step ran):

1. **The home is jolly-roger**, the root of this tree's contracts. Bump it on jolly-roger `main`, then cascade to every node that has contracts: jolly-roger's branches (`with/local-signer`, `with/hosted-account`, `with/embedded-chain`, `integration`, `website`), template-commit-reveal's (`main`, `with/pixi-js`, `with/nft-identity`, `with/all`), reveal-or-die, bleeps and mandalas.
2. **template-ethereum-contracts is updated too**, but it shares no history with this tree, so it is an independent hand port, done after jolly-roger, in its own commits, following that repo's own work protocol (it has none written down: a branch, verified, then its `main`).
3. **bomber-world is left out of the cascade** (`offshoot-fanout --ignore bomber-world`): an agent is working in it. It is reported as deferred, and picks the bump up at its next cascade from reveal-or-die, together with U2.
4. **Every push is asked for, per repo**; the user may approve them all at once at the end.

Found while doing it: `@rocketh/web` (the embedded chain's in-tab deploy, `web/package.json` on jolly-roger `with/embedded-chain` and below) has to move with rocketh, since 0.19.23 pins rocketh ^0.21 as its peer and brings `@rocketh/core` 0.21; 0.19.27 is the release for rocketh 0.23. Its home is `with/embedded-chain`, the branch that introduces it.

### Step 4: the merge

Branch `join-template`, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/join-template`. Needs decisions 1, 2 and 4 confirmed, steps 1 to 3 done (and U, or a recorded decision to skip it).

**Start here.** Step 0's measurements, especially the list of framework files tied to the reference game. The mandalas merge message (`git -C ../mandalas log -1 --format=%B c96382d9`) for the shape of the result and of the message. The bomber-world task's hazards (step 0 reads it). The template's `scripts/{apply-omissions.sh,check-omissions.mjs,check-dangling-imports.mjs}` and `web/test/offshoot-omissions.test.ts`, which arrive with the merge.

**Do.**

1. `git remote add stem git@github.com:wighawag/template-commit-reveal.git && git fetch stem --prune`, then `git merge --no-commit --allow-unrelated-histories stem/main`.
2. Resolve the 40 add/add conflicts BY INTENT, file by file, and read every result:
   - stratagems' side for its game and its shell: the three Solidity files (they compile into the deployed alpha1 bytecode), `+layout.*`, `+page.svelte`, `web-config.json`, `preview.png`, `app.html` unless the template's is needed by the framework, README (stratagems');
   - a real merge of both for configuration: root and package `package.json`s (scripts of both; stratagems' workspaces plus the template's; the template's framework dependencies, including Tailwind, next to stratagems'), `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, `svelte.config.js` (both alias sets; the template's `$ui` and friends), `vite.config.ts` and `vite.plugins.ts` (the template's `extraPlugins()` slot; stratagems' worker config), `tsconfig.json`s, `.gitignore`s, `.env`s, `.prettier*`, `hardhat.config.ts` and `rocketh/*` (stratagems' accounts, environments and tags; the template's shape);
   - the lockfile is never hand-merged: take either side and run `pnpm install`.
3. Omit what decision 2 says to omit, with `git rm`, and list EVERY omitted path in `.offshoot-omissions` with its reason (the template's format: a directory entry covers its contents; entries are stem-relative). At least: the template's reference contracts, deploy scripts, contract tests and `contracts/js` unless something kept needs it (decision 5: their reason says they are omitted UNTIL step 10b rebuilds stratagems on the template's commit-reveal base, so nobody reads the entry as a permanent choice); `web/src/lib/placement` and whatever else step 0 found tied to the reference game; the routes that need the template's app context; the reference game's e2e and its fixtures. A framework file that fails to compile only because of the omitted reference game is a finding about the seam (the template's plan says exactly that is what this member is for): record it in the log and in the report, and resolve it the least invasive way (omit it, with a reason), never by editing framework files.
4. Run `node scripts/check-dangling-imports.mjs` once `package.json` resolves and installs, and `node scripts/check-omissions.mjs` (every absent stem path is listed).
5. The template's boundary tests (`web/test/*-boundary.test.ts`) will judge stratagems' legacy code by the template's conventions. Do not weaken them. Where one fails on stratagems' code, measure how many files and decide per test: exempt `web/src/lib/stratagems/` with a stated reason and an end date (the port), or fix. Record which.
6. Commit the merge with a message in the mandalas shape: why the histories are joined, what the template brings, what stratagems keeps, what is omitted and why, and what the merge fixed or found. `git log -1 --format='%h parents: %p'` must show two parents.

**Done when**, all measured, in the worktree:

- the node's verify passes: `pnpm install && pnpm --filter ./web check && pnpm --filter ./web run test:unit` (framework tests included);
- stratagems' own checks are what they were: contracts tests (69 pass; run them as `CI=1 pnpm --filter ./contracts test`, or vitest stays in watch mode after passing and never exits), `pnpm format:check`, `MODE=alpha1 pnpm --filter ./web build`, `compare:alpha1`, and the real-Chromium `verify:alpha1` (both tests) plus the `vite dev` start;
- `check-omissions` and `check-dangling-imports` are clean, and `web/test/offshoot-omissions.test.ts` passes;
- the merge commit has two parents, and nothing in `web/src/lib/{core,game,kit}` differs from `stem/main` (`git diff --stat stem/main -- web/src/lib/core web/src/lib/game web/src/lib/kit` is empty), unless a difference is recorded as a finding with its reason.

Then ask the user before merging anything into `main` and pushing: the integration branch (see "Branches") is what carries the result until then.

**If step U2 has landed, step 4 changes** (re-derive this in step 4's own context; it is a prediction from the prototype, not a measurement on stratagems): stratagems does not omit the framework files that read the app context, because they no longer reach the reference game. It supplies its own game module (for example `web/src/lib/stratagems/game.ts`, exporting what the template's game module `placement/game.ts` exports: `createAppContext`, `AppMembers`, `SIGNER_GRANT`, `delegationRegistry`, `operationScope`, `startDiagnostics`; U2 renamed the prototype's `createGameContext`/`GameMembers`/`$game` to jolly-roger's names) and points the `$app-specific` alias at it, in `svelte.config.js`, which step 4 merges by hand anyway. What it still omits is the reference game itself (`placement/`, the offline world, `routes/{play,offline}`, the template's `+page.svelte`) and the tests that test the reference game's fixtures or guard the framework's use (`test/lib/embedded/{world,deployments}.test.ts`, `test/lib/game/render/canvas2d.svelte.test.ts`, `test/identity-boundary.test.ts` until step 8, `test/render-host-boundary.test.ts` until step 9), each with its reason.

### Step U2: the game becomes a slot, and the delegation registration is typed

In the template tree, so it follows `reconcile-template-tree` and each repo's own work protocol, and every push is asked for. Needs step 0's finding 4 (and the 2026-10-01 log entry, which is the measurement and the prototype). Does NOT need steps 1 to 3, and can run in parallel with them.

**Why.** Step 0 measured that the reference game reaches 65 of the template's 240 framework files, nearly all through the app context: `context/types.ts` names the game's types, and `context/game.ts` is the reference game's composition, which every game rewrites (reveal-or-die: 992 changed lines against `with/all`, about 620 of them comments). A game that is not a variant of the reference game, which stratagems is the first of, cannot compile the framework without editing it. The home test answers yes: reveal-or-die and bomber-world conflict on those files on every cascade.

**Start here.** The prototype: template-commit-reveal branch `proto/game-slot` (4 commits on `main` b4d101ba, pushed to origin 2026-10-01 at 4e1ee404, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/template-commit-reveal/proto/game-slot`), and `/tmp/stub-test.sh` with `/tmp/stub-game.ts` if they still exist (the log entry describes both, so they can be rebuilt). Then `git diff stem/integration main -- web/src/lib/context web/src/lib/ui/delegation web/src/lib/ui/credits/top-up-flow.ts` in the template: what is already jolly-roger's.

**Do**, as separate changes, each at its home:

1. **The delegation registration is typed** (home: jolly-roger, where `ui/delegation/{register-delegate,registration}.ts` and `ui/credits/top-up-flow.ts` live byte-identical or nearly; the template's `game/acquire/{acquire,authorise}.ts` follow by cascade plus a template commit). `RegistrationRequest` becomes a union per entry point with `args: ContractFunctionArgs<typeof DELEGATION_ABI, 'payable', F>`; `submitRegistration` and its `Writer` take `abi: typeof DELEGATION_ABI`; the three `as unknown as RegistrationWriter` casts go, because viem's tracked client is assignable to the typed writer as it is. Callers pass `delegation.registry`, never a contract's own ABI. Prototype commit d3df3490.
2. **The app is a slot, and core names no app type** (home: jolly-roger, whose `core.ts` already has `AppContext = {onchainState: Context['onchainState'], ...}`): `Context = CoreContext & AppMembers`, with `AppMembers` taken from ONE app-owned module through an alias, the way `$ui` already works (`svelte.config.js`); `AppContext` states the requirement (`onchainState: {update(): Promise<unknown>; status: Readable<PollingStatus>}`, `start?`) instead of naming the app's store; `createCoreContext` returns `CoreContext & Omit<App, 'start'>`, built by spreading the app's members whole (spreading a generic rest and putting one member back does not type-check). The alias name is jolly-roger's to choose (its app is not a game; the prototype calls it `$game`; it landed as `$app-specific`). Prototype commits d1416564 and 5accc66b.
3. **The reference game moves behind the slot** (home: template-commit-reveal): `context/game.ts` to `placement/context.ts`, `debug/diagnostics.ts` to `placement/diagnostics.ts`, and a `placement/game.ts` that declares the game to the framework: `createGameContext`, `SIGNER_GRANT`, `GameMembers`, `startDiagnostics`, and the two contract choices as FUNCTIONS of `TypedDeployments` (`delegationRegistry`, `operationScope`), not names, so naming a contract the deployment lacks is a compile error in the game. `game/acquire/acquire.ts` reads the address and chain from `deps.delegation.registry`; the poller (`onchain/state.ts`) takes `cycleDuration` instead of casting `contracts.Game.linkedData`, and loses its `deployments` parameter. Prototype commits d1416564 and 4e1ee404.
4. **Cascade** to reveal-or-die and bomber-world: their `context/game.ts` moves into `world/` the same way, and their `.offshoot-omissions` gains nothing new. This is where the cost lands, and it is a RENAME of their most conflicted file: plan it with the template's maintainer, do not discover it during a cascade.
5. **NOT in this step, and named so it is not forgotten:** extracting the commit-reveal skeleton that every game repeats (chain time, cycle trackers, advance, submission, recovery, held board, setup gate, `start()`'s re-check wiring: about 300 code lines, 123 of the body's 183 unchanged in reveal-or-die) into a framework function in `game/core/`. That is what makes step 6 "supply the game's parts" rather than "write a 900-line composition", so it belongs before step 6, as its own upstream step.

**Done when**, all measured: the template's verify green with unchanged counts; the three teeth from the log (a `number` deadline, a dropped argument, `Game.abi` as the registry) each fail `check`; the stub test passes: in a throwaway worktree of the template, the reference game removed, `Game` renamed in `deployments.ts`, `$app-specific` (the prototype's `$game`) pointed at a stub with no contract named `Game`, and `check` reports 0 errors in `web/src` (the prototype: 0); every node of the subtree is green after the cascade; the user has approved every push.

**The subtree is wider than parts 1 to 4 say** (found 2026-10-23): part 2 lands on jolly-roger `main`, so its cascade reaches every node under it, not only template-commit-reveal's: jolly-roger's `with/local-signer`, `with/hosted-account`, `with/embedded-chain`, `integration` and `website`, and two other repos that stem from jolly-roger `main`, **bleeps and mandalas**, which conflict on it (3 and 5 files under `web/src/lib/context/`, measured with `git merge-tree`). Their resolutions are their maintainer's call, the same as reveal-or-die's.

**How it landed** (2026-10-24, the log entry of that date): every jolly-roger and template node fast-forwarded to its verified preview, the alias renamed `$app-specific` first; reveal-or-die, bleeps and mandalas merged by hand and verified; **bomber-world deferred** at the user's request (an agent is working in it), so it picks U2 up at its next cascade from reveal-or-die, which the trial measured as clean.

**What this step decides about delegation.** The framework stays delegation-only: `delegationRegistry` returns an address, it is not optional. stratagems gets delegation when step 10b rebuilds it on the template's commit-reveal contracts, which include `UsingDelegation` (the user, 2026-10-01: the port adopts the template's commit-reveal system). Until then its `delegationRegistry` names a contract that compiles and never runs, which holds only while stratagems' shell does not mount the template's context. **So step 6 (adopting the app context and connection) depends on 10b, or on delegation reaching stratagems' contracts some other way first.** The order of steps 6 to 11 was reopened by this, and decided on 2026-10-24: see "The port proper: order and measurements".

### The port proper: order and measurements (specified 2026-10-24)

The order was decided with the user on 2026-10-24, from the measurements below (the log entry of that date has the method). Every step still starts with the home test from "The direction".

**What decided it.**

- **The contracts, mechanism by mechanism** (template-commit-reveal `main` 3fe2b29d, `contracts/src/game/internal/UsingGameInternal.sol`, against stratagems' alpha1 contracts). Both are routed (`*Commit`, `*Reveal`, `*Getters` routes), keep an ERC20 reserve, chain a commitment through `bytes24` heads one chunk at a time, forward a `payee` payment on commit and reveal (fuzd), and let anyone submit a reveal. What differs: the template's commitment carries a **bond** declared at commit time, the amount at risk for that turn; stratagems' carries none, and its stake is taken per placement at reveal (each placed gem is `NUM_TOKENS_PER_GEMS` held in the cell), with the whole reserve unwithdrawable while a commitment is open. The template has three cycle policies (`_cycle()`), a permissionless unanimous `advanceCycle`, attendance (who the cycle waits for), the `_playerOf` delegation seam with a `GameDelegation` route, a uint256 identity, a chunk size set at deployment and ONE hash encoding; stratagems has a timed `_epoch()` only, `msg.sender` identity, a constant chunk of 32 and TWO encodings (the last chunk drops `furtherMoves`). Forfeit: the template takes the bond in one call; stratagems walks the chain with the secret and burns per move, or lets the player burn their whole reserve. stratagems has things the template does not: EIP-2612 `permit`, a reveal paid from the wallet (`useReserve` false), `poke`, cells as ERC721, the rewards generator, a debug route.
- **The first commit-reveal rule is broken by alpha1, through one move the client never offers.** stratagems' resolution reads state another reveal of the same epoch writes (`epochWhenTokenIsAdded == epoch`, `_ownerOf(position) == player`, `life`). Those reads are not an optimisation: they ARE the collision rule (two placements on one cell in one epoch make it Evil, stake 2, both kept) and the owner's right to re-place or remove their own cell. Replayed in every order of three players over random epochs on a 3x3 board, with `common/`'s TypeScript copy of the contract (board, owners and reward points; token payouts are not in that model): **0 of 60,000** trials change with the order when every move is one the client allows (`web/src/lib/stratagems/actions/ActionHandler.ts`), and **659 of 3,000** do when every move the contract accepts is allowed. All of the difference comes from one class: a coloured move onto a cell another player owns, which the contract accepts and the client never builds (10,000 trials each: that class alone 2,358 differ; removing any cell, or colouring your own full-life cell, 0). What that move does, measured on one cell: B takes A's live cell for one gem, A's gem stays in the contract with nobody's name on it, and A KEEPS the reward points for the cell (the generator is never told), so points are counted twice. In one epoch where A re-places their own cell and B overwrites it, A-then-B gives B the cell and B-then-A makes it Evil with different points: whoever pays more gas decides. Refusing that move against the CURRENT board does not fix it (2,197 of 10,000 still differ), because it breaks the collision symmetry: the first of two placements on an empty cell would own it and the second be refused. The rule the user decided (2026-10-24) is judged against the board as it stood when the player committed, which no reveal of the same cycle can have changed yet. The scripts are `join-template-commit-reveal/order-check.ts` and `overwrite.ts` beside this file.
- **A second alpha1 defect**: `acknowledgeMissedReveal(player, ...)` burns from the CALLER's reserve (`_tokensInReserve[msg.sender] -= amount`), so whoever acknowledges another player's missed reveal pays for it, or reverts.
- **The client skeleton, sized.** The template's composition (`web/src/lib/placement/context.ts`, 926 lines, 440 without comments) against reveal-or-die's (`web/src/lib/world/context.ts`, 1238, 535) and bomber-world's (1264, 550): 307 of the template's 440 code lines are unchanged in reveal-or-die, and in the `createGameContext` body alone 181 of 260; bomber-world differs from reveal-or-die by 1 line removed and 16 added. The skeleton reaches the contract only through readers it is handed (cycle, attendance, advance, board, commitment), so it does not need a contract base to exist. stratagems needs one seam it does not have: its board comes from the etherfold indexer, not the poller, so the skeleton must take the board store rather than build the poller.
- **reveal-or-die's contracts are another design**: its `UsingGameInternal` differs from the template's by 1,422 lines (avatar custody, no reserve, no chunks, a manual `_moveToNextPhase`), and its stake is the avatar's life: `_getResolvedAvatar` kills an avatar that has gone more than `numMissesAllowed` cycles (3 in `rocketh/config.ts`) without a REVEAL, since only `_reveal` moves `lastCycleNumber`, so a missed reveal counts as a missed turn. What its `_acknowledgeMissedReveal` does is only clear the slot (its `TODO burn / stake`), so withholding one reveal costs one of the allowed misses and no more; whether a missed reveal should kill outright is the game's choice (the user, 2026-10-24: it should in a harsh game, and is too harsh for the demo), which is exactly what the base's `_forfeit` seam has to let a game say. bomber-world inherits them. No repo in the tree has committed deployment records, so no stake is at risk anywhere but alpha1.
- **alpha1 is dormant**: the oracle's README records the last alpha1 log at Base block 23,303,136 (about December 2024) and calls the game discontinued, so no commitment can be in flight and discontinuing it costs no player a stake. Its account storage key (`_account_<address>_<chainId>_<genesisHash>`) has no contract address in it, so a new deployment on the same chain would load alpha1's records.
- **What a new deployment changes**: the indexer is keyed by the contracts' names (`INDEXED_CONTRACTS = ['Stratagems', 'GemsGenerator']`) and handles events by name (`on<Event>`), and the template's events differ in shape; `indexer/src/stratagems-contract.ts` (601 lines) is a third implementation of the game rules beside the Solidity and `common/src/stratagems.ts` (707); `compare:alpha1`, its oracle and `verify:alpha1` are alpha1's, and the new deployment has no oracle. `epoch` appears 268 times in `contracts/src` (14 files), 14 in `contracts/test`, 117 in `common/src`, 151 in `indexer/src`, 273 in `web/src/lib/stratagems`.
- **Contracts were not inherited in this tree** (template-commit-reveal `work:HANDOFF.md`: "Contracts are NOT inherited: every game writes its own"). The user decided on 2026-10-24 to change that for the commit-reveal mechanism (10a-contract), which is decision 5 made at its home.

**Why this order.** The client skeleton is independent of any contract and has three users already, so it goes first and alone. The contract base is a bigger decision with one existing user, so it is its own step, and reveal-or-die's adoption of it is NOT in its acceptance: it is proposed there as a backlog task of the template, since reveal-or-die would benefit (a forfeit it can configure, chunks) but it is a rewrite of contracts bomber-world inherits. stratagems' contracts come next because step 6 needs delegation (step U2's last paragraph) and the composition needs the contract's surface. Steps 6, 7's client half and 8's core are the same file once the skeleton exists, so they are one step. The renderer and the conventions follow, as before.

### Step 10a-client: the client skeleton, in the template tree

In the template tree, so it follows `reconcile-template-tree` and each repo's own work protocol, and every push is asked for. Its task text lives on template-commit-reveal's `work` branch, `work/tasks/backlog/the-commit-reveal-skeleton.md` (drafted from here on 2026-10-24); that file is the brief, this is stratagems' summary of it. The stratagems side is branch `step-10a-client`, which carries only the log entry.

**Start here.**

```sh

git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/template-commit-reveal show origin/main:web/src/lib/placement/context.ts | wc -l   # 926
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/reveal-or-die show origin/main:web/src/lib/world/context.ts | wc -l              # 1238
```

**Do**, at template-commit-reveal `main` (branch `commit-reveal-skeleton`, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/template-commit-reveal/commit-reveal-skeleton`):

1. Re-derive the shared part: strip comments from both compositions and diff them; on 2026-10-24, 307 of 440 code lines unchanged, 181 of 260 in the body. If it has moved, say so before extracting.
2. Extract into one framework function under `game/core/` (the name is the template's to choose; the brief proposes `createCommitRevealGame` in `commit-reveal-game.ts`) what both compositions repeat: chain time, the cycle trackers and the phase derivations (`threePhase`, `boardBehindClock`, `phase`), the cycle advance, the per-identity submission storage, the derived secret signed by the local signer, the submission, the openable-actions bound, recovery, the held board and the view state, the setup gate's framework half (signed in, authorised), `readyToPlay`, and `start()`'s wiring (submission and advance start, the re-checks on identity change, on submission step and on cycle turnover, resume when gas arrives, the pending acquisition). The game supplies the rest as parameters: its config, its readers and advancer, its BOARD STORE (built by the game, so a poller in the reference game and an indexer in stratagems), its commit-reveal adapter and `buildCommitment`, its stake and acquisition, its missed-reveal reader and settler, its planning, its hold rule and merge, its renderer, what a click means, and any extra setup gate.
3. The reference game becomes the first user: `placement/context.ts` is the call plus the reference game's parts. Then cascade, per `reconcile-template-tree`: `with/pixi-js`, `with/nft-identity`, `with/all`, then reveal-or-die, the second user, whose `world/context.ts` becomes the call plus its game (avatar, position, enumeration recovery, controls, reveal outcome, and the framework code it grew locally, `onEachNewCycle` and `currentCycleNumber`, which go up with the skeleton if both compositions want them). bomber-world takes it from reveal-or-die like any other node: its agent is done (the user, 2026-10-25), and it has already caught up on U and U2 (see the log entry of 2026-10-25). stratagems takes it by cascade and does not use it until step 6: it only has to stay green.
4. Extend U2's stub test: a stub game composed WITH the skeleton, its board store not a poller, must `check` to 0 in `web/src`.

**Done when**, all measured:

### Step 10a-contract: the commit-reveal base contract, in the template tree

In the template tree, so it follows `reconcile-template-tree` and each repo's own work protocol, and every push is asked for. Its brief is a task on template-commit-reveal's `work` branch, `work/tasks/backlog/the-commit-reveal-base.md`, drafted from this text as the step's first commit there; this is stratagems' summary of it. The stratagems side is branch `step-10a-contract`, which carries the log entry and the cascade. Specified 2026-10-25 (the log entry of that date has the measurements).

**Decided** (the user, 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25):

- **A base that games KEEP**, reversing "contracts are not inherited" for the commit-reveal mechanism: the reserve, the bonded commitment, cancellation, the chain of chunks (one encoding, chunk size set at deployment), the cycle policies, advance, attendance and the tally, delegation through `_playerOf`, the membership pair callable from custody, and both settlements, with the template's existing seams (`_playerOf`, `_forfeit`, `_cycle`).
- **Its shape: a Solidity `library` over ERC-7201 namespaced storage, with a convenience abstract contract beside it**, the shape of `@etherplay/delegation` (`Delegation.sol` and `UsingDelegation.sol`), whose library the template's `_playerOf` already composes. It lives IN the template, under its own folder `contracts/src/commit-reveal/`, not in a package: the package is postponed, and the code is written so that the move is a change of import. Composition is preferred, not a strict rule.
- **A game's actions stay typed (option T).** Solidity has no generics, so the game keeps its own typed routes (`reveal(player, Move[] calldata, ...)`), computes the one encoding `bytes24(keccak256(abi.encode(secret, actions, furtherActions)))`, and hands the base the digest and the number of actions; the base checks the chunk against the head, advances the chain, and charges the cost the game's resolution reports against the bond. The ABI and the indexers stay typed, and the reference game's client hash does not change.
- **The bond may exceed what the turn costs** (option b). The reveal takes each chunk's cost from the bond and releases the surplus with the last chunk (already built: `_reveal` sets `commitment.bond = 0` on the last chunk). A missed reveal settled WITH the chunks may be settled by anyone: it walks the chain, forfeits the game's penalty for what was committed (a hook; stratagems: one gem per move) and returns the surplus to the player. One settled WITHOUT them may be settled only by the player or their delegate, and loses the whole remaining bond. **Nobody but the player ever receives any of it**: what is forfeited goes to the game's `_forfeit` (burnt, kept, or a custody seized), never to whoever settles.
- **The penalty is the game's, and a custody stake still loses on both routes**: `with/nft-identity` bonds zero, so "only what was committed" would cost it nothing and let a player escape a bad reveal by settling with their own chunks; its `_forfeit` seizes the avatar whichever route settles.
- **Liveness: nothing new, and nothing in this step.** A member whose past commitment nobody settles is waited for exactly like a member who does not commit, which exists today ("falls back to the clock when a member never commits"): under a timed policy the clock moves the cycle on and only early advance is lost, and today's one-call settlement does not remove a member either. Under the manual policy an unopened commitment already freezes the world and settlement is unreachable (the template's finding `a-derived-turn-is-a-wire-and-a-manual-cycle-cannot-settle-one-it-cannot-open.md`); the way out is the zero-timer forfeit that `games-on-this-foundation.md` already records ("someone can remove a member from the set the epoch waits for", bounded by unanimity of the others or wall-clock silence; the user also suggests the game's host as an admin), which is its own future item, not this step's.
- **The reference game over-bonds**: its client bonds a whole number of chunks rather than the exact cost, so the surplus settlement has a real user in the template, and the finding `the-bond-is-public-so-bonding-the-exact-cost-leaks-the-turn.md` closes.
- The reference game is the first user, a stub second game in the template's contract tests the second; reveal-or-die's adoption (its stake is the avatar's life, so its `_forfeit` would count a miss or kill, by configuration) is a template backlog task, not done here.

```sh
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/template-commit-reveal show origin/main:contracts/src/game/internal/UsingGameInternal.sol | wc -l   # 827, 357 code lines

git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/template-commit-reveal show origin/work:work/notes/findings/the-bond-is-public-so-bonding-the-exact-cost-leaks-the-turn.md
git -C ~/dev/github/wighawag/template-commit-reveal show origin/with/nft-identity:contracts/src/game/avatar/UsingAvatarIdentity.sol | head -220   # the custody game: _playerOf and _forfeit overridden
```

### Step 10b: stratagems' contracts on the base

On the integration branch's side only (no template push is expected: the base is already the template's, and this step consumes it). Branch `step-10b`, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-10b`. Specified 2026-10-25 (the log entry of that date has the measurements). Every proposal below was confirmed by the user on 2026-10-25, as written; the P numbers stay so the log's questions can be found.

**Decided** (the user, 2026-10-24 and 2026-10-25):

- **A new deployment on the base, alpha1 not supported after it.** stratagems keeps the base (`contracts/src/commit-reveal/`, its `.offshoot-omissions` entry goes) and rebuilds its game on it: placement, life, delta, collision, distribution and rewards become the game's resolution between `_openChunk` and `_closeChunk(chunk, cost)`; its own reserve, commitment, epoch clock and `UsingVirtualTime` go (the base has `TIME` and `_timestamp()`). `epoch` becomes `cycle` in the rewrite: contracts, `common/` and the indexer. The last commit that builds and verifies alpha1 is tagged `alpha1-final`; alpha1's deployment records stay on `main` as history; `compare:alpha1` and `verify:alpha1` leave stratagems' checks.
- **The order of reveals never matters.** A placement on a cell that was already there before the cycle's reveals is invalid and skipped; a placement on a cell placed by another reveal of the SAME cycle is a collision, which makes the cell Evil as alpha1 already does. "Already there" is judged against the board before any reveal of the cycle, never the current board (measured 2026-10-24: judging against the current board leaves 2,197 of 10,000 trials order-dependent).
- **The missed-reveal settlements are the base's**: shown its chunks, anyone settles and the penalty is one gem per committed move (alpha1's rate), the surplus returned; without them, `forfeitMissedReveal` (player or delegate only) takes the whole remaining bond and replaces `acknowledgeMissedRevealByBurningAllReserve`. That ends alpha1's `msg.sender` defect.
- **A contract test replays one cycle's reveals in more than one order, payouts included**, which `common/`'s model could not measure.
- The new deployment's client storage keys include the contract address.

**Decided** (the user, 2026-10-25, answering the questions of that day's log entry, each recommendation accepted as written):

- **P1, which cells are "already there"**: a cell that is ALIVE at the cycle's start (its life computed at the current cycle from its record as it stood before the cycle's first reveal touched it) AND owned by another player, or Evil. A player's own cells stay theirs to act on as today (re-place one at any life, remove one at full life), which was never order-dependent (0 of 10,000). Dead cells, anyone's, are free, and two placements on one in the same cycle collide. This changes what the client offers (today it never offers another player's dead cell) and is what alpha1's contract already accepted.
- **P2, payouts follow the same invariant**: found by reading, not measured: a dying cell's share is paid to `_ownerOf(neighbour)` at the moment the cell is first touched in the cycle, generator points go to `_ownerOf` at each touch, and an owner can change inside a cycle (a dead cell placed on, an owner removing a full-life cell, so `_ownerOf` becomes zero and the share stays in the contract). So who receives a death's share can depend on the order of reveals, and on whether a `poke` lands before or after a reveal. Decided: the rule covers payouts and points too, judged against the owners at the cycle's start, and the step designs the per-cell record that makes it so (the ERC721 owner slot already packs a block number); the randomized replay is the judge.
- **P3, the game parts that survive**: `poke` and `pokeMultiple` stay (the winnings panel collects through them); the ERC721 route stays read-only as it is (transfers already revert `NotImplemented`); the rewards generator and Gems stay unchanged, enabled by the deploy as today; the debug route stays for the tests, adapted; `useReserve` goes (the client always sends `true`, and the base charges the bond); EIP-2612 `permit` goes (the deploy gives the game a global approval on TestTokens, so the client's allowance check already passes without one); `makeCommitmentWithExtraReserve` stays as one transaction that deposits and commits.
- **P4, the chain and the deploy**: this step deploys nothing live. It adds the deploy scripts and an environment for the new deployment, and the generated files (`web/src/data/contracts.ts`, `web/src/lib/deployments.ts`, `indexer/src/contracts.ts`, `helper-services/fuzd/contracts.json`) come from the template's ephemeral deploy of the NEW contracts, not from alpha1's records. Which chain, and whether it reuses alpha1's TestTokens and GemsGenerator on Base or deploys fresh ones, is decided when step 6 makes the game playable.
- **P5, the legacy web client compiles against the new ABI and is not run**: its six contract call sites (`actions/commit`, `actions/commit-cancel`, `actions/reveal/index.ts`, `RevealPanel.svelte`, `state/balance.ts`, `ui/winnings/TokenToCollect.svelte`) and `common/src/commitment.ts` (the one encoding, `furtherActions` always hashed) are adapted so `check` passes, and `account-db.ts`'s key gains the contract address; nothing verifies the legacy client at runtime, because step 6 replaces it. The web's own `epoch` (its clock state, 111 identifier uses) is step 6's: no web file reads `common/`'s epoch-named fields today.
- **P6, the test runner**: the base's suite stays byte-identical to the stem's, so stratagems adds what it needs to run (`@nomicfoundation/hardhat-node-test-runner`, `earl`, a `test` network, `paths.tests.nodejs = 'test/js'`), and stratagems' own suites stay on vitest, which excludes `test/js`. The contracts `test` script runs both.
- **P7, the indexer keeps its own simulation** (`stratagems-contract.ts`), follows the contract, and is checked against it by the scenario tests' existing indexer comparison (`expectIndexedGridToMatch`) and by the randomized replay. Making the contract emit each touched cell's state, so the indexer needs no third copy of the rules, costs gas per neighbour and is its own redesign: not this step.
- **P8, alpha1 tooling that leaves**: `web/verify/` and the `verify:alpha1` script; `indexer/oracle/2026-09-29-alpha1/` and `compare:alpha1`; `scripts/stratagems-generated.mjs`'s alpha1 mode; the scripts under `contracts/scripts/` that call functions the new contracts no longer have. The other deployment folders (`alpha1_old`, `alpha1test`, `base`, `sepolia`, ...) stay untouched as history. Pushing the `alpha1-final` tag is asked for, like any push.
- **The defaults, accepted by the user without change**: identity is the base's `uint256 player` through `_playerOf` and a `StratagemsDelegation` route (`UsingDelegation`, as the template's `GameDelegation`), which is what `web/src/lib/stratagems/game.ts`'s `delegationRegistry` already names; cells' owners stay addresses; the cycle policy is `Timed` with alpha1's 23 h commit and 1 h reveal; `ACTIONS_PER_REVEAL` is 32 (alpha1's `MAX_NUM_MOVES_PER_HASH`); the client bonds one whole chunk (32 gems) capped at the reserve, which leaks nothing since the client caps a turn at 30 moves; `_forfeit` is overridden to debit the reserve and send what it takes to `BURN_ADDRESS`; a returned gem (removal) and neighbours' shares go to wallets as in alpha1, not to the reserve; stratagems' moves get an event of their own (`MovesRevealed(player, cycleNumber, moves)`, emitted between open and close), because the base's `CommitmentRevealed` carries no actions; the compiler stays 0.8.24 with viaIR (the base asks `^0.8.21`), and `@etherplay/delegation` ^0.1.1 and `solidity-kit` ^0.5.7 join `contracts/package.json`.

### Step 6: the shell, the connection and the composition

Specified 2026-10-26 (the log entry "2026-10-26, step 6 specified" has the measurements and the questions). The paragraph this section held is kept at its end, as what was asked. Step 6 is three steps, 6a, 6b and 6c, in that order.

**Decided** (the user, 2026-10-26, answering that entry's questions):

- **P1, P2**: the base's client moves upstream first (6a), and step 6 is 6a, 6b and 6c.
- **P3**: 6b renders with a stopgap immediate renderer on the template's canvas2d host; twgl stays step 9's.
- **P4**: the template's home page at `/`, byte-identical, and the game at `/play/`.
- **P5**: the offline world is part of the migration, as its own step (O, after 6c). Until then `/offline/` is a stratagems page saying the offline world is in construction, so the home page's entry leads somewhere.
- **P6**: the indexer worker is given the framework's provider (`connection.provider`), not the legacy wallet wiring. That provider uses `PUBLIC_NODE_URL` or the chain's RPC when there is one and falls back to the wallet's otherwise (`core/connection/remote.ts`), so the worker reads through the wallet exactly when the framework does. The indexer stays stratagems-local for now, to be revisited in a later step (it is an upstream candidate in "The direction").
- **P7**: the template's secret and storage, no tolerance for the legacy `_account_*` records (the new contracts have no deployment). The remote sync goes: `account-data`, `account-db` and `helper-services/secp256k1-db` leave in 6c; a sync service will come later from jolly-roger.
- **P8**: a local chain only until step D, a step of its own after step 8, decides the deployment.
- **P9**: the template's e2e harness and its 13 framework suites come back with a stratagems cycle suite, run by stratagems; the node's verify stays check and unit (decision 4).
- **P10**: missiv is dropped in 6c, until a messaging layer exists in jolly-roger.
- **P11**: the defaults as proposed: the brand in `web-config.json`, the template's `app.css` only in 6b, the palette into the theme and `css/reset.css` dropped in 6c, 6c's components in runes mode, the TestTokens claim screen as the way to get gems.

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-26 on the integration branch ae86fb73, `stem/main` 85814a55; the log entry has the method):

- **The legacy client is most of the web.** `web/src/lib/stratagems/` is 119 files, 6,463 lines of `.ts` and 4,753 of `.svelte` (63 components, Svelte 5 legacy mode), plus `web/src/utils/` (2,185 lines, imported by 66 files as `$utils`) and `web/src/css/`. Its shell (`routes/+layout.svelte` 89 lines, `+page.svelte` 83) mounts 21 overlays (menu, events, transactions, commitments, leaderboard, indexer, view state, missiv, welcome, admin, debug, reveal-phase information, modals, banners, install notice, WIP and erase notices, the connection UI, the action flow, the splash screen, the claim screen) and the twgl canvas. 33 files reach `web3-connection`, 7 directly and the rest through `$lib/stratagems/blockchain/connection`, whose `accountData` (`account-data.ts` 543 lines, `account-db.ts` 399) holds the secret, signed by the wallet with `personal_sign`, and syncs it remotely through `helper-services/secp256k1-db`.
- **The base's client is in the template, inside the reference game.** `web/src/lib/placement/` is 23 files, about 4,100 lines. About 1,530 of them talk only to the commit-reveal BASE, which every game keeps since 10a-contract: `cycle.ts` (41, `getCycle`), `advance.ts` (110, `getAttendance`, `advanceCycle`), `reserve.ts` (167, `getReserve`, the token's `balanceOf`, `withdrawFromReserve`), `missed-reveal.ts` (313, `getCommitment`, `getCycleNumber`, both settlements), `storage.ts` (184, the submission and the committed-turn record), `errors.ts` (47), `commit-reveal.ts` (670: the chunk chain, the commitment hash, `makeCommitment` with the whole-chunk bond, chunked `reveal`), and the bond and cost half of `config.ts`. stratagems' routes since 10b have EXACTLY those names and, apart from the action tuple in `reveal` and `acknowledgeMissedReveal`, the same signatures (`makeCommitment(uint256 player, bytes24 hash, uint256 bond, address payable payee)` is byte-identical in both). So stratagems needs all of that, and the only way to have it today is to copy 1,500 lines out of a directory it omits, which is the divergence-by-copy the tree keeps paying for. The home test answers yes: the base is kept by every game, so its client is too (the reference game now, stratagems next, reveal-or-die when it adopts the base, its backlog task).
- **The skeleton fits.** `createCommitRevealGame<T>(core, parts)` (807 lines) takes the board as a factory, and an indexer store that ignores `poll` is what the extended stub test checks. Its parts are what the reference game supplies from the modules above, plus the game's planning, hold, merge, renderer and click. The renderer part is a `GameRenderer<TSurface>` (`onAppStarted`, `onAppStopped`, `tick`), and the skeleton builds the camera.
- **Moves go through the local signer, assets through the wallet** (`CoreServices`: `signerExecutor` silent, `accountExecutor` prompting, `delegation` gating), which matches the base: the signer commits and reveals for the player through `StratagemsDelegation`, the player's wallet funds the reserve.
- **The framework's e2e suites are framework tests.** Of the template's 16 suites under `web/e2e/tests/`, only `game`, `offline` and `out-of-gas` are the reference game's; reveal-or-die keeps the other 13 unchanged and adds its own game's (`board`, `recover-submission`, `signer-out-of-gas`). They are all omitted here today, because stratagems has no template shell.
- **The two generated deployment files are already the same file**: `web/src/data/contracts.ts` and `web/src/lib/deployments.ts` are byte-identical (321,337 bytes, from the throwaway deploy), and only `blockchain/networks.ts` imports the first.
- **The template's home page is the game menu, "the same file in every game"**: `/` links to `/play/` (online) and `/offline/` (the in-tab chain world). reveal-or-die has both routes; stratagems has neither.
- **No deployment of the new contracts exists**, so no record of the new client's storage can be in flight: its keys and fields are free to choose once, now (AGENTS.md's storage rule starts at the first deploy).

#### Step 6a (in the template tree): the base's client

In the template tree, so it follows `reconcile-template-tree` and each repo's own work protocol, and every push is asked for. Its brief is a task on template-commit-reveal's `work` branch, drafted from this text as the step's first commit there. The stratagems side is branch `step-6a`, which carries the log entry and the cascade.

**Start here.**

Move the base's half into one framework module (its place and name are the template's to choose; `web/src/lib/game/commit-reveal/` beside the skeleton is the obvious one), generic over the game's action type and given the action's ABI encoding and the game's contract ABI as parameters, the way the skeleton is generic over `GameTypes`: the cycle and attendance readers and the advancer, the reserve, the chunk chain and the commitment hash (the base's ONE encoding, `furtherActions` always hashed), the adapter's commit (whole-chunk bond capped at the reserve) and chunked reveal, the missed-reveal reader and both settlements, the submission storage and the committed-turn record (its key and fields unchanged: they are a wire of the reference game), `SignerOutOfFundsError`, and the bond policy.
3. The reference game becomes its first user: `placement/` keeps its game and calls the module. Its ABI is unchanged, so its e2e is the judge.
4. Extend the stub test: a stub game with another action type composes the module and the skeleton and `check`s to 0 in `web/src`.

#### Step 6c: stratagems' own UI on the composition, and the legacy app leaves

Branch `step-6c`, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-6c`. Needs 6b and S. Specified 2026-10-26 (the log entry "2026-10-26, step 6c specified" has the measurements and the questions). Every P line below was accepted by the user on 2026-10-26 as written, the recommendation of P4 included (the events list, the welcome and the tour are dropped and captured as ideas).

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-26 on the integration branch 621edffa, `stem/main` b31b8d53, with an import-graph walk from every route, `lib/index.ts` and the service worker; the script is `join-template-commit-reveal/reach.mjs` beside this file):

- **Nothing the app runs reaches the legacy app.** Of `web/src/lib/stratagems/`'s 132 files, the 15 that 6b and S wrote are reached (the composition, `board/`, `canvas2d/`, `hud/`, `planning`, `view`, `hold`, `stake`, `moves`, `game-config`, `submission-storage`, `diagnostics`, `game`: 2,318 lines) and the other 117 are not: 106 files and 9,353 lines of the legacy client, plus the twgl host `render/` (11 files, 1,697 lines). Nor is anything in `src/utils` (39 files, 2,185 lines), `src/css` (7, 461) or `src/data` (`networks.ts`, `assets/sheet.{png,json}`, which only `render/` reads). No test under `web/test` or `web/e2e` imports any of it. So 6c deletes nothing the game uses: every overlay is a PORT (new code on the composition's stores, reading the legacy file as a reference) or a deletion, never a rewiring.
- **`web3-connection` is reached only from dead files**: 4 import it, 3 `web3-connection-viem`, 8 `ethereum-tx-observer`, 1 `radiate-legacy`, and the rest through `blockchain/connection`. `render/` reaches it too, through `state/ViewState.ts` (its view type) and `actions/ActionHandler.ts`, so keeping the twgl host compiling means porting its types.
- **Packages only dead files import** (`web/package.json` against `stem/main`'s, 34 entries stratagems adds): `web3-connection`, `web3-connection-viem`, `ethereum-tx-observer`, `radiate-legacy`, `missiv-client`, `fuzd-client`, `fuzd-scheduler`, `@noble/ciphers`, `@scure/base`, `immer`, `driver.js`, `tippy.js`, `focus-trap`, `lz-string`, `pretty-ms`, `lucide-svelte` (the template uses `@lucide/svelte`), `svelte-headless-table`, `svelte-portal`, `@zerodevx/svelte-json-view`, `twgl.js`; and five no file under `web/` imports at all (`lodash-es`, `theme-change`, `@metamask/onboarding`, `abitype`, `tslib`; the last two may be peers, so remove them only if `check` stays green), and `@rocketh/export`, which no `web/` script names either (the root's export runs from `contracts`). The ones the live app needs stay: `@etherfold/*`, `stratagems-common`, `stratagems-indexer`, `@sentry/vite-plugin` (`vite.plugins.ts`), `wait-on` (the `dev` script).
- **The five `KNOWN_LEAKS` entries of `test/framework-boundary.test.ts` are all dead files** (`config.ts`, `state/State.ts`, `ui/header/PageLink.svelte`, `ui/install/service-worker-handler.ts`, `ui/loading/SplashScreen.svelte`), and so are both of step 3's svelte-check warnings (`actions/flow/Flow.svelte`, `ui/indexer/IndexerView.svelte`). Deleting the legacy files empties the list (the test's stale-entry check then demands the entries go) and makes check 0 warnings. That is most of step 11.
- **The gems a player stakes come from nowhere today.** S's stake moves gems from the wallet, and the composition offers no way to get them; the e2e fixture transfers them from the deploy's token holder. On a local deploy (`name == 'localhost'`, which the e2e's deploy also is) the contracts include `TestTokensDistributor` (`TestTokensInfiniteDistributor.topup()`: 15 gems to the caller, anyone, any number of times), and the legacy InfoBar's "Get Test token" button called it. alpha1's other way, the claim screen, is a URL-hash private key holding gems and ETH that `tokenClaim.ts` (258 lines) sweeps into the player's wallet: a distribution scheme for a live chain, which is step D's question.
- **The reveal-or-die precedent: a game's UI is its HUD on `/play/`.** reveal-or-die's routes are the template's set plus nothing, and all its game UI is `world/ui/GameHud.svelte` and `hud.ts`; the navbar's links are the framework's own (`lib/ui/navbar/navbar.svelte`: home, play, transactions, contracts, explorer, repo, community), with no slot a game fills. A stratagems page would need that slot upstream first.
- **The theme is `app.css`, at the stem's bytes in every game**, reveal-or-die included: no game has a palette of its own yet, and the template has no seam for one (its `core/ui/README.md` says a theme owns the `bg-*`/`text-*` variables, but the variables live in the inherited `app.css`).
- **What the indexer already holds for the ported panels**: `Data.points` (global rate, fixed and shared points per player) and `computedPoints` for the leaderboard; `placements` (per cycle, per cell) for an events list; the cells and owners for the info panel and winnings (`ViewState.ts`'s `tokensToCollect` is the derivation to port, `pokeMultiple` the call). `board/indexed-board.ts` exposes the worker's `progress` (`HostProgress`), which the composition does not pass on yet.

**Decided** (the user, 2026-10-26, accepting each recommendation as written):

- **P1, the twgl host**: delete `render/` with the rest, and step 9 restores its programs (`Colored2D`, `Blockies`, `Grid`, `Textured2D`, `TerrainLayer`, `tiles`, the sprite sheet under `src/data/assets`) from 6c's parent commit, named in the log, onto the template's render seam; `twgl.js` leaves `package.json` and step 9 brings it back. Rather than keeping 1,697 dead lines compiling by porting their `ViewState` and camera types now, which is step 9's work done early against a seam step 9 may change.
- **P2, how a player gets gems**: a HUD action "Get test gems" calling `TestTokensDistributor.topup()` through the wallet, shown only when the deployment has that contract (every local deploy, the e2e's included); the claim-link screen is not ported, and step D decides how a live chain hands out gems (claim links, a faucet, a sale), and whether that is upstream's. Rather than porting the claim screen now for a chain that does not exist yet.
- **P3, where stratagems' panels live**: in the HUD on `/play/`, as reveal-or-die's do; no new route and no navbar entry. The leaderboard is a HUD panel (a toggled drawer), not a page. Rather than pages, which need a navbar slot upstream first.
- **P4, each overlay's fate** (the home test asked of each: none is something a sibling would want that the template lacks, so nothing goes upstream):
  - PORTED onto the composition, runes mode, logic in `.ts` beside them: the **faction picker** (the colour the planning uses, replacing `defaultColorOf` as the default only); the **cell info** (InfoBar's cell half: owner, life, stake and, for a refused click, why); the **land menu** (removing the player's own full-life cell, a `Color.None` move, which `canPlaceOn` refuses today); **winnings** (`tokensToCollect` derived from the indexer's board, collected with `pokeMultiple`); the **leaderboard** (points from `Data.points`, the player's row marked); the **test gems** action (P2); a **syncing indicator** (the indexer's `progress`, passed through the composition).
  - DELETED, each replaced by something already on the framework: the connection UI (`blockchain/connection/*`: `@etherplay/connect` and the navbar); the action flow and panels (`actions/flow`, `ActionPanel`, `commit`, `commit-cancel`, `reveal`, `PermitComponent`, `NotEnoughEthComponent`: the skeleton's submission and the HUD); the commitments view (the HUD's submission and recovery); the transactions view (the template's `/transactions/` route); the reveal-phase information modal (the HUD's clock and phase); the menu and header (the navbar); the splash screen (the template's shell); the version and install notices (the template's service worker); the debug, indexer-JSON and view-state-JSON views (the template's `/debug/` and `diagnostics.ts`); `$utils/ui` modals and banners (the template's overlays).
  - DELETED, with nothing in their place: missiv (P10 of step 6), `helper-services/missiv` and its scripts; the account data and remote sync (P7 of step 6), `helper-services/secp256k1-db` and its scripts; `actions/auto-reveal` (step 8 rebuilds automatic reveal through the seam); the admin time controls (the local cycle is 30 s + 10 s since 6b, so skipping time is not needed); the WIP and erase notices (alpha-era); **the events list, the welcome and the driver.js tour** (recommendation: drop now and capture each as an idea under `work/`, since the welcome was built around missiv and the claim link and the tour around the legacy panels; the alternative is porting the events list as a HUD panel of the last cycle's placements, which the indexer already holds).
- **P5, the faction colour's storage**: a local preference, not a commitment's wire, stored per player address in `localStorage` under one key chosen now (`__stratagems_faction__<address>`); absent, the default is `defaultColorOf(address)` as today. Rather than per chain and contract, since a faction is the player's choice across deployments.
- **P6, what a click means**: on a cell planned this turn, take it back (as now); on a cell `canPlaceOn` allows, plan it in the picked colour (as now, with the colour); on the player's own full-life cell, open the land menu (remove); on any other cell, select it, and the cell info says why it cannot be played. The composition's `click` part and `planning` change accordingly (`planning` gains a remove, and a selection store joins the game's surface).
- **P7, who sends `pokeMultiple`**: the wallet (`accountExecutor`), as alpha1; the browser key's balance is sized by S's stipend for reveals, and a collection is the player's own asset move.
- **P8, the palette**: drop `src/css` whole and keep the template's theme unchanged in 6c, and draft a template backlog task for a game-owned theme seam (`app.css` importing a theme file a game replaces, the default the template's current variables), which stratagems then takes by cascade. Rather than editing `app.css` here (a conflict on every cascade) or doing the upstream seam inside 6c (a template step with its own approvals and cascade to every node). This changes P11 of step 6 ("the palette into the theme in 6c"), so it is asked.
- **P9, the e2e**: the stratagems suite grows, on the same chain run: get test gems from the HUD (replacing the fixture's transfer for one test, so the button is exercised), pick a faction and see a planned move in its colour, remove a full-life cell of the player's own over a second cycle, and click a refused cell and read why. Winnings are judged by unit tests of their derivation and the call (a collection needs a neighbour's death, several cycles of setup), and the leaderboard by a unit test of its ordering. Every ported `.ts` gets unit tests in `web/test/lib/stratagems/`.
- **P10, step 11 after 6c**: with the legacy files gone, `KNOWN_LEAKS` empty, both warnings gone and the `radiate-legacy` alias gone, step 11's remaining content is whatever 6c's ported code and 6b's HUD copy leave against the conventions. Recommendation: 6c's log measures what is left, and step 11's text is rewritten then (or the step removed if nothing is).
- **P11, the generated and alpha-era leftovers**: the `web-config`, `$data` and `$utils` aliases leave `svelte.config.js` with the last file that imports them (the comment there says so); the root's `sync:dev` and `missiv:dev` scripts and their zellij panes go, `fuzd:dev` stays for step 8; in the alpha-era env files (`web/.env.{alpha1,alpha1test,base,composablelabs}`, `vercel.json`) only the missiv and sync variables go, the files stay as history as 10b left the deployment folders; `vite.plugins.ts`'s comment naming `state/indexer.worker.ts` is corrected to `board/`.
