**What was asked of 6c** (the paragraph this section held until 2026-10-26, kept as what was asked): Branch `step-6c`. Needs 6b. Each legacy overlay is ported onto the composition's stores or deleted, one commit each, with the home test asked of each (a sibling would want it: upstream first): the faction picker, info bar and land menu (the game's); winnings and `poke` (the game's); the leaderboard (the indexer's); the events and commitments views (against the template's `transactions` route and HUD: keep only what they lack); the tutorial and tour; the TestTokens claim screen (as the stake acquisition's way to get gems); admin and debug (against the template's `debug` route); missiv is deleted (P10, until jolly-roger has a messaging layer), with `helper-services/missiv` and its scripts; the splash and syncing screens (against the indexer's progress). Since 6b the composition has stopgaps for three of these, which the ported UI replaces: `planning.ts` plans every move in the player's default faction colour (`(address % 5) + 1`, the legacy default) where the faction picker chooses it; it refuses a click on the player's own full-life cell where the land menu offers its removal (a `Color.None` move); and a refused click says nothing, where the info panel said why. The HUD is the template's (`lib/stratagems/hud/`, with stratagems' words), and each stratagems panel either joins it or replaces it. Ported components are written in runes mode with their logic in `.ts`, so step 11 shrinks to what is left. Done when `grep -rn web3-connection web/src` is empty, the node verify and the e2e pass, and each overlay's fate is a line in the log.

**What was asked of step 6** (the paragraph this section held until 2026-10-26): adopt the template's app context and `@etherplay/connect` in place of `web3-connection`; the omitted template routes (explorer, contracts, transactions, debug, `+error.svelte`) come back, their `.offshoot-omissions` entries go, with the rest of that file's group 3 (the template's service worker entry, its `static/_redirects`, `test/routes`, and the three framework tests omitted for their premise: `layers`, `json`, `fatal`). `web/src/lib/stratagems/game.ts` becomes stratagems' real composition, on the skeleton: the clock (`state/Epoch.ts`, step 7's client half), the submission, secret and recovery (step 8's core) come with it, and stratagems supplies its parts, among them its indexer-backed board store and its `common/` simulation for the view. The board is a factory that receives the clock and the camera, and stratagems returns its indexer store from it and ignores the `poll` it is offered; its types go in as one record, `GameTypes`. The two generated deployment files become one. Reuse what is useful of stratagems' own client (the user).

#### Step S: the one-transaction stake (done 2026-10-26)

Asked for by the user after 6b, which had found staking to be two wallet transactions (the stake, then authorising the browser's key with a top-up) because `addToReserve` is not payable and so the template's acquisition rail could forward no stipend. Done as one step on branch `step-stake`: the contract's `addToReserveAndFund(player, amount, stipendTo)` (payable, forwarding `msg.value`), the client's stake through it with a stipend sized by the template's `signerStipend`, chain properties for the local chain, and the e2e asserting one wallet transaction. Its log entry has the measurements.

### Step 8: automatic reveal

Branches in five repos, each named by that repo's own work protocol, and `step-8` in stratagems (worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-8`). Needs O and T1. Specified 2026-10-27 (the log entry "2026-10-27, step 8 specified" has the method and the questions). **Decided** by the user on 2026-10-27: P1 to P12, with the notes at each P line; P8 on the condition that the games keep their types (see P8). The paragraph this section held is kept at its end, as what was asked. **fuzd is improved INSIDE this step** (the user, at P4). Its validation came first, in a session of its own, and is RELEASED (2026-10-27, fuzd `main` 215a846): ADR 0003 (zod 4 pinned by `zod-schema-for`, every `0x` value normalised to lowercase, offline tests of address case on every route), and the packages RENAMED to the `@fuzd` scope (`@fuzd/client` 0.11.4, `@fuzd/scheduler` 0.11.1, `@fuzd/common` 0.11.1, `@fuzd/server` 0.12.0, `@fuzd/executor` 0.11.1), with `platforms/nodejs` published as `fuzd` 0.11.2 (bin `fuzd`). The unscoped `fuzd-*` names this section quotes above stop at 0.11.x; the layer depends on the `@fuzd` ones. Left for this step, each already a finding on fuzd's `work` (`scheduled-execution-batch-is-already-multi-transaction.md`: no server or route change needed, only `@fuzd/client`'s `scheduleExecution` signature; `fuzd-nodejs-cli-shape.md`: only `--port` and `--process-interval` are flags today): the multi-transaction submission (P4) and the flag-driven CLI (P12). fuzd's own observation `end-to-end-tests-use-port-8546.md` matters here too: 8546 is another project's port on this machine. The paragraph this section held is kept at its end, as what was asked.

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-27, read-only, on the integration branch f0ea50cf, `stem/main` de924635 = template-commit-reveal `origin/main`; jolly-roger `origin/work`; fuzd-client 0.11.1, fuzd-scheduler and fuzd-nodejs 0.11.0 unpacked from npm; `wighawag/fuzd` `main` read on GitHub):

- **The contract is ready and the client is not.** stratagems' `reveal(player, moves, secret, furtherActions, payee)` takes no sender (`_openChunk`: "ANYONE MAY REVEAL", naming "a reveal service"), and `makeCommitment`, `makeCommitmentWithExtraReserve` and `reveal` are payable and forward `msg.value` to `payee`, which is how alpha1 paid fuzd. But the framework's adapter (`$lib/game/core/commit-reveal/adapter.ts`, byte-identical to the stem's) passes `zeroAddress` as `payee` and no value to both.
- **The seam is half there.** `CommitRevealAdapter.commit` already receives `secret`, `cycleNumber` and `revealDueAt` for a scheduler (`seams.ts` names fuzd and drand), and `createSubmission` has `autoReveal: 'immediately' | 'fallback' | 'never'` with `fallbackRevealAfter` (0.5 of the reveal phase). Three gaps, all in the template's `game/core` (its home: jolly-roger has no `game/`): (1) the skeleton `createCommitRevealGame` passes only `commitWhenIdle` to `createSubmission`, so a game cannot choose `fallback`; (2) the adapter's commit has no way to carry a payment to a payee; (3) **nothing turns a reveal sent by someone else into `Revealed`**: a submission still `Committed` when the cycle turns is set to `Missed` and its storage cleared (`submission.ts`), and a `fallback` reveal after the scheduler has opened the turn throws "There is no commitment on chain left to reveal" (the adapter reads a cleared commitment as `cycleNumber == 0`). So a fuzd reveal that worked would be reported to the player as a missed one.
- **The template tree has already placed this upstream.** jolly-roger's `work:work/specs/proposed/service-layers.md`, Wave 4: "`with/fuzd` at `template-commit-reveal`, for long-period commit-reveal", under its rules R1 (a layer is a dependency, one client in the composition root, a zellij pane, env, e2e; "if a layer needs to change application source, stop and fix the seam instead"), R2 (degrades to its absence), R3 (`main` stays decentralised) and R4 (the service green and API-stable first). template-commit-reveal's `work:work/specs/proposed/games-on-this-foundation.md`: "Reveal agency ... No fuzd adapter in this lineage yet (`with/fuzd`, Wave 4)", and Phase 5, "`with/fuzd` here, proven on a 24-hour deployment of the reference game, including C3 and C4 under an early advance" (C4: under early advance a scheduled reveal can only be redundant, never lost). Three of its five games need the long cycle; catacombs' fuzd plumbing "is fully written and never called".
- **stratagems stems from template-commit-reveal `main`** (`offshoot`: `stemBranch: main`); reveal-or-die stems from `with/all`. The template's layers today: `with/pixi-js`, `with/nft-identity`, `with/all`.
- **fuzd 0.11** (npm, and `wighawag/fuzd` `main`, last release 2025-06-19, a `work/` protocol of its own since 2026-07-24): `createClient({privateKey, schedulerEndPoint, drand?})`; `assignRemoteAccount(chainId)` gives the player's remote account (derived per signing key) and its fees; `computeBalanceRequired` gives what is already reserved for it; `scheduleExecution({slot, chainId, transaction, maxFeePerGasAuthorized, timing, paymentReserve?, inClear?})` with `timing` `fixed-time`, `delta-time` or `fixed-round`, timelocked to drand unless `inClear`. **The client always builds a ONE-execution payload, but the payload type is `executions: []`, and the scheduler broadcasts a batch's executions in order from one account** (`scheduler.ts`, a loop with a `batchIndex`). stratagems' legacy was on 0.7 (`account-data.ts`, `actions/commit/index.ts` at 54cafc4d: remote account before the commit, the commit paying it, the schedule after the commit's broadcast, a retry screen when scheduling failed).
- **The key fuzd signs with is available**: the connection exposes `account.signer.privateKey` (`core/connection/executor.ts`), the browser key the local signer is built from.
- **Chunks**: stratagems caps a turn at 30 moves, under one chunk of 32, so its turn is always ONE reveal transaction; the reference game reveals 4 actions a chunk (`rocketh/config.ts`), so its turns are routinely several.
- **The local service is broken since step O**: `helper-services/fuzd` runs fuzd-nodejs ^0.7.6, and its `generate-env.js` reads `contracts.Time.address`, which step O removed from every local deploy, so `pnpm fuzd:dev` (and `pnpm start`'s fuzd pane) throws. fuzd-nodejs 0.11 runs from env alone (`DB=:memory:` sets itself up, `CHAIN_<hexId>=<rpc>`, `HD_MNEMONIC`, `--port`, a 1 s queue loop), which an e2e can spawn.
- **The hosted service is behind** (CORRECTED 2026-10-29 by fuzd's item-3 session: `fuzd api : 0.7.0` was a hard-coded string, which the published `fuzd` 0.11.2 answers too, so it says NOTHING about the hosted version; since `@fuzd/server` 0.12.1 the root route answers the package's version): `https://api.fuzd.dev/` answers `fuzd api : 0.7.0` (its remote-account route still answers); `alpha1test.fuzd.dev` does not answer. Which service a live chain uses is step D's.
- **NOT MEASURED** (no install, no run): that fuzd-nodejs 0.11 installs and runs under Node 24.19 and pnpm 10 (its libsql is native; d0e2c34b pinned `@libsql/core` for 0.7); that a 0.11 client and server agree on `paymentReserve` against a local chain; how a schedule in an existing slot behaves (alpha1's client marked the old one `replaced`, which suggests the server replaces it).

**Proposed** (each with the alternative rejected; the numbers are the questions in the log entry):

- **P1, the home**: upstream, in template-commit-reveal, in two parts. First the three seam gaps on `main`, because they are the framework's whatever reveals (a third party may always reveal, and today the framework reports that as missed): the skeleton passes `autoReveal` and `fallbackRevealAfter`; the adapter's commit takes an optional scheduler (P3); the submission reads the chain before calling a turn missed, and a fallback reveal that finds this cycle's commitment already opened is `Revealed` (P9). Then the layer, `with/fuzd`, stemming from `main` (Wave 4, Phase 5): `fuzd-client`, the scheduler module, `helper-services/fuzd` on fuzd-nodejs 0.11, its zellij pane, `PUBLIC_FUZD_URI`, the e2e, with the reference game as its first user. Rather than stratagems-local (the tree's plan names the layer, three games need it, and the gaps are in framework files stratagems must not edit), and rather than fuzd on template `main` (R3). The user: not jolly-roger either (its first thought, but its greetings example would make a convoluted demonstration of a scheduled reveal).
- **P2, how stratagems takes it**: its `offshoot` config's `stemBranch` becomes `with/fuzd`, so `main` takes the layer by the ordinary fanout, then a between-steps merge into this branch (as T1). Rather than `with/all` (pixi-js and nft-identity, which stratagems does not use) or copying the layer's files (the divergence-by-copy the tree keeps paying for). The user: fine for now, to revisit once stratagems or other games have more layers.
- **P3, the scheduler's seam**: a part the game may hand the skeleton, `scheduler`, with two halves the commit calls: `prepare` before `makeCommitment` (the remote account, the payment to send and to whom; the commit then carries them as `payee` and `value`) and `submit` once the commit is broadcast (the reveal's transactions, timed). A scheduling failure never fails the commit: the turn is committed, the HUD says the reveal is not scheduled and the tab must stay open or come back, and the fallback covers it. Rather than a scheduler that sends the commit itself (it would own the bond and the delegation) or scheduling before the commit (a schedule for a commitment that may never land). This is alpha1's order (`actions/commit/index.ts` at 54cafc4d: `getFUZD` for the remote account and the payment before `sendTransaction`, `scheduleExecution` after it, a retry screen on failure, the commit never undone), confirmed by the user.
- **P4, a turn of several chunks**: the whole chain as ONE batch of transactions in one schedule, which the scheduler already broadcasts in order from one account; fuzd-client gains a multi-transaction submission at its home first (a task under fuzd's own `work/` protocol, and a release), asked for as its own approval. Rather than one schedule per chunk (separately broadcast, so chunk `i + 1` can arrive first and revert on the hash), rebuilding `scheduleExecution` in the layer (a second implementation of fuzd's signing and encryption), or scheduling single-chunk turns only (enough for stratagems, whose turn is one chunk, but the reference game's turns would mostly go unscheduled). Decided by the user: the batch, with fuzd improved as part of this step.
- **P5, timing and price**: `fixed-time` at `revealDueAt` (the latest the reveal phase can open, so by C3 it lands inside the window) with `expiryDelta` the reveal phase; each transaction's gas the deployment's `revealGas`; `maxFeePerGasAuthorized` the chain's `expectedWorstGasPrice` (step S's chain properties); the payment `computeTotalMaxCost` of the batch less what the remote account already holds beyond its reservations (`computeBalanceRequired`), sent with the commit. A chain without `expectedWorstGasPrice` schedules nothing (R2). Rather than alpha1's hard-coded Base minimums and its doubling.
- **P6, encryption**: timelocked to drand on every chain but the local one (31337), which schedules `inClear`, so the local loop and the e2e need no internet and no drand round trip. Rather than timelock everywhere (the e2e then depends on drand being reachable) or in clear everywhere (the service would read every turn before it is revealed, which is what commit-reveal exists to prevent). The timelocked path is covered by a unit test of the submission's shape and one manual run against drand, recorded in the log.
- **P7, the mode** (explained, and confirmed by the user): `fallback` (`fallbackRevealAfter` 0.5) when a scheduler is configured (`PUBLIC_FUZD_URI` set) and the chain is not embedded: fuzd reveals as the phase opens, and the browser, if still open, waits until half the reveal phase has gone, asks the chain (P9), and reveals only if nobody has. `immediately` when THIS turn's schedule failed, when no scheduler is configured, and in the offline world (a service cannot reach a chain inside a tab), which is today's behaviour (R2). Rather than `immediately` with fuzd (the browser and fuzd race every turn, and the loser's reverted reveal costs the browser key gas each time) or `never` (a fuzd outage costs the stake). The HUD says "will be revealed for you" once scheduled.
- **P8, the submission moves into the account data, and carries the schedule** (re-proposed 2026-10-27 after the user's second question, and confirmed "as long as we can keep types for the games"). MEASURED: the submission, its secret included, is kept in plain `localStorage` by `game/core/commit-reveal/storage.ts`, OUTSIDE the account data; the account data (`$lib/account/AccountData.ts`, jolly-roger's) is a synqable store whose schema is fixed at `{operations: map<OnchainOperation>()}`, persisted through `createLocalStorageAdapter`, with an `adapterFactory` handed the account's key, which is where `with/sync` (synqable over waxdb, jolly-roger's Wave 2) and its encryption plug in; and no game in the tree has a deployment of its current contracts (`contracts/deployments` empty on template-commit-reveal, reveal-or-die and bomber-world `main`; stratagems' are alpha-era), so no submission record is in flight anywhere and the move costs no player anything today, which is the moment to make it. Proposed: (a) at jolly-roger, `createAccountData` takes fields an app adds to the schema beside `operations` (the account data's seam, its home); (b) at template-commit-reveal, `SubmissionStorage` gets an account-data-backed implementation (a `submissions` field, one record per chain, contract and identity), and `PersistedSubmission` gains an open `extensions` record, namespaced per layer and round-tripped untouched by the framework, so a layer stores its own data with the turn it belongs to and is cleared with it, without changing the shared shape again; (c) `with/fuzd` writes `extensions.fuzd`: fuzd's `chainId`, the signing account, the `slot` (`cycle_<n>`), the batch size and when it was scheduled, which are what `GET /api/scheduling/scheduledExecution/:chainId/:account/:slot` and `GET /api/execution/execution/:chainId/:account/:slot/:batchIndex` answer by; the HUD polls them in the reveal phase and shows scheduled, broadcast, confirmed or failed, and a failure switches the browser to revealing at once (P7). So the secret, the actions and the schedule are synced and encrypted wherever the account data is, as alpha1's were (`account-data.ts`). NOT MEASURED: `SubmissionStorage.load()` is synchronous and the account data becomes `ready` asynchronously, so the submission's start may have to wait for it; measured first in the step. **MEASURED 2026-10-27 (item 1): it cannot be served before `ready`, and the step stopped there; see the log entry "2026-10-27, step 8: stopped at item 1" for the re-proposal (Q8-1 to Q8-4), awaiting the user.** TYPED, the user's condition: `PersistedSubmission<TAction, TExtensions extends object = {}>`, each layer exporting the type of its own namespace (`with/fuzd`: `{fuzd?: FuzdSchedule}`), a game composing them in its `GameTypes` record, and the account data's added field typed through synqable's `defineSchema` (`map<...>` of the game's record), so a game reads its own fields and the layers' without a cast, and nothing in the framework is `unknown` that a game wrote. Rather than a record aside in plain `localStorage` (not synced, not encrypted: the user), a record aside in the account data (two records with one lifetime, cleared separately), or a fixed `fuzd` field in `PersistedSubmission` (every new layer would change every game's shape).
- **P12, fuzd's local service** (added 2026-10-27 from the user's note): fuzd already has a host-agnostic node CLI, `platforms/nodejs` (`fuzd-nodejs`, no wrangler; stratagems' `helper-services/fuzd` runs it since b09e532a), but it is configured by env only, with an `.env.default` that points `CHAIN_31337` at 8545 (another project's port here) and a native libsql dependency. It is improved at its home in this step, in the shape of waxdb's and faucet's `platforms/nodejs` (flags for port, database, the chain's RPC and the mnemonic, `:memory:` by default, nothing implicit about a port), so the layer's `helper-services/fuzd` and the e2e start it with flags and no generated env file. What exactly changes is measured in the step, against waxdb's `cli.ts`.
- **P11, what is not this step**: the 24-hour deployment and the hosted service (`api.fuzd.dev`, its version unknown: see the correction above) are step D's; Phase 5's 24-hour proof of the reference game stays the template's own and does not block stratagems. Cancelling a commitment leaves a schedule that reverts when it fires (one transaction from the prepaid balance); cancelling the schedule too is left as an idea unless the measurement shows the server already supports it.

The Steps list's order stands: 8 before D (D configures the live service 8 builds), and 9 after both.

### Step O: the offline world

Branch `step-O`, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-O`. Needs 6c. Specified 2026-10-26 (the log entry "2026-10-26, step O specified" has the method and the questions). **Decided** by the user on 2026-10-26: P1 to P10 as recommended, with the notes recorded at P3 to P8. P11 changed: the second tab is refused, upstream first (a step of its own, T1, after O).

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-26, read-only, on the integration branch c67da8ae, `stem/main` b31b8d53 = template-commit-reveal `main`, unchanged since 6a; reveal-or-die `origin/main` 8039e707):

- **The mechanism is already here and compiles**: `web/src/lib/embedded/` (9 files, 1,047 lines: `node.ts` boots `webevm`, `deploy.ts` runs an app's own rocketh deploy scripts through `@rocketh/web`, `wallet.ts` announces a burner wallet, `world.ts` hands back a `ConnectionFactory` and the world's deployment records, `chain-id.ts` mints ids and exports `isEmbeddedChainId`), with `webevm` ^0.6.0 and `@rocketh/web` ^0.19.27 in `web/package.json` and three of its five suites run (`chain-id`, `deploy-config`, `wallet`). The framework pieces a world is composed from are here too: `$lib/game/lobby/{lobby,seats}.ts`, `$lib/game/core/played.ts` (the keyring, the serialised loop, `pokeWhenTheHumanActs`), `$lib/game/core/secret.ts` (`playedSeatSecret`), `$lib/game/acquire` (`authoriseTheBrowsersKey`), `$lib/context/InWorld.svelte`, and the manual cycle trackers in `$lib/game/core/cycle.ts`.
- **Only two files of the template's offline world are the game's.** `offline.ts` (772 lines: which deploy scripts, what the in-tab deployment declares, what each seat is given) and `offline-players.ts` (489: what the world's seats play). Everything else imports only `$lib/offline`'s `offlineWorld`, `startOfflineWorld` and `CHAIN_ID_STORAGE_KEY` and is byte-identical in reveal-or-die: `offline-chrome.ts` (79), `offline-lobby.ts` (82), `ui/offline-world/OfflineWorldBar.svelte` (46) and `OfflineWorldNavbar.svelte` (105), `routes/offline/+page.svelte` (166, which mounts `../play/+page.svelte` inside `InWorld`) and `+page.ts` (25), `test/lib/offline-lobby.test.ts` (120). reveal-or-die writes its own `offline.ts` (735) and `offline-players.ts` (562) AT the template's paths, and adapts `offline-players.test.ts`, `embedded/world.test.ts` and `offline.e2e.ts` at theirs.
- **The template's world is the MANUAL cycle policy with both durations zero**, and says why (`embedded/node.ts`): an automined chain has no clock between transactions, so a timed game in the tab cannot even estimate a reveal once its commit phase closes. stratagems' base supports it (`_advanceCycle`, the `advanceCycle` route, the skeleton's advance client and `contractIsTheJudge: true` are all wired since 6b), but **stratagems' deploy does not read `data`**: `contracts/deploy/020_deploy_game.ts` hard-codes `cyclePolicy: 0` (Timed) and branches its durations on `env.name` (`localhost`, `fast`), and `rocketh/config.ts` has `data: {}`. The template's `offline.ts` declares its world through `data` (`OFFLINE_DEPLOYMENT.data.Game`, spread from the deploy's `default`). No stratagems contract test runs the Manual policy.
- **The board stamps its cycle from block timestamps** (`board/indexed-board.ts`, `calculateCycleInfo`), which under Manual divides by a zero cycle duration and stamps `Infinity`. The indexer already receives `CycleAdvanced` (`indexer/src/processor.ts`, `onCycleAdvanced() {}`, a no-op), which the base emits exactly when a pushed advance happens (a clock-driven turnover emits nothing).
- **The indexer worker would fold the world's chain unchanged in principle**: the tab hands `connectToIndexerHost` its provider (P6 of step 6, `connection.provider`, which in a world is the node's), and etherfold indexes unconfirmed blocks up to the tip (its core keeps them for reorgs and prunes past the finality window). NOT MEASURED: that a worker reaches the tip of an automined chain whose blocks exist only where transactions happened, with `STREAM_FINALITY` 12.
- **But two of the worker's settings are wrong for a chain in a tab.** `tabElection: {name: 'stratagems'}` is ONE election per app (etherfold names it by the app on purpose, not by the store), so an online tab and an offline tab contend for one lease and the loser reads a database nobody writes; and no other tab can read this tab's chain at all, so an embedded chain must not be in an election. And the store is IndexedDB (`stratagems-<stream>`), persisted independently of the world's chain (`offline-world-chain:<id>`) and its records (`offline-world-deployments:<id>`): the template's own README names "three stores persist independently and nothing makes them atomic" as the hazard `restoreIsCoherent` exists for, and this would be a fourth. The publication lookup also asks `/indexed-states/offline/publication.json` (the world's deployment name is its environment), a harmless 404.
- **The web cannot import stratagems' deploy scripts yet**: `stratagems-contracts` has no `exports`, no `dist` (its `tsconfig.json` is `noEmit`, its `prepare` is `pnpm compile` only) and `web/package.json` does not depend on it. The template's contracts package exports `./deploy/*`, `./rocketh/*`, `./artifacts/*` from a `tsc`-built `dist`, its `prepare` is `pnpm compile && tsc` with the reason written in `_prepare`, and its e2e harness builds the worktree's package; stratagems' harness skips that with a `STRATAGEMS:` note naming this step.
- **The deploy's accounts and branches**: five scripts (`001` TestTokens, `002` TestTokensDistributor on `localhost` only, `010` Gems, `011` GemsGenerator, `020` Stratagems) plus `000_debug/001_deploy_time.ts` (a `Time` contract, deployed when `useTimeContract`, which is true for any non-mainnet environment but `fast`); three named accounts (`deployer`, `tokensBeneficiary` holding the 10^9 TestTokens, `timeOwner`); TestTokens' `requireAuthorization` lets the named accounts and the game transfer, and the game has a global approval. A seat's stake is `stakeAmount`, one whole chunk (32 gems), and `addToReserve(player, amount)` credits any player's reserve from the caller's gems, which is the template's `StakeSale.purchase` shape without a price. The local chain's properties (step S) are in `rocketh/config.ts` under 31337, which the template's world spreads into its own.
- **A seat's gems only go down.** A placement moves a gem from the reserve onto its cell, and a dead cell's shares and a removal's gem go to WALLETS, not reserves (10b's defaults). So the template's played seat, which commits an empty turn when its reserve cannot cover a placement, would place 32 times and then never again, and the board would go still; the human can restake from the wallet through the HUD, but only from gems they won.
- **Measured sizes of what this step writes or adapts**: the template's `offline-players.test.ts` 593 lines, `embedded/world.test.ts` 300, `offline.e2e.ts` 238 (one test: the lobby, a world booted, a whole cycle played). `embedded/deployments.test.ts` (110) indexes `contracts.Game` and cannot type-check here; reveal-or-die's is byte-identical because its game is called `Game`. The template's README already names the upstream fix (a fixture name read off `expectedContractNames()`).

**Decided** (each with the alternative that was rejected, and the user's notes):

- **P1, where the game's offline files live**: as reveal-or-die, `web/src/lib/offline.ts` and `offline-players.ts` are stratagems' own AT the template's paths (a cascade that touches them conflicts and resolves to ours, as `routes/play/+page.svelte` does), and the byte-identical files come back at the stem's bytes: `offline-chrome.ts`, `offline-lobby.ts`, `ui/offline-world/`, `routes/offline/+page.{svelte,ts}` (replacing the "in construction" page), `test/lib/offline-lobby.test.ts`. Their `.offshoot-omissions` entries go. Rather than a thin `$lib/offline.ts` re-exporting from `$lib/stratagems/offline/`, which shrinks each conflict but invents a layout no other game has.
- **P2, how the deploy learns it is offline**: an `offline` environment branch in the deploy scripts, beside `localhost` and `fast`: `020` sets `cyclePolicy: 1` (Manual) with both durations `0n` when `env.name === 'offline'`, and `OFFLINE_DEPLOYMENT` names that environment and carries no `data`. Rather than making stratagems' deploy `data`-driven as the template's is (`config.data.Stratagems` with a `default`), which is the template's shape but a rewrite of every branch the deploy has. Either way the world test deploys exactly what the route deploys (`OFFLINE_DEPLOYMENT` exported, as the template's).
- **P3, what the world deploys**: `001`, `002`, `010`, `011`, `020`, not `000_debug` (no `Time`: the manual policy reads no clock, and without the script `020` finds no `Time` and passes the zero address); `002` deploys the distributor on `offline` too (see P5). Named accounts `deployer`, `tokensBeneficiary`, `timeOwner` as hardhat's well-known keys 0, 1 and 0, as the template's world does for its own. The user asked whether `Time` is needed, and whether it belongs in the template if so: it is not needed. It is only a time source for tests and local play (the base's `TIME`, zero means the chain's clock), the manual policy passes no time to `advance` at all, and GemsGenerator reads `block.timestamp` itself. The template's base already takes the time source as config, and its deploy passes the zero address, so nothing is missing upstream.
- **P4, what each seat is given**: gas by `evm_setBalance` on every boot (the template's rule: gas is a number in a tab), and a stake of `stakeAmount` bonded by `tokensBeneficiary` through `addToReserve(player, stakeAmount)` only when the chain says that seat's reserve is zero (the template's "a stake refillable by F5 is not a stake"). The human's browser key is authorised by the world after sign-in, as the template's (`authoriseTheBrowsersKey`). The user: 32 gems is fine for now; later the stake could be a lobby setting (captured as an idea in this step).
- **P5, where more gems come from**: the human uses the HUD's "Get test gems" (the distributor, deployed in the world by P3) and the HUD's stake, exactly as on a local chain; a played seat whose reserve cannot cover its placement takes test gems from the same distributor and restakes, as the human can, rather than committing empty turns for ever. So "what the world can do, the human can do" holds in both directions. Rather than the template's empty turn alone (the board goes still after 32 cycles) or a world-funded top-up of the played seats only (an asymmetry the template's comments argue against). The user: kept simple for now; an offline game should probably END after a number of cycles, and may hand out gems over time (captured as an idea in this step).
- **P6, what a played seat plays**: the template's rule, derived and holding nothing in memory: one placement a turn, its position a pure function of (game, identity, cycle) within the 13 by 13 block around the origin, its colour the seat's default faction (`(address % 5) + 1`, `faction.ts`). The contract judges legality (a placement on another player's living cell is skipped, two on one empty cell collide), so a seat never reads the board. Removals are not played. Rather than seats that read the indexer and choose legal or clever moves, which is the "NPCs a later phase wants" the template's file says this is not. The user: one placement a turn for now; the seats should get smarter later (captured as an idea in this step).
- **P7, the board's cycle under Manual**: the indexer tracks the cycle from `CycleAdvanced` (a singleton entity, its number and whether it is committing), and the board stamps a manual deployment's state with it; a timed one keeps the timestamp; a hybrid one is refused with a named error (stratagems deploys none). The processor bundle changes with it, which costs nothing while no deployment publishes snapshots (step D). Rather than reading `getCycle` at the stamped block, which needs historical `eth_call` from `webevm` (not measured, and its README says calls run at the last mined block). The user asked what hybrid is, and noted that no `CycleAdvanced` fires when nobody commits or reveals. HYBRID is the base's `TimedWithEarlyAdvance`: the clock sets each deadline, and unanimity may bring the next phase forward, so its cycle moves both by time (no event) and by push (an event), and a stamp would need both; stratagems deploys none. NO EVENT IS CORRECT under Manual: there the cycle changes ONLY through `advance` (`CommitReveal.sol`), every successful `advance` emits `CycleAdvanced`, the commit-to-reveal turn included (`commiting` false), and an `advance` with anyone still to commit or reveal reverts (`StillWaitingToCommit`, `StillWaitingToReveal`). So when nobody acts the cycle has not moved, and the stamp the board holds is still the true one. The one way a manual world stalls is by design: it waits for the human to commit.
- **P8, the indexer of a world**: a second worker entry for an embedded chain (`board/indexer.embedded.worker.ts`), with no tab election and no publication lookup, chosen by the board when `isEmbeddedChainId(deployments.chain.id)`. AMENDED 2026-10-26 (the measurement, and the user): its store is IndexedDB, one database per world persisted across reloads, because etherfold's in-memory store answers no query and webevm's awaited per-block save keeps the index from ever being ahead of the restored chain (the log entry "step O: stopped at item 2" has both). So the world's chain stays the only persisted truth beside its records, and the indexer cannot disagree with a restored chain. And the online worker's election becomes per stream, named through the worker's `name` (`new Worker(url, {type: 'module', name})`, read as `self.name`), so an online tab and a world in another tab never contend. Rather than the online worker with an IndexedDB store keyed by the world's stream, which would be a fourth independently persisted store. Confirmed by the user: offline, one indexer in the tab that holds the chain (no player is expected to open a second tab to join the same world, and P11 refuses one); online, the tab election, now per stream.
- **P9, the contracts package**: `stratagems-contracts` gains the template's `exports`, a `tsc` build to `dist` (its `tsconfig.json` emitting, as the stem's) in `prepare` after `pnpm compile`, with the template's `_prepare` reason; `web/package.json` depends on it (`workspace:*`); the e2e harness's `STRATAGEMS:` note about the contracts package goes and the stem's build step comes back.
- **P10, the tests**: stratagems' own `offline-players.test.ts` and `embedded/world.test.ts` at the template's paths (as reveal-or-die), the world test asserting what this game decides: the policy read back as `manual` with every gas figure untouched, every seat at the table staked and waited for (counted off `getAttendance`, not the table), a reload that does not restake. `embedded/deployments.test.ts` stays omitted, its reason updated, and a template task is drafted (beside this file, as 6c's theme task, not pushed) for the name-free fixture its README already asks for. A contracts test runs one Manual cycle on stratagems' own contracts (commit, advance, reveal, advance, the cell on the board). The e2e is a suite of stratagems' own, `e2e/tests/stratagems-offline.e2e.ts`, as 6b put the cycle suite beside the omitted `game.e2e.ts`: the lobby, a world of three seats booted, the human plans two moves, commits, the world's seats commit, the cycle advances, everyone reveals, and the human's cells are on the board from the world's indexer; then a reload restores the world without restaking. The template's `offline.e2e.ts` stays omitted for ever, its reason updated.
- **P11, a second tab on the same world** (changed by the user): REFUSED, with a page saying the world is open in another tab and offering to take it over there or to go back. Both tabs would restore the same chain from IndexedDB and diverge, and nothing in the template stops it today. Its home is the template: every game's world has the hazard, and the guard belongs in what every game shares (the boot in `$lib/embedded` holding a Web Lock named by the world's chain id, and the byte-identical route showing the refusal), not in a game's `offline.ts`. So it is a step of its own in the template tree, T1, after O and not blocking it, drafted in this step as a template task beside this file (as 6c's theme task) and cascaded to stratagems when the user approves it. Step O itself does nothing about a second tab. The rest of the home test: the embedded worker and the per-stream election are stratagems' (the indexer is stratagems-local, P6 of step 6); P7 is stratagems'; the drafted `deployments.test.ts` task is the template's.

**What was asked of step O** (the paragraph this section held until 2026-10-26, kept as what was asked): Specified in its own context, after 6c (the user, 2026-10-26: offline is part of the migration). stratagems' contracts deployed on the template's in-tab chain (`$lib/embedded`, the mechanism, kept), the indexer worker folding that chain through the embedded provider, and stratagems' own `$lib/offline.ts`, `offline-players.ts`, `offline-lobby.ts` and `routes/offline/` in place of 6b's "in construction" page, with their `.offshoot-omissions` entries gone and the template's `offline.e2e.ts` adapted, as reveal-or-die did (its `offline.ts` imports its contracts' deploy scripts).

### Step T1: one tab per offline world (in the template tree)

Branches in the template tree, named by each repo's own work protocol; nothing in stratagems until the cascade. Needs O. **Decided** by the user on 2026-10-27: T1-1 to T1-7 as proposed (the log entry "2026-10-27, step T1 specified" has the questions). It supersedes the "Proposed" of the draft beside this file (`join-template-commit-reveal/template-task-one-tab-per-offline-world.md`, deleted once filed), which was rewritten from this section and filed on the home repos' `work` branches (jolly-roger `work/tasks/done/one-tab-per-world.md`, template-commit-reveal `work/tasks/done/one-tab-per-offline-world.md`).

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-27, read-only: template-commit-reveal `origin/main` e1378e3b, jolly-roger `origin/integration` 5b4567a4 and `origin/with/embedded-chain` 225bb258, webevm 0.9.1, Node 24.19):

- **The mechanism's home is jolly-roger, not template-commit-reveal.** `$lib/embedded` (node, world, chain id, deploy, wallet) arrives from jolly-roger's `with/embedded-chain` branch (its fanout config: stem `main`, merged into `integration` beside `with/local-signer`); template-commit-reveal's copy differs from it only by `README.md` (55 lines). And jolly-roger's own world has the same hazard: its `$lib/offline.ts` (293 lines) persists the chain under `offline-world-chain:<id>` with the id remembered in `localStorage`, and its `/offline-demo` route (116) boots it on mount. The lobby, its route and the world's chrome are template-commit-reveal's (`offline-lobby.ts` 82, `routes/offline/+page.svelte` 166, absent from jolly-roger).
- **One browser holds ONE world per app at a time, and its chain id changes inside a boot.** The id is one `localStorage` key (`offline-world:chain-id`), every game's `openWorld` remints it when a restore is incoherent (`localStorage.setItem(CHAIN_ID_STORAGE_KEY, String(fresh))`, then boots the new id in the same call), and the lobby's `forgetGame` drops it for a new table. A lock named by the chain id would therefore be released or missed exactly at those moments: a tab that remints runs the new id unclaimed until something claims it, and a second tab reading the new id from `localStorage` would claim it successfully.
- **Every game boots through the byte-identical lobby.** `offline-lobby.ts`'s `startGame` is the only caller of `startOfflineWorld` in the template, reveal-or-die and stratagems; each game's `offline.ts` (the game's own file at the template's path) exports the same `offlineWorld` store, whose `Ready` carries `world: EmbeddedWorld` with `provider` and `dispose()`. So a guard taken in the lobby, before `startOfflineWorld`, covers every game without editing any game's file, and covers the remint because it is taken before the id is read.
- **A world's node does not go quiet on `dispose()`.** webevm 0.9.1 (`dist/node.js`, `dispose`): it clears the timer, the head subscribers and the served ports, and "does NOT CANCEL anything already queued", nor refuse what arrives after. Every public request is serialised and resolves only after its block's `persistence.save()`. So "the holder's writes are durable" is a barrier, one provider request after the world has stopped issuing them, and not `dispose()` alone.
- **Unmounting the world stops its writers.** `$lib/context/Context.svelte` returns `context.start()`'s stop from `onMount`, and the world's `start` wraps the context, the played seats and the poke. stratagems' indexer worker only reads the chain, and its IndexedDB writes are transactions.
- **Node 24.19 has `navigator.locks` and `BroadcastChannel`**, so the mechanism is unit-testable in the `server` project without a fake.

**Decided** (each with the alternative rejected; the numbers are the questions in the log entry):

- **T1-1, where it lands**: the mechanism (`$lib/embedded/claim.ts` and its unit test) in jolly-roger `with/embedded-chain`, used there by `/offline-demo`; the wiring (the lobby, the route's refusal, the e2e) in template-commit-reveal `main`. Rather than all of it in template-commit-reveal (the draft), which leaves jolly-roger's demo with the hazard and makes a folder that is byte-identical to its home diverge below it.
- **T1-2, what the lock is named by**: the app's world, one name (`offline-world`, the namespace of `CHAIN_ID_STORAGE_KEY`), not the chain id. CHANGES P11's "a Web Lock named by the world's chain id", for the measured reason above. Consequence: while one tab holds the world, a second tab can neither play it nor start a new one; it is told the offline world is open in another tab.
- **T1-3, where it is taken**: in the byte-identical `offline-lobby.ts`, around `startGame`, before `startOfflineWorld` runs; the refusal is a store exported beside `offlineLobby`, and the route renders it before the world or the lobby. No game's `offline.ts` changes, so reveal-or-die, bomber-world and stratagems take it by cascade with no hand edit. Rather than inside `startEmbeddedNode` or `createEmbeddedWorld` (every boot disposes a probe node and starts another, so a node-scoped lock is released mid-boot, and the refusal would reach the route as the game's `Failed` string), and rather than a step in each game's `offline.ts` (a hand edit in four repos on every cascade, and a route comparing a status the games' unions lack until they are edited).
- **T1-4, how it is held**: `navigator.locks.request(name, {ifAvailable: true}, cb)`, `cb` returning a promise resolved only on let-go, so the browser releases it when the tab closes, crashes or reloads. Rather than a `localStorage` heartbeat or a `BroadcastChannel` election, which leave a stale holder after a crash and reimplement what Web Locks guarantee.
- **T1-5, the take-over**: the refused page offers "Take it over here" and "Go back" (the app's home). Taking over posts `let-go` on a `BroadcastChannel` of the same name and queues a waiting `navigator.locks.request(name, cb)`, showing "waiting for the other tab" with a way back. The holder: the refusal store flips first, so the route unmounts the world (the context and the seats stop), then `await world.dispose()`, then one barrier request through `world.provider` (every write accepted before it is durable when it resolves), then `location.reload()`, which releases the lock and lands that tab on the refusal page. A holder still booting awaits its boot first; a failed one just reloads. The new holder boots by the ordinary restore. Rather than an in-process release without a reload (every app-scoped thing a world started, the game's `pending`, the lobby, the seats, stratagems' worker, would need a reset that no game's `offline.ts` has, and one missed is a second writer), and rather than a reload without the barrier (it can cut a save in flight).
- **T1-6, the tests**: jolly-roger `test/lib/embedded/claim.test.ts` on Node's own locks and channel (a second claim refused while the first holds, let-go asked over the channel, granted once released), the claim taking `locks` and `channel` as parameters with the globals as defaults; template `test/lib/offline-lobby.test.ts` (byte-identical, cascades) extended: a refused lobby never calls `startGame`. One e2e at the template, `web/e2e/tests/offline-one-tab.e2e.ts`, written against the byte-identical route and world bar only, so it is byte-identical in every game and runs there unchanged: two pages in one browser context, the second refused, take-over, the first now refused and the second playing the restored world (same chain id). ### Step D: the new deployment

Branch `step-D` (worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-D`). Needs step 8. Specified 2026-10-30 (the log entry "2026-10-30, step D specified" has the method and the questions); **Decided** by the user on 2026-10-30: PD1 to PD10 as proposed. **Split** by the user the same day: what needs them (the hosted fuzd's upgrade and the live deploy, items 5 and 6 below, and the parts of "Done when" measured on Base itself) is **Step D-live**, deferred until the steps that do not need them are done; items 1 to 4 are this step, landed on their own. The paragraph this section held is kept at its end, as what was asked. Two things in it are NOT this repository's to do and no session can do them: deploying to a live chain signs with the deployer key (alpha1's `0xbe19...b792` owns everything alpha1 deployed), and the hosted fuzd is a Cloudflare worker on the user's account; the step prepares both and the user performs them.

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-30, read-only but for one local build, on `step-8` 3ff26dae):

- **The offline world in a build the harness did not make: CAUSE FOUND, a framework defect.** Reproduced 1 of 1 (a build made by hand with `PUBLIC_WALLET_HOST= pnpm build localhost`, `web/src/lib/deployments.ts` from `scripts/stratagems-generated.mjs`'s throwaway deploy, the app's node pointed at a closed port, served by the e2e's own `webServer`, driven by `stratagems-offline.e2e.ts`): "Commit failed: The commitment was rejected by the contract", the cycle stuck at 2. A probe read the world's own chain: the human's `makeCommitment` (from the browser key 0xd681...) reverted at the world's game (0x0dcd...) with `NotDelegate(player 0x245c..., sender 0xd681...)` (`@etherplay/delegation`'s `requireAccountFor`), while the browser key's registration had gone to **0xb7f8...**, the BUILD-TIME deployment's `Stratagems` address (the throwaway deploy has no `TestTokensDistributor`, so every later address is one nonce off the world's). The world's game is right (`offlineWorld.world.deployments` names 0x0dcd...); what is wrong is `web/src/lib/context/core.ts` at template-commit-reveal `main` (line 1244, byte-identical here): `delegationTarget`, the pair every delegation read and write uses (`createDelegationState`'s `registry`, the writers, `authoriseTheBrowsersKey`), is read from the app's BUILD-TIME store (`$lib/deployments-store`) and not from the connection's deployments, so in an embedded world the delegation is registered and read at the build's address and the world's game refuses every human move. jolly-roger `integration` does not have this: its `createDelegationState` takes `delegationRegistryAddress(deployments.get())`, the connection's, and only the connect-time PERMISSION reads the build-time store; template-commit-reveal's merge of the typed delegation registration (85c9be41) unified the three on the build-time store. The e2e passes only because the harness's build-time deployment is the same deploy sequence as the world's (the same deployer, the same nonces, so the same addresses). **A published build would break the offline world for every player**: its build-time deployment is the live chain's, whose addresses are never the world's. reveal-or-die and bomber-world inherit the same file.
- **The hosted fuzd is older than the layer needs**: `https://api.fuzd.dev/` still answers `fuzd api : 0.7.0`, the hard-coded string; since `@fuzd/server` 0.12.1 the root answers the package's version, so the hosted service is older than 0.12.1 (its `/api/publicKey` answers). `alpha1test.fuzd.dev` does not answer at all. The hosted service is `platforms/cf-worker` (`fuzd-cf-worker`, D1, cron every minute; its `package.json` 0.11.0), deployed from the user's Cloudflare account.
- **Base** (chain 8453, `https://mainnet.base.org`, block 53,306,020): alpha1's and `base`'s TestTokens (`Tokens`/`TOKEN`, 10^9 minted, 0xf1af... and 0xbf0d...), Gems and GemsGenerator (`Points`/`POINT`) are live, all owned by 0xbe19...b792; alpha1's generator holds 1,134 points from alpha1's game. Reusing them needs that key for `enableGame` (the generator), `authorizeGlobalApprovals` and `enableRequireAuthorization` (the tokens), which the deploy script already calls. Gas: the base fee was 0.005 gwei in every one of the last 1,024 blocks, the 99th-percentile tip up to 3.2 gwei in a spike; `eth_gasPrice` 0.006 gwei.
- **What the price property costs a player**: `signerStipend` = `expectedWorstGasPrice` x (commitGas + revealGas = 7,170,000) x 100 turns, paid with the stake's one transaction: 0.0072 ETH at 0.01 gwei, 0.0143 at 0.02, 0.0717 at 0.1. fuzd's payment per scheduled turn is `revealGas` at the same price (0.00014 ETH at 0.02 gwei). Both scale with `revealGas` (7,000,000, a 32-placement chunk with every neighbour alive measured at 6,019,419; dying neighbours not measured), which is why the ceiling is this step's.
- **The deploy script's live cycle** (`020_deploy_game.ts`, any environment not named `localhost`, `fast` or `offline`): a 23-hour commit phase and a 1-hour reveal phase, Timed; actions per reveal 32. No `TestTokensDistributor` outside `localhost` and `offline`, so a live player has no way to get gems today.
- **The rest**: `rocketh/config.ts` declares the environments `localhost`, `alpha1` and `base` (both 8453, tag `mainnet`) and chain properties for 31337 only; `web/.env` leaves `PUBLIC_FUZD_URI` empty and names `PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_URI=https://snapshots.stratagems.world`, which serves `alpha1/` (an index page); the board reads the remote publication first and the copy embedded in the build second (`board/indexed-board.ts`); `web/.env.{alpha1,alpha1test,base,composablelabs}` are alpha-era (`PUBLIC_FUZD_URI=https://api.fuzd.dev`, `PUBLIC_ETH_NODE_URI`, a variable the template does not read); `.github/workflows/ipfs.yml` builds with `pnpm build` and publishes to IPFS and an ENS name.

**Proposed** (each with the alternative rejected; the numbers are the questions in the log entry):

- **PD1, the offline world's delegation target, upstream first**: at template-commit-reveal `main`, the context's delegation pair comes from the CONNECTION's deployments (`deployments.get()`, as jolly-roger `integration` does), and only the connect-time permission keeps the build-time store; a unit test that a context whose connection deploys elsewhere than the build reads, registers and writes at the connection's address; cascaded to every node (a task on its `work`). In stratagems, the harness's offline suite also run once against a build whose deployment differs from the world's (the throwaway export), which is the case a published build is in. Rather than making the build-time deployment match the world's (a published build cannot), or a stratagems-local override (the file is the template's, and every game's offline world has the defect).
- **PD2, the chain**: Base mainnet (8453), where alpha1 played and the hosted fuzd already ran; a new environment `alpha2` in `rocketh/config.ts` (tag `mainnet`) with its own deployment records, and `alpha1` and `base` kept as history. Rather than Base Sepolia (a faucet between every player and the game, and fees that are not the real ones) or another chain (no player, no service there).
- **PD3, the tokens**: fresh TestTokens, Gems and GemsGenerator in `alpha2`, not alpha1's. Rather than reuse: it saves nothing (the same key must sign the generator's and the tokens' configuration calls either way), mixes alpha1's points and balances into a game with different rules, and couples the new deployment's admin to alpha1's contracts.
- **PD4, how a live player gets gems**: the `TestTokensDistributor` deployed in `alpha2` too, handing out one stake (32 gems) a call instead of 15, so a new player presses once, not three times. Rather than claim links (alpha1's: a funded key per link and a sweep) or a sale (a real-money gate for test gems). Consequence to accept: test gems are free, so the stake costs a player time and the cells it buys, not money; the penalty for a missed reveal is still a loss in the game.
- **PD5, the reveal gas ceiling**: measured first, with neighbours dying in the reveal's own cycle (the unmeasured path), and `revealGas` set to the worst measured chunk plus 15% (as `commitGas`); if that exceeds what Base accepts comfortably, `actionsPerReveal` lowered (and `MAX_MOVES_PER_TURN` with it) rather than the limit raised. The paid first commit re-measured on Base (a fork of Base at a recent block, `HARDHAT_FORK`, with the `alpha2` deploy) against `commitGas` 170,000. Rather than trusting the Hardhat figures on a chain with other account-creation and calldata costs.
- **PD6, the chain properties**: `expectedWorstGasPrice` 0.02 gwei for 8453 (four times the measured base fee, and the stipend 0.0143 ETH with today's `revealGas`, less after PD5), `creditsGasMultiplier` as the local chain's. Rather than 0.1 gwei (a 0.07 ETH first transaction) or the base fee itself (a spike would leave fuzd's schedule unbroadcastable; the browser's fallback still covers that, which is why a modest figure is safe).
- **PD7, fuzd**: the hosted `api.fuzd.dev` upgraded to `@fuzd/server` 0.12.1 or later and configured for 8453 (the user deploys the worker; the step writes what to set and checks `GET /` answers the version), `PUBLIC_FUZD_URI=https://api.fuzd.dev` in the `alpha2` mode. Rather than a node instance on a server of ours (one more service to keep up) or no fuzd at launch (every player would have to keep the tab open through each reveal phase, the 24-hour game's whole problem).
- **PD8, the cycle**: alpha1's, a 23-hour commit phase and a 1-hour reveal phase, Timed. Rather than a shorter test cycle (a day is the game; the `fast` environment exists for a quick one).
- **PD9, the build and its publication**: one web mode, `web/.env.alpha2` (Base's RPC for `PUBLIC_NODE_URL` and `PUBLIC_CHAIN_INFO_NODE_URL`, `PUBLIC_FUZD_URI`, `PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_URI`), the alpha-era `.env.{alpha1,alpha1test,base,composablelabs}` deleted (their deployments stay as records); the IPFS workflow builds that mode; no snapshot job at launch (a young deployment folds from its first block in seconds, and the board falls back to the embedded copy), the job added when folding grows slow. Rather than keeping every alpha-era mode buildable (none of them can play the new contracts).
- **PD10, what is not this step**: the snapshot job, a rate-limited faucet, the 24-hour proof of the template's reference game (its own Phase 5), cancelling a schedule (an idea).

Which chain the new contracts deploy to, whether alpha1's TestTokens and GemsGenerator on Base are reused or fresh ones deployed (10b's P4), the environment in `rocketh/config.ts`, the published indexer snapshot for it, and fuzd's configuration. **The hosted service's version is unknown**: `api.fuzd.dev` answered `fuzd api : 0.7.0` on 2026-10-27, but step 8 measured that string to be HARD-CODED (the published `fuzd` 0.11.2 answers it too), so it said nothing; since `@fuzd/server` 0.12.1 `GET /` answers the package's version, so ask the hosted service again (and `alpha1test.fuzd.dev`, which did not answer), and upgrade it or run another instance until it answers 0.12.1 or later, which the layer's client (`@fuzd/client` 0.12, batch schedules, `cycle_<n>_<game>` slots) needs. The live environment's `PUBLIC_FUZD_URI` is set here (`web/.env` leaves it empty since step 8, and `web/.env.{alpha1,alpha1test,base,composablelabs}` still name the alpha-era services), and the chain needs `expectedWorstGasPrice`, without which the layer schedules nothing. **A live chain's commit gas must cover the PAID commit** (step 8): with fuzd configured the first commit pays the player's empty remote account (`payee` and `value` on `makeCommitment`), measured 147,123 gas on Hardhat against 112,716 unpaid, and `commitGas` (now 170,000, the paid case plus 15%) is passed as the gas LIMIT of every commit, so re-measure the paid first commit on the chosen chain (its account-creation and value-transfer costs) before trusting the figure. One thing step 6b found belongs here, because it is a deployment decision: **the reveal's gas ceiling** (`contracts/deploy/.config/gas.ts`: a chunk of 32 placements measured 6,019,419 gas with every neighbour alive, declared 7,000,000, and passed by the client as the LIMIT of every reveal; neighbours that die in the reveal's own cycle add token transfers and were not measured, so measure that path, and choose `actionsPerReveal` from it, before a live chain trusts the figure: a reveal over the limit is a missed reveal). (The one-transaction stake 6b wrote here was done at once, as step S; see its log entry.) How a player on a live chain gets gems is this step's too (6c's P2): the local deploy's `TestTokensDistributor` is what the HUD's "Get test gems" calls and is deployed on `localhost` only; alpha1's way was claim links (a URL-hash key holding gems, swept by `actions/claim/tokenClaim.ts`, at 54cafc4d), and a faucet or a sale are the others; whether that is upstream's (the template's acquisition rail already sells a stake) is the home test. Measured in 6c: a top-up is 15 gems and a stake a whole chunk of 32, so a fresh local player presses three times (three wallet prompts) before the stake; a live distributor should hand out at least a stake. Chain properties for the chosen chain (`expectedWorstGasPrice`, `creditsGasMultiplier`) are this step's too: stratagems' `rocketh/config.ts` declares them for the local chain only (step S), and a chain without them stakes with no stipend, which is two wallet transactions again. The stipend is sized from `revealGas` (7,000,000 at one reveal a turn and 100 turns), so the gas ceiling chosen here also decides how much a player's first transaction sends to their key.

### Step 9: the renderer

Branch `step-9` (worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-9`). Needs D (Step D-live is not needed: the renderer draws any deployment). Specified 2026-10-31 (the log entry "2026-10-31, step 9 specified"); **Decided** by the user on 2026-10-31: P9-1 to P9-11 as proposed, with the faction wash OFF by default (P9-5).

**The user's decision (2026-10-30)**: the twgl.js renderer specified on the unlanded branch `specify-9` (11e4a1ee, the "world" terrain renderer of 54cafc4d) is **deferred**; `specify-9` stays unlanded, as history. stratagems gets instead a simpler style reminiscent of Go, rendered with pixi.js. The problem the earlier "gems on a board" style never solved is the one this step exists for: **showing a cell's life, and whether it grows or decays, without printing numbers**. The style was chosen on 2026-10-30 and 2026-10-31 through a throwaway prototype, **`proto/go-style`** (pushed to origin with the user's approval on 2026-10-31; worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/proto/go-style`, `prototypes/go-style/`, seven rounds, the last 00abf520; its README records each round and the user's verdicts; its screenshots are under `prototypes/go-style/shots/`, in git LFS). It is the primary source for P9-5: open it with `pnpm install --ignore-workspace && pnpm dev` in that folder (port 5199).

**What decides the shape** (measured 2026-10-31, read-only on the integration branch e1b922ea and template-commit-reveal `with/pixi-js` 9d47aa8b, plus the prototype):

- **The rule the board must show** (`contracts/src/game/internal/UsingStratagemsState.sol`, `_computeNewLife`, `_effectiveDelta`; `UsingStratagemsSetters.sol`): a cell has four neighbours, along the grid lines, as in Go. Life is 0 to 7 (`maxLife`), a placement starts at 2, and each cycle a living cell moves by exactly one: up if it has more friends than enemies or no enemy at all, otherwise down. **A dead cell stays**: its colour is never cleared and `_updateCell` judges friend or enemy by colour, not life, so it keeps pressing its neighbours until someone places on it (free to anyone; not removable, which needs full life). **Evil is a colour like the others**: placed at life 2 by a collision, the same rule, Evil cells each other's friends; its owner is `EVIL_OWNER`. **Only Evil stacks gems**: `_placeColor` sets every other cell's stake to 1, and a collision adds one gem per colliding player to the Evil cell; when it dies its gems go to its enemies' owners. The TypeScript copy (`stratagems-common`, `StratagemsContract`) has the same `_effectiveDelta`, and `view.ts` already runs every cell through `getUpdatedCell`, whose result carries `delta` and `enemyMap`: so the trend is the copy's, not a reimplementation.
- **The template's render seam fits a pixi renderer as it is.** `GameRenderer<TSurface>` (`$lib/game/core/seams`); the stateful style (`$lib/game/render/stateful.ts`, `reconcile.ts`, on `main`, library-free) diffs the view state into `add` / `update` / `remove`, empties the scene on `Unloaded`, announces cycle changes before applying them, and hands `tick` the live objects for per-object animation. stratagems' context builds its renderer in one place (`context.ts`, `renderer: ({viewState}) => createBoardRenderer(...)`), its page loads the host through one function (`routes/play/+page.svelte` imports `loadCanvasComponent` from `$lib/stratagems/canvas2d/board-renderer`), and the stopgap is 72 lines on the canvas-2d host.
- **pixi lives on template-commit-reveal `with/pixi-js`** (14 files on top of `main`: the host `game/render/pixi/{PixiCanvas.svelte,world.ts,assets.ts,LoadingGate.svelte}`, the reference game's pixi board, `vite.assetpack.ts`, `assets/sprites/cell.png`, `pixi.js` ^8.19 locked at 8.22.0), and **stratagems stems from `with/fuzd`**, a sibling. The offshoot configs: template-commit-reveal's `with/all` stems from `[with/pixi-js, with/nft-identity]` and has no fuzd; reveal-or-die stems from `with/all`, bomber-world from reveal-or-die. So no node has both fuzd and pixi.
- **The pixi host is configured for pixel art**: `PixiCanvas.svelte` passes `resolution: 1`, `antialias: false`, `roundPixels: true`, and `assets.ts` sets `TextureStyle.defaultOptions.scaleMode = 'nearest'` for every texture, at import. The chosen look is smooth (gradients, rings, pips a few pixels wide), so it needs the device ratio, antialiasing and linear filtering. The host draws its own grid (white, alpha 0.15) unless `showGrid` is false; both hosts declare `showGrid` and `backgroundColor`.
- **The camera's range** (`game-config.ts`): `cellSize` 10, limits 10 to 100 cells across, so on a 1,400-pixel screen an intersection is 14 to 140 pixels apart, which is the range the prototype was judged in (its thresholds: the dial from 34 pixels, the bezel from 48).
- **pixi runs in the harness's Chromium**: in the prototype, Playwright 1.62.1 (the harness's version, `web/package.json`) in headless Chromium got a WebGL context on SwiftShader (`ANGLE (Google, Vulkan 1.3.0 (SwiftShader Device (Subzero)))`), pixi 8.22.0 initialised its WebGL renderer and drew 859 to 902 cells, every layer and animation, with no page error; its ticker reported 60 frames per second (the browser's frame rate, not a measured frame time). The e2e clicks by coordinates through `clickCanvas` (`page.locator('canvas')`, one canvas), which a pixi host keeps.
- **The click before the board loads** (found in the 0aeaab2e between-steps merge, step 8): `planning.ts` judges a click against the indexed board, and a click while it is `Unloaded` is dropped without a word; the e2e waits for the board (`boardLoaded`) since then, and the player-facing side was left to this step, which owns what the board shows while loading. The indexer's progress is already a store with words (`syncing.ts`, `describeSyncing`).
- **`test/render-host-boundary.test.ts` is omitted since step 4** because it reads the selector by a hard-coded path (`src/lib/placement/render/index.ts` at the stem); reveal-or-die keeps it by editing that constant to `src/lib/world/render/index.ts`, a divergence every game repeats.
- **Who placed an Evil cell**: the indexer keeps who placed where for the last 7 cycles only (`placementPlayer`, `PLACEMENT_WINDOW` 7 in `indexer/src/entities.ts`), so the players of an older collision are not known to the client.

**Proposed** (each with the alternative rejected; the numbers are the questions in the log entry):

- **P9-1, the home of pixi: a new template-commit-reveal node `with/fuzd-pixi`**, stemming from `[with/fuzd, with/pixi-js]` (the shape of `with/all`), verified by the tree's verify, with stratagems' `offshoot` config moving `main`'s `stemBranch` from `with/fuzd` to it, and cascaded by offshoot-fanout; a task on template-commit-reveal's `work`, by that repo's protocol, every push asked for. Rather than fuzd added to `with/all` (it would bring fuzd to reveal-or-die and bomber-world, and nft-identity to stratagems), or pixi copied into stratagems (divergence by copy, every pixi fix then ported by hand). What `with/pixi-js` brings that stratagems does not want is omitted with its reason (the reference board is under `web/src/lib/placement`, already omitted; whether `assets/sprites/cell.png` and the assetpack plugin stay is measured in item 1: with no bundle, `assets.ts` drives the progress to 1 through its failure path and logs an error on every load, which a game with no sprites should not). **Refined after item 1 (the user, 2026-10-31)**: the node is the plain merge (one conflict, the lockfile); stratagems omits `assets/sprites/cell.png` only, keeps `vite.assetpack.ts`, its test and the plugin line (the host imports the manifest only the plugin writes), and resolves `vite.plugins.ts` by keeping both plugins; the premise of an error on every load is withdrawn (an empty bundle loads silently).
- **P9-2, a smooth pixi host, upstream at `with/pixi-js`**: `PixiCanvas.svelte` takes a prop for smooth art (the device ratio as resolution, antialiasing, no pixel rounding), defaulting to today's pixel-art settings, and the global `scaleMode = 'nearest'` becomes the pixel-art default only (or stays, with smooth games setting `linear` per texture, whichever item 1 measures to be the smaller change); **refined after item 1 (the user, 2026-10-31)**: the prop sets `resolution`, `antialias`, `roundPixels` and the CSS `image-rendering`, never `autoDensity` (with `resizeTo: canvas` it locks the canvas at 800 by 600), and `scaleMode` stays `nearest` by default, a smooth game setting `linear` per texture; `Canvas2DCanvas.svelte` on `main` accepts and ignores the prop, so the props stay identical. Home test: any pixi game with smooth art wants it. Rather than a stratagems copy of the host (divergence by copy) or changing the default (the reference game is pixel art).
- **P9-3, the renderer is the template's stateful style**: `createStatefulRenderer<Container, BoardView, string, CellView, CellObject>` in `web/src/lib/stratagems/render/`, a `CellObject` (a pixi `Container`: its stone or husk sprite, the pending pip, the rim, the bezel, the planned marks) updated by a typed `changed`, plus board-wide layers rebuilt when the view changes (bridges and notches, the territory wash, the ivory wash) and per-camera ones (the grid, the far-zoom swap). **Every decision is a pure function in a `.ts` file with unit tests** (`render/look.ts` or similar): the trend, the pips to fill, the links between neighbours, the stone's size by life, the zoom thresholds, the owner card's and the Evil card's data. **The trend comes from the contract's copy**: `view.ts` adds `trend` (`grow`, `shrink`, `full`, `dead`) from `stratagems-common`'s effective delta of the simulated cell, so the board can never draw a rule the contract does not apply. Rather than an immediate renderer (pixi is retained-mode; the template's README says stateful is the answer for it) or recomputing the rule from the drawn neighbours (the prototype did, as a stand-in).
- **P9-4, the board is drawn by the game**: the host's grid off (`showGrid={false}` on stratagems' page), the wood, its lines and the hoshi (a dot every 8 intersections) drawn by the renderer under the cells, every texture generated in code once and reused (stones per colour, life marks and stake; husks; rings), no art assets and no assetpack bundle. Rather than the host's grid (white, faint, wrong on wood) or a sprite sheet (the look is procedural and needs none).
- **P9-5, the look (CHOSEN by the user, rounds 1 to 7 of the prototype)**: Rich 2D: **jewel gems** (a cabochon with a table ring, in deeper jewel tones of the legacy faction hues) on **kaya wood** (straight grain, dark ink lines, hoshi). **Life**: a dial of seven pips round the rim when an intersection is at least 34 pixels, the stone's size when farther. **Trend**: one pending pip pulsing, every gem in step, gold where life is coming and red where it is going; a narrow, steady light ring on a growing gem and a red rim on a shrinking one. **Bridges and notches**: a bold bridge along the grid line between two same-colour neighbours, a red pinch on the line of an enemy contact, dead neighbours included (they count). **Dead cells** stay as flat, sunken, cracked husks of their colour. **Dying**, at the cycle's turn: cracks race from an impact point, the gem shudders, chips fly, and it sinks into its husk (about 1.6 s). **Evil**: a charcoal gem with bone pips, one stacked disc per gem at stake. **Yours**: a gold bezel with four prongs from 48 pixels, and an **ivory wash** under your gems at every zoom (it reads far out, where the bezel cannot). **Factions**: a soft wash of each faction's colour under its living cells, a toggle, **off by default** (the user, 2026-10-31). **Ownership**: hovering or tapping a gem lights that owner's gems with a fine ring and dims the rest (the hit area is the cell's whole square, and leaving to empty board keeps the highlight 250 ms, so moving between gems does not flicker), and a card at the top right gives the owner's blockie, short address, the gem's life and trend with its friends and enemies, and the owner's living cells and gems at stake. **Planned moves**: a placement is a translucent ghost with a dashed ring in the faction's colour, a removal a red cross. Not taken (tried and judged in the prototype): cracks as the life display, stone size alone close up, blockie inlays, per-owner outlines, the yunzi and lacquer materials, the slate, washi and felt boards, a live three.js board and 3D-baked sprites.
- **P9-6, an Evil cell's card**: its gems at stake, what its death pays and to whom (its enemies' owners), and the players who made it when the indexer still knows them (the 7-cycle window), otherwise "a collision of N players". Rather than widening the indexer's window (the indexer's shape is Step X's question).
- **P9-7, the fallback, CHANGED after item 1 (the user, 2026-10-31)**: **no canvas-2d fallback**. pixi 8.22's own Canvas renderer is the fallback, which the host already selects by itself when WebGL cannot start (measured: every sprite and graphic drawn, filters skipped), so the look uses no filter (the washes are soft-edged textures drawn in code, not blurred containers) and looks the same on both renderers; the stopgap renderer and the canvas-2d selection go; a unit test that the look's layers carry no filter. What was decided before item 1, kept as what was asked: the canvas-2d host and the stopgap immediate renderer stay, moved under `render/`, chosen at runtime when a WebGL context cannot be made (one probe in stratagems' selector, which then names both the host and the renderer factory together); a unit test of the choice. Whether pixi 8.22 itself renders without WebGL is measured in item 1. Rather than no fallback (a player with WebGL off sees nothing) or a second full look in canvas 2D (the stopgap is enough to play).
- **P9-8, while the board loads**: the board draws its wood and lines at once, with the indexer's words (`describeSyncing`) over it until the indexed board is loaded, and a click before then is refused with a word ("the board is still loading") through `planning.ts`' judgement and the cell panel, like every other refusal, with a unit test and one e2e case (a click before the board loads shows the word). Rather than queueing the click (judged against a board the player has not seen) or keeping it silent.
- **P9-9, the render-host boundary test, upstream first**: at template-commit-reveal `main`, `render-host-boundary.test.ts` finds the selector by following the page's `loadCanvasComponent` import instead of a hard-coded path, so every game keeps it unedited (reveal-or-die's edit then goes, by cascade); stratagems un-omits it, with the pixi host's props. Rather than a stratagems copy with its own path.
- **P9-10, measured, then tested, REFINED after item 1 (the user, 2026-10-31)**: **no culling** (measured to cost more than it saves); the board-wide layers are rebuilt only when the view or the zoom class changes, never per frame, and the washes carry no filter (P9-7). The harness's Chromium renders WebGL on SwiftShader at about 10 frames per second for 10,000 cells, which is recorded, not optimised for. As first written: item 1 measures the real host in the harness's Chromium on a generated board of 2,000 and 10,000 cells (frame time from `performance`, not the ticker), and the step culls off-screen cells (visibility from the camera's bounds) if the measure asks for it; the e2e runs as step D measured it, plus P9-8's case and one that the pixi host mounted (a WebGL canvas). Rather than trusting the prototype's ticker figure.
- **P9-11, what is not this step**: the 3D look, other materials and boards, sound, a per-owner territory, Evil players beyond the indexer's window, and the twgl renderer (deferred, `specify-9`).

### Step X: the indexer host and bytecode simulation, upstream

Added 2026-10-29 by the user, after step 8's item 3, while planning a new game closer to stratagems than to reveal-or-die (a coin stake, armies on a map, a captured chief handing over its army and its stake, teleport zones to leave with it), and with catacombs to port later. Specified in its own context, after step 9. **Each game's indexer LOGIC is its own** (the user): what is shared is its hosting. stratagems' processor (`indexer/src/entities.ts`, `processor.ts`, an etherfold `EntityProcessor`) stays the game's; its host (`web/src/lib/stratagems/board/indexer.worker.ts` for a live chain, `indexer.embedded.worker.ts` fed by the offline world, `worker-choice.ts`, the board's indexed state, and `contracts/utils/indexer.ts`, the same processor in Node over sqlite for tests and the published snapshot) is what any game repeats, so it is the home test's candidate: a game hands the framework its processor, and the framework runs it in all three places. **Simulation** (previewing what a turn will do before the chain resolves it), two ways: reuse the processor's logic (stratagems does this through `common/`, a 700-line TypeScript copy of its contract's rules that can drift from it), or run the contract's own bytecode, as catacombs does (`contracts-js/src/index.ts`: `GameReveal` and `GameUtils` through `@ethereumjs/evm`'s `createEVMRunner`), with webevm's execution part, which the offline world already ships, in place of `@ethereumjs/evm`. The bytecode way is the general one (nothing reimplemented, nothing to drift) and asks one rule of a game's contracts: the resolution callable as a pure function of its inputs, which catacombs gets by separate contracts and which the commit-reveal order rule (resolve once the cycle closes, never at reveal time) already pushes a game towards. To measure first: how webevm exposes execution without its chain; whether stratagems' contracts can resolve as a pure function, so it moves off `common/` onto the bytecode runner; what catacombs' port needs of the same helper.

### Step 11: Svelte conventions

**Absorbed by 6c** (P10 of 6c, measured 2026-10-26 after the legacy app left): no component under `web/src/lib/stratagems` is in legacy mode (the seven HUD components declare `runes={true}`), no file there imports `$app/*` (`KNOWN_LEAKS` empty and `web/test/framework-boundary.test.ts` byte-identical to the stem's), svelte-check reports 0 warnings (both of step 3's went with their files), the `radiate-legacy` alias and package are gone, no `.svelte.ts` file exists, and every decision a panel shows is a pure function in a `.ts` file with unit tests (`hud/panels.ts`, `hud/hud.ts`, the stores beside them). Nothing is left for this step, so it is ticked rather than run. What it said before 6c is kept below as what was asked.

What was asked: components out of legacy mode, logic out of `.svelte` files into `.ts` stores, per `AGENTS.md`; the step-4 boundary-test exemptions end here (the five `KNOWN_LEAKS` entries in `web/test/framework-boundary.test.ts`, and the `radiate-legacy` alias once stratagems' account handler is gone). Two svelte-check warnings from step 3 end here too: `actions/flow/Flow.svelte` and `ui/indexer/IndexerView.svelte` name a store `state`, which Svelte 5 reads as the `$state` rune once the component leaves legacy mode (`store_rune_conflict`), so the store is renamed in the same change. Steps 6 to 9 may already have replaced some of these files.
