Caveat a, **which pnpm "it uses today"**: the repo has no `packageManager`, so a shell here gets the system pnpm, **11.25.0**, and that one cannot install the committed lockfile at all. It fails twice over: `ERR_PNPM_MINIMUM_RELEASE_AGE_VIOLATION` (pnpm 11 defaults to a one-day `minimumReleaseAge`, and etherfold 0.11.0 with its 13 `@etherfold/*` packages and rocketh 0.23.1 were published on 2026-09-30), and with the age check disabled, `ERR_PNPM_LOCKFILE_CONFIG_MISMATCH`, because the lockfile records the `overrides` that pnpm 11 no longer reads from `package.json`. CI pins pnpm 9, and pnpm 9 installs the lockfile frozen and cleanly, so the baseline above is pnpm 9 (`npx pnpm@9`). The age failure disappears on its own tomorrow; the overrides one is step 1's job. Step 1 should also know: the system pnpm 11 honours `packageManager` and switched to 10.28.1 in the template's worktree (after warning that the template's own `package.json` still has a `pnpm` field with `onlyBuiltDependencies` and `overrides`, which pnpm 11 ignores).

Caveat b, **`check` is only 0 errors after a bootstrap**: straight after install it reports 32 errors in 19 files, all of them `Cannot find module 'stratagems-common'`/`'stratagems-indexer'`/`'$data/contracts'` and the `unknown`/implicit-`any` types that follow from them. Those are two workspace packages whose `dist/` is not built by install, and a generated file. `pnpm indexer:build alpha1` (contracts export, then the `common` and `indexer` builds) produces all three, and then `check` is 0. So the plan's "0 errors" holds, but only in a tree where that has been run; a fresh worktree needs it, and step 4's verify (`pnpm install && pnpm --filter ./web check && ...`) will hit exactly this, see finding 4 below.

The root `prepare` (`scripts/ensure-deployments.mjs`) ran alongside `contracts prepare` (the compile), found no committed records, and its throwaway deploy failed; it said so and let the install succeed, as designed. Likely an ordering race between the two `prepare` scripts on a fresh clone; observed once, not reproduced or diagnosed further (it is the template's, not this plan's). It matters here because the node's verify runs in a fresh worktree.

**4. which framework files tie to the reference game.** An import graph over `web/src/lib` of the template (every `import`/`export ... from`/`import()` resolved through `$lib` and relative paths), for the 240 files under `core game context kit embedded account ui`. "Reference game" = `placement/`, `view/index.ts`, `onchain/state.ts`. (`view/operation.ts` and `onchain/delegation.ts` are NOT the reference game: they are generic operation display and `@etherplay/connect` delegation, reveal-or-die keeps both unchanged, and they are imported by 7 and 6 framework files respectively.)

- **Direct imports, 2 files, both in `context/`:**
  - `context/game.ts`: `$lib/onchain/state`, `$lib/view`, and 15 `$lib/placement/*` modules (`config`, `commit-reveal`, `storage`, `planning`, `cycle`, `advance`, `hold`, `display-plan`, `errors`, `reserve`, `acquisition`, `missed-reveal`, `state`, `view`, `render`, `cells`). This IS the reference game's composition.
  - `context/types.ts`: `import type` of `OnchainStateStore` (`$lib/onchain/state`), `ViewStateStore` (`$lib/view`), `BoardState` (`$lib/placement/state`), `BoardView` (`$lib/placement/view`), and `Game, Render` from `./game`.
- **Transitively, 63 more** (core 11, game 5, context 6, kit 1, embedded 2, account 6, ui 32), nearly all through two hubs: `context/types.ts`, reached directly or via `$lib` (`index.ts`) or `account/AccountData.ts`; and `context/index.ts` -> `context/game.ts` (the path for `embedded/{world,index}.ts`). Two go through `offline.ts` -> `placement/config.ts` instead: `ui/offline-world/OfflineWorld{Bar,Navbar}.svelte`. The 63 are: `account/{AccountData,connectors,operation-intent,operations-migration,recorded-nonces,toastConnector}.ts`; `context/{AcrossPages,Context,InWorld}.svelte`, `context/{core,index,sender-registry}.ts`; `core/connection/{ConnectionFlow.svelte,executor.ts,senders.ts}`, `core/transaction/{AccountCannotSend,ErrorDetails,InFlightRequests,InsufficientFunds}Modal.svelte`, `core/ui/confirm/ConfirmationModal.svelte`, `core/ui/faucet/{FaucetButton.svelte,faucet-actions.ts,index.ts}`; `embedded/{index,world}.ts`; `game/acquire/{AcquireModal.svelte,acquire.ts,authorise.ts,index.ts,pending.ts}`; `kit/KitNavigation.svelte`; `ui/chrome.ts`, `ui/credits/*` (7), `ui/debug/*` (3), `ui/delegation/*` (5), `ui/in-flight/SendingBar.svelte`, `ui/navbar/*` (2), `ui/nonce-cache/NonceCacheBanner.svelte`, `ui/offline/OfflineBanner.svelte`, `ui/offline-world/*` (2), `ui/pending-operation/*` (8), `ui/rpc-health/RpcHealthBanner.svelte`.
- **Independent: 175** (core 118, game 33, context 3, kit 4, embedded 6, account 1, ui 10).
- **The `Game` deployment by name**, a tie that survives even if `placement/` stayed: `deployments.contracts.Game` in `context/config.ts:87`, `context/core.ts:1212`, `context/game.ts` (2), `game/acquire/acquire.ts` (4), and `onchain/state.ts:206`. stratagems' contracts are `Stratagems`, `Gems`, `GemsGenerator`, `TestTokens`, `Time`, so these do not type-check against a stratagems deployment.
- **`$lib/deployments` (generated)**: imported directly by `deployments-store.ts` and `embedded/deployments.ts`, and through them by 11 framework files directly and 71 transitively. After the merge, `ensure-deployments.mjs` would find stratagems' COMMITTED records (9 networks: `alpha1`, `alpha1_old`, `alpha1test`, `base`, `blast_testnet`, `composablelabs`, `fast`, `redstone_holesky`, `sepolia`) and export the first one `readdir` returns, so the generated file names stratagems' contracts and no `Game`; the throwaway deploy would never run. That meets the `Game`-by-name tie above.

What this says for step 4 (for that step to decide, not decided here): the reference game is not a leaf. It is wired into the framework through the app context (`context/game.ts`, `context/types.ts`), and reveal-or-die, which omits `placement/` too, compiles only because it REWRITES that context (`git diff stem/main main` in reveal-or-die: `context/game.ts` +988 lines changed, `context/types.ts`, `index.ts`, `offline.ts`, `kit/environment.ts`, `game/identity.ts`). stratagems does not adopt the app context until step 6, so omitting `placement/` alone leaves 65 framework files that cannot compile, and omitting those too leaves the merge carrying mostly `core/` and `game/` plus the unit tests that do not reach them. That is the seam finding the template's plan asks this member to produce: the app context is where a game plugs in, and today it is the reference game's own file.

Surprises: the pnpm 11 lockfile refusal; `check` needing `indexer:build` first; the template's `deployments.ts` not generated on the first install; and the size of the context tie (the plan's step 4 expected "a framework file that fails to compile only because of the omitted reference game" to be the exception, and it is 65 of 240).

**Template `main` moved overnight**: b4d101ba (2026-09-30 19:56, after step 0's 2694b172), 10 commits of app-shell chrome (`ui/chrome`, `AppShell`, nav progress), none under `context/`, `placement/`, `ui/delegation` or `game/acquire`. At b4d101ba: `check` 0, unit 1697 + **78** (client 14 files; step 0 measured 76 in 13).

**`context/game.ts`, template (`with/all`) against reveal-or-die (`main`), comments stripped**: 439 against 534 code lines (930 against 1238 in all, so of `git diff`'s 992 changed lines about 620 are comments). 304 of the template's code lines survive unchanged; reveal-or-die removes 135 and adds 230. By section: imports 66 of 101 unchanged, exported types and helpers 60 of 79, the composition body 123 of 183, `start()` 32 of 49, the returned object 23 of 27. Read hunk by hunk, the additions are mostly the same call with the game's constructor or type (`createPlacementCommitReveal` to `createWorldCommitReveal`, `BoardState` to `WorldState`, `reserve` to `deposited`), about 90 lines are reveal-or-die's game only (avatar, position, enumeration recovery, controls, reveal outcome, what a click means), and about 40 are framework code grown locally (`onEachNewCycle`, wiring for `settleBoardWhenCycleStarts`/`refreshDuringReveal` which the template ships in `game/core/refresh.ts` and does not wire, a `currentCycleNumber` store). These were counted by reading, not by a script.

**The app context type**: `Context` has 44 members, 4 of them the game's (`onchainState`, `viewState`, `game`, `render`). Framework code reads them in two places only: `game/core/diagnostics.ts` (its own structural `DiagnosableApp`, which needs `game.submission` and `game.cycleInfo`) and `context/core.ts` (`onchainState`, for the refresh connector and RPC health).

**`contracts.Game` in framework code**: 8 address reads (`context/{config,core}.ts`, `game/acquire/acquire.ts` 3, the composition 2), one ABI (`acquire.ts` into `submitRegistration`), one `linkedData` (`onchain/state.ts`, cast to `{commitPhaseDuration: unknown, ...}`). The ABI was already erased: `submitRegistration` took `abi: readonly unknown[]` and `args: readonly unknown[]`, and its three callers cast their client `as unknown as RegistrationWriter`. The delegation surface is typed separately and exactly (`DELEGATION_ABI` from `@etherplay/delegation` 0.1.1, through `@etherplay/connect`: `registerDelegate(delegate, payee)` and `registerDelegateViaSignature(owner, delegate, deadline, signature)`, both payable).

**The prototype**, template-commit-reveal branch `proto/game-slot` from b4d101ba, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/template-commit-reveal/proto/game-slot`, 4 commits, 20 files, +189/-144:

- d3df3490, the registration typed against `DELEGATION_ABI` (the erasure fixed): `check` 0. Teeth, each tried and reverted: a `number` deadline, a dropped argument to `registerDelegate`, and `Game.abi` passed as the registry ABI each give 1 `check` error; on `main` all three compile. The three client casts were then REMOVED and `check` stayed 0, and that is real checking, not `any`: changing the writer's `value` to a `string` gives 3 errors, one at each caller.
- d1416564, the `$game` alias (the `$ui` precedent): `context/game.ts` to `placement/context.ts`; `placement/game.ts` declares the game; `Context = CoreContext & GameMembers`; delegation registry and operation scope are functions of `TypedDeployments`; `acquire.ts` reads `deps.delegation.registry`; the poller takes `cycleDuration`.
- 5accc66b, `AppContext` states what core needs (`onchainState: {update, status}`) and `core.ts` imports no game type. The first try, spreading `Omit<App, 'onchainState' | 'start'>` and adding `onchainState` back, does not type-check as `Omit<App, 'start'>` (a TypeScript limit with generic rests); spreading the app's members whole does, with the same runtime object.
- 4e1ee404, `debug/diagnostics.ts` (the game's, it reads `context.game`) moves to `placement/` and `AcrossPages.svelte` imports it through `$game`.

After every commit: `check` 0, unit 1697 + 78, unchanged.

**The stub test**, in a second throwaway worktree (`proto/stub-game`, detached, not committed): the reference game deleted (`placement/`, `offline*.ts`, `ui/offline-world`, `routes/{play,offline}`, `routes/+page.svelte`, their tests), the deployment's `Game` renamed `Stub` so no contract is called `Game`, `$game` pointed at a 40-line stub (an `onchainState` with `update` and `status`, a no-op `startDiagnostics`, contract functions naming `Stub`). That is the likely cause of step 0's missing `deployments.ts` on the first install too. Not diagnosed further; worth a note in the template's own work tree.

### 2026-10-01, step 1: pnpm 10

Branch `pnpm-10` from the integration branch at a4fb8f0, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/pnpm-10`. Two commits: 4bc97be (the move) and d4864bf (a hoisting fix the move needed). Node v24.19.0.

**What changed.**

- `packageManager: "pnpm@10.28.1"` in the root `package.json` (the template's). The system pnpm here is 11.25.0; it honours the field and runs 10.28.1, so step 0's caveat a (pnpm 11 refusing the lockfile, and its one-day `minimumReleaseAge`) no longer applies on a machine whose pnpm honours the field.
- The root `pnpm` field (`supportedArchitectures`, `overrides` (21 entries), `auditConfig`) moved verbatim to `pnpm-workspace.yaml`. pnpm 10.28.1 itself still read the field silently; the "no longer read" warning came from the pnpm 11 front-end, on every command, and is gone.
- Install scripts: pnpm 10 skipped 11 (`@parcel/watcher`, `@sentry/cli`, `@sveltejs/kit`, four `esbuild`s, `sharp`, `svelte-preprocess`, `vue-demi`, `workerd`). `onlyBuiltDependencies: [esbuild]`, as in the template. The other seven are in `ignoredBuiltDependencies` (so pnpm stops warning about them), each checked to work without its script, with the reason in a comment: `sharp` rendered a PNG, `workerd` 2024-06-05 and `sentry-cli` 2.32.1 answer `--version`, all from their `optionalDependencies` platform binaries; the four esbuild binaries answer a transform; `vue-demi` already defaults to Vue 3; kit's postinstall is a `svelte-kit sync`, which web's `check`, `dev` and `build` run anyway; `svelte-preprocess` only prints a reminder. The plan's other candidates do not exist here: contracts' hardhat and solc have no install script (solc is downloaded at compile time), and there is no `better-sqlite3`; libsql ships prebuilt optional binaries.
- CI (`ipfs.yml`, `github-pages.yml`): `pnpm/action-setup@v2` with `version: 9` became `@v4` with no `version:`, so it reads `packageManager`. Not run in CI (both workflows only run on push to `public`/pages); judged by reading.
- **Found: pnpm 10 dropped `*prettier*` from its default `public-hoist-pattern`**, so the root `prettier --check .` failed on `docs/.prettierrc` (`Cannot find package 'prettier-plugin-svelte'`): `docs/` is not a workspace, and only `web` declares the plugin. Fixed by declaring it at the root (`^3.2.4`, the same resolved 3.2.4 as web). Step 4 merges the root `package.json`, so keep it there.
- The lockfile: the first install with pnpm 10 was frozen and wrote nothing (pnpm 10 keeps lockfile v9, and the moved overrides match). The `pnpm add` above was pnpm 10's first write: the set of resolved packages is identical (1420 keys in `packages:` before and after), only peer contexts were re-derived: `fuzd-nodejs`'s optional `hono` peer now points at the 4.13.11 already in the tree instead of 4.6.9, and a `zod` peer context drops from viem, abitype, ox and the fuzd packages. `fuzd-nodejs --help` runs (it prints "Failed to find Response internal state key", which is `@hono/node-server` 1.13.5 probing Node 24's own `Response`, independent of the hono version).

**Baselines, from a clean install** (all `node_modules`, `common/dist`, `indexer/dist`, `web/build`, `web/.svelte-kit` removed first), against step 0:

| check                                   | step 0 (pnpm 9.15.9)                      | step 1 (pnpm 10.28.1)                                                        |
| --------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`        | exit 0, 1241 packages, no warning         | exit 0, 1241 packages, no warning, no ignored-build notice (4 s, warm store) |
| `pnpm indexer:build alpha1`             | exit 0                                    | exit 0 (6 s)                                                                 |
| `pnpm --filter ./web check`             | 0 errors, 0 warnings                      | 0 errors, 0 warnings                                                         |
| `pnpm --filter ./contracts test`        | 6 files pass, 1 skipped; 69 pass, 67 skip | the same, 62.5 s                                                             |
| `pnpm format:check`                     | clean                                     | clean (after d4864bf; failed before it, see above)                           |
| `MODE=alpha1 pnpm --filter ./web build` | exit 0, no `(!)`, 14 MB, 102 files        | exit 0, no `(!)`, 14 MB, 102 files                                           |

Surprises: the prettier hoisting change; and `pnpm --filter ./contracts test` (`pnpm compile && vitest`) does not exit when run from this agent's shell: vitest 1.x decided it was interactive and stayed in watch mode after passing (timed out at 900 s). With `CI=1` it exits 0 in 64 s. Not a pnpm effect (the same `vitest` binary and script), so step 0 presumably ran it non-interactively; step 4's criteria now say `CI=1`.

### 2026-10-01, step 3: the web toolchain the template runs on

Branch `web-toolchain` from the integration branch at cfd0528, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/web-toolchain`. Three commits:

- 393f7cf, **the versions**: `svelte ^5.56.9`, `@sveltejs/kit ^2.70.3`, `@sveltejs/vite-plugin-svelte ^7.3.0`, `@sveltejs/adapter-static ^3.0.10`, `vite ^8.2.1`, `svelte-check ^4.7.6`, `typescript ~6.0.3` in `web/package.json` (resolved: svelte 5.57.1, kit 2.70.x, vite 8.3.1, TypeScript 6.0.3). Components are untouched Svelte 4 syntax, running in legacy mode.
- 22e9d46, **what svelte-check 4 and TypeScript 6 reported**: 4 errors and 8 warnings, against 0 and 0 before. The errors: `actions/flow`'s `Step.component` was a `ComponentType`, which under Svelte 5's types cannot hold a component with a required prop (3 errors, one per commit-flow step); it is now `Component<{state: Writable<State>}>`, what `Flow.svelte` actually mounts, and `Flow.svelte` passes `$currentFlow.state` (the same store `state` is derived from, but narrowed inside the `{#if $currentFlow ...}` it renders in). And `utils/url.ts`: `void !e && (...)` is always `undefined`, since `void` binds tighter than `&&`, so its right-hand side never ran and TypeScript 6 reports it as always falsy; removed, with a comment, as the no-op it was. The warnings: five self-closing non-void tags (`canvas` 2, `label` 2, `progress`), written open/close (Svelte parses both as an empty element), and an `aria-label` on an icon-only close button.
- 4c47d43, **the template's specifiers**: `pnpm add` had written the resolved versions as floors (`typescript ^6.0.3`, `svelte ^5.57.1`, ...); now the template's exact strings, so step 4's merge of `web/package.json` agrees on these lines. The lockfile resolves the same packages.

**Left, on purpose**: 2 svelte-check warnings, both `store_rune_conflict` (`Flow.svelte`, `IndexerView.svelte` subscribe to a store called `state`). Harmless in legacy mode, a rename to fix, so left for step 11 (whose text now says so).

**Dependencies.** `web3-connection@0.1.39` declares a Svelte 4 peer but imports only `svelte/store`, which Svelte 5 keeps unchanged; the connection is exercised by both Chromium tests (the app reads the chain through it). The other packages with an unmet Svelte 4 peer (`svelte-headless-table` and its `svelte-render`/`svelte-subscribe`/`svelte-keyed`, `missiv-client`, `@zerodevx/svelte-json-view` 1.0.9) import nothing from `svelte/internal`, so their components compile in legacy mode like stratagems' own; they pass check and build, but the views that use them (transactions table, events, missiv, the JSON debug views) are not reached by the verification. The lockfile change beyond `web`: svelte-check 3 had pulled TypeScript 5.6.3, which was the peer of `syncpack`, `vitepress` and `typia`/`fuzd-nodejs` (`helper-services/fuzd`); they now see 6.0.3 (`typia` 6.11.3 declares `<5.7.0`). `fuzd-nodejs --help` still runs. `lightningcss` 1.33.0 arrived with Vite 8 and threads through vitest's and vitepress' peer contexts. Contracts, `common` and `indexer` keep their own TypeScript 5.4.5.

### 2026-10-23, step U2: built and verified as preview branches, NOT landed (awaiting the user's approval of the cascade and the pushes)

Everything below is on local branches in jolly-roger and template-commit-reveal, nothing on a node branch, nothing pushed, in any repo. Premises re-derived first: template `main` still b4d101ba, `proto/game-slot` still 4e1ee404, jolly-roger `integration` still d971344e (the template's `stem/integration`), and `offshoot-fanout status` reported the whole tree (22 nodes, 14 repos) up to date. `/tmp/stub-test.sh` and `/tmp/stub-game.ts` were gone; the old stub worktree (`proto/stub-game`) still held the stub, which was rebuilt from it.

**What was built, at each home.**

**Omitted: 140 paths**, listed in `.offshoot-omissions` in four groups, each with its reason and the step that ends it: (1) the template's contracts, deploy scripts, contract tests and `contracts/js`, until 10b; (2) its game client (`placement/`, the offline world, `routes/{play,offline}`), their tests, the framework tests that drive its fixtures (`embedded/{world,deployments}`, `canvas2d`, `context/{resume-on-gas,setup-gate}`), and its e2e suite with `playwright.config.ts`, `run-e2e-tests.sh` and `e2e-account-claims.test.ts`; (3) until step 6, the routes that read the template's context (explorer, contracts, transactions, debug, `+error.svelte`) with `test/routes`, the template's service-worker entry (two SvelteKit entries are ambiguous), its `static/_redirects` (it shadowed stratagems' `_redirects` route and failed the prerender) and brand icon, and three framework tests whose premise is the template's shell or fixture: `layers` (reads the template's `+layout.svelte`), `json` (its guard needs a stringify that only the omitted routes have), `fatal` (needs a deployment chain with no rpcUrl; Base has one); (4) `identity-boundary` until step 8 and `render-host-boundary` until step 9, both measured failing here (3 tests). The template's own list (jolly-roger's demo) was not copied: those paths are not in our stem.

**Found, and fixed outside the framework** (each says so where it is):

- **`@rocketh/export` writes a chain's `formatters` and `serializers` as JSON residue** (`{"type": "block"}`), and for Base, alpha1's chain, that is a type no viem client accepts: with the framework's `deployments.ts` exported from alpha1, `check` reported 47 errors, 19 of them this. `contracts/rocketh/config.ts` now describes chain 8453 with both `undefined` (left out, rocketh's merge puts viem's back). The residue never ran (it has no `format`). Home of the real fix: rocketh's exporter, which should drop function-valued chain fields.
- **Four packages publish types that import `eip-1193` but declare it only as a devDependency**: `@etherplay/connect` 0.14.0, `@etherplay/connect-core` 0.7.3, `@etherplay/wallet-connector-ethereum` 0.1.1, `@etherkit/tx-observer` 0.2.0. Their types resolved against stratagems' hoisted 0.5.0 (`context/core.ts`, `embedded/wallet.ts` failed); the template works because 0.6.5 is its only copy. `packageExtensions` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`. Home: those packages.
- `radiate` 0.3 (the framework's) has no `initEmitter`: stratagems' account handler imports its 0.0.x as `radiate-legacy`. `eip-1193` 0.6 types `request` by method: `utils/ethereum/gas.ts` annotates the results instead of passing a type argument, and drops an empty `params` from `eth_gasPrice`.
- `test/alias-targets` caught a **dead alias of stratagems' own**, `$external` (its directory went in 2023, 00477bec); removed. `DefaultHead` needed a `preview` in `web-config.json` (`static/preview.png`, which stratagems has).
- `test/framework-boundary`: stratagems' five files that import `$app/*` are `KNOWN_LEAKS`, each with its reason, ending with the port; the paragraph above the list that says it stays empty is the template's, and the entries say so.
- **A fresh worktree could not `check`** (step 0's caveat b): the root `prepare` now runs `scripts/stratagems-generated.mjs` before `ensure-deployments.mjs`; it generates, only when missing, `web/src/data/contracts.ts`, `indexer/src/contracts.ts`, `common/dist`, `indexer/dist` and the framework's `web/src/lib/deployments.ts` from the committed alpha1 records (`pnpm indexer:build alpha1`), and the root export scripts write the framework's file too, so both are the same chain. Without it, `ensure-deployments` exports whichever network `readdir` returns first.

**Found, not fixed** (the template's): its own `web/src/lib/game/render/README.md` fails its own `prettier --check` (a table prettier 3.9 aligns), so it is in `web/.prettierignore` here; `test/lib/context/fatal.test.ts` reads the generated deployments' chain instead of stating its fixture. Root `format:check` here covers files the template never checks, so its root READMEs and `scripts/*` are in the root `.prettierignore` (except stratagems' own script).

What the 30 are, every one: the parts list, a framework key naming each game's own module, identical because reveal-or-die was cut from the template and kept its module names (`createCycleReader({publicClient: core.publicClient,`, `createAttendanceReader`, `createCycleAdvancer(core)`, `blocksCommitting`, `holdResolvingCycle`, `holdPlanUntilBoardReleases`, `isOutOfGas: (error) => error instanceof SignerOutOfFundsError`, `grant: SIGNER_GRANT`, the storage key's `chainID`/`gameAddress` lines, `camera: config.camera`, the renderer line), plus `const account = core.account` and `const deployments = core.deployments.get()`. Getting under 20 is possible by moving the three readers into one game-side `createCycleAccess(core)` per game and having the skeleton pass `chainId` and the contract to `storageFor`, which is a reorganisation of game modules for the metric's sake rather than a removal of repetition; left for the user to decide.

**Found** (behaviour kept, the decision is reveal-or-die's maintainer's): **reveal-or-die runs the board's refresh policy twice.** `createPollingOnchainState` runs `refreshDuringReveal` and `settleBoardWhenCycleStarts` internally since the template moved them there, and `world/context.ts` runs its own pair too (its comment says they "went back to being the framework's", which is true of the code and not of the call). The two differ slightly (the poller's phase reads `isCommitPhase`, the composition's reads `twoPhase`), so this merge keeps reveal-or-die's pair in its `start` hook, unchanged, rather than silently halving its refreshes. The other differences between the compositions were WHAT, not WHEN, and became parts: reveal-or-die re-reads its stake on account change and every cycle (the template on identity change), and plans only in the play window.

**Left behind on purpose, for the landing**: the preview branches above and their worktrees; fetch refs `refs/skeleton/*` in reveal-or-die, bomber-world and stratagems (delete with `git update-ref -d` after landing); the stub worktree. At landing, template-commit-reveal's `work` branch also needs `work/tasks/backlog/the-commit-reveal-skeleton.md` moved to `done/` with its outcome, and reveal-or-die's `work` branch a finding for the duplicate refresh.

Surprises: that TypeScript could not infer the skeleton's type parameters through the factories (the fix, one record of the game's types, reads better than nine parameters would have); that the template's `with/nft-identity` line merged into a rewritten file cleanly; that the first `pnpm install` of a fresh template worktree failed in the contracts' `prepare` and the second passed; and that reveal-or-die's duplicate refresh had been hiding behind a comment that described the framework correctly.

**What was built** (template-commit-reveal, branch `commit-reveal-base` on `main` 2dd771d3, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/template-commit-reveal/commit-reveal-base`):

- cb8594a4 `contracts/src/commit-reveal/`: `CommitReveal.sol`, a library over ERC-7201 storage (`etherplay.storage.CommitReveal`, slot computed the way `Delegation`'s is and checked against it), holding the reserve, the commitments, the cycle state, the tally and who the cycle waits for, with every function `internal` and the layout `private`; `UsingCommitReveal.sol`, the convenience contract, with the config as immutables, its own `TIME`/`_timestamp()`, and the three seams `_playerOf` (address identity through the delegation library by default), `_forfeit(player, amount)` (debit the reserve by default) and `_cycle()`; and `interfaces/UsingCommitReveal{Types,Events,Errors}.sol`, which the game's own interfaces inherit so the game's ABI keeps `Cycle`, `Commitment` and every mechanism error unqualified. A game reveals through a PAIR: `_openChunk(player, digest, numActions, furtherActions)` checks and writes nothing, the game resolves, `_closeChunk(chunk, cost)` charges the bond and the reserve, moves the chain, and counts the turn on its last chunk. A missed reveal shown its chunks is the same pair opened with `_openMissedChunk` and closed with the game's penalty, capped at the bond and handed to `_forfeit`, the surplus released with the last chunk; one not shown them is `_forfeitMissedReveal(sender, id)`, which resolves `_playerOf` itself so no route can skip it, and forfeits the whole remaining bond. Events changed shape: `CommitmentRevealed` carries no actions (the base never sees them; the reference game's `Placed` does), and `CommitmentVoid(player, cycleNumber, commitmentHash, furtherActions, forfeited)` is emitted per settlement transaction, shaped like the reveal event.
- 1047db94 the reference game on it: `contracts/src/game/` keeps only `Config` (the base's plus `tokens` and `placementCost`), `Placement`, cells, zones, `_place`, `_digest` and its typed routes; `reveal` is open, place, close; `acknowledgeMissedReveal(player, placements, secret, furtherActions)` (anyone, penalty = what the placements would have cost) and `forfeitMissedReveal(player)` (player or delegate) replace the one-call settlement. The two tests that settled by a stranger in one call now settle by the player, with a stranger refused (`NotDelegate`); a new test settles a whole-chunk bond shown its two chunks, by a stranger, forfeiting 6 of 8 and returning 2, with the stranger's token balance and reserve unchanged and the player committing again.
- a4a8b3f4 `src/commit-reveal/test/CommitRevealStubGame.sol`, a second game in one contract with no router and no token: an `Order{kind, quantity}` action, a cost of `quantity * UNIT_COST` per action and a penalty of `PENALTY_PER_ORDER` per order, so cost and penalty differ; and `test/js/CommitRevealBase.test.ts`, 8 tests: the encoding, the surplus returned with the last chunk, `BondTooLow`, a chunk opened and never closed leaving the commitment untouched (the pair's misuse is loud), a two-chunk settlement by a stranger taking exactly the penalty, a half-revealed turn settled shown the rest, the chunkless route refused to a stranger and taking the whole bond for the player, and a delegate settling and being paid nothing. Teeth: settling without `_playerOf` fails 1, charging the whole bond instead of the penalty fails 2. 85814a55 made the suite stand on the base alone (no `./utils`), because it arrives in every game, whose test helpers are their own.
- cab311cf the client: `bondFor` in `placement/config.ts` bonds `ceil(n / actionsPerReveal)` whole chunks, capped at the reserve and never below the cost (the commit reads `getReserve` for the cap); `storage.ts` copies a committed turn aside under `__placement_committed_turn__` because the framework clears the submission when a reveal is missed, which is exactly when the chunks are worth most; `missed-reveal.ts` rebuilds the chain from that record, matches the head the chain holds, and settles with the remaining chunks (state `forfeit` = their cost) or without them (`forfeit` = the bond); the HUD says what settling will take; the offline world's played seats settle with the chunks they derive, falling back to `forfeitMissedReveal`. 4cb9ce41 and 0b37887a: comments that named the reference game's internals now name the base, and `AGENTS.md` says a game's contracts are its own and the base is kept (the bomber-world citation paragraph now says the citation left with the seam).

### 2026-10-25, step 10b specified

Not a step: the step prompt was asked for step 10b, whose section had no "Start here" block, so this session measured, wrote the specification above as a proposal, and asks the questions below. Branch `specify-10b` from the integration branch at 4a5696e6, worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/specify-10b`. Read-only: no install, no build, no test run, nothing pushed in any repo. The integration branch was fast-forwarded only after the user answered (below).

Premises re-derived first: stratagems `main` and `origin/main` at 5c817de4, the integration branch at 4a5696e6 and containing `origin/main`, the main checkout clean; template-commit-reveal `main` 85814a55 (10a-contract's tip), `CommitReveal.sol` 664 lines and `UsingCommitReveal.sol` 431. The `epoch` counts of 2026-10-24 still hold exactly (268 in 14 files of `contracts/src`, 14 in `contracts/test`, 117 in `common/src`, 151 in `indexer/src`, 273 in `web/src/lib/stratagems`).

**Measured, by reading** (the numbers are in the specification above):

- **What the base asks of a game, against alpha1.** The base is token-agnostic: its reserve is a number, so the game moves the tokens, and `_closeChunk(chunk, cost)` reverts `BondTooLow` when the cost exceeds the bond. alpha1's cost per reveal is `tokensPlaced + tokensBurnt` (`tokensBurnt` is never set: every placed move costs one gem, a skipped or removing move nothing), so the cost is the number of placed moves times `NUM_TOKENS_PER_GEMS`, and the bond has to cover it. A removal returns its gem and neighbours' shares are paid to wallets from the contract's balance, which the base does not see. `commit` requires the player to be waited for, which `_addToReserve` grants. Cycle numbers start at 2 as alpha1's epochs do; the base's are `uint64`, alpha1's cells store `uint24`.
- **The rule, read in Solidity rather than in `common/`.** alpha1 decides collision with `epochWhenTokenIsAdded == epoch`, and does NOT set it when an owner acts on their own live cell, so the new rule built on that record alone would still be order-dependent when an owner removes their full-life cell in the same cycle as another player's placement on it (B first: skipped against A's live cell; A first: placed on a cell that is now empty); the per-cell record must remember the cell's state before the cycle's first touch, not only whether it was placed. Life at the current cycle is order-independent, because a neighbour's change in cycle C only alters the delta from C on. And the finding that became P2: shares and generator points go to `_ownerOf` at the moment of the touch (`_propagate` pays `_ownerOf(move.position)` before `_placeColor` changes it; a just-died cell's `ownersToPay` reads its neighbours' owners when first touched; `_setCell` adds and removes points for the owner at that time), and owners change inside a cycle, so the recipient of a death's share can depend on the order, and on a `poke` landing before or after a reveal. Not measured: the scenario harness can measure it in the step.
- **The client**: `ActionHandler.ts` offers a placement on an empty cell, on the player's own dead cell, and on their own cell at any life between 0 and full (the `else` branch), a menu for their own full-life cell (removal) and only a menu for any cell another player owns, dead or alive. It always reveals with `useReserve` true, uses `permit` only when the allowance check fails (the deploy gives the game a global approval on TestTokens), never uses `furtherMoves` (a turn is capped at 30 moves), and calls the contract from six files. No web file reads `common/`'s epoch-named fields (`lastEpochUpdate`, `epochWhenTokenIsAdded`, `producingEpochs`): its 111 `epoch` identifiers are its own clock.
- **The tests**: the base's suite runs on Hardhat's node test runner with `earl` and a `test` network; stratagems' suites run on vitest, whose config would pick `test/js` up. The scenario suite already folds the chain through the indexer in process and compares it with the contract (`expectIndexedGridToMatch`), which is the differential check P7 relies on.
- **The generated files**: `scripts/stratagems-generated.mjs` exports from alpha1's committed records; the template's `ensure-deployments.mjs` falls back to `contracts/scripts/deploy-ephemeral.ts`, which stratagems already has, so P4 is a change of mode, not new tooling. The template's `contracts/src/game/internal/UsingVirtualTime.sol` stays upstream, so dropping stratagems' file at that path needs an omission entry.
- The ERC721 route's `_transferFrom` reverts `NotImplemented`, so cells cannot change hands outside reveals.

**Questions for the user** (the recommendation is the P line of the same number above):

1. P1: are "already there" cells those alive at the cycle's start and owned by another player (or Evil), with the player's own cells as today and anyone's dead cell free (a change to what the client offers)? Or should another player's dead cell stay blocked, as the client has it, which leaves it blocked until its owner re-places it?
2. P2: does the invariant cover shares, generator points and `poke` too, judged against the owners at the cycle's start, with the step designing the record?
3. P3: drop `permit` and `useReserve`; keep `poke`, the read-only ERC721, the generator and Gems, the debug route and `makeCommitmentWithExtraReserve`?
4. P4: no live deploy in this step, the generated files from an ephemeral deploy of the new contracts, and the chain and tokens decided at step 6?
5. P5: adapt the legacy client's six call sites and its storage key so `check` passes, with no runtime check of it until step 6 replaces it? (The alternative, keeping the web typed against alpha1 until step 6, leaves two exports of two different contracts in one tree.)
6. P6: two runners in `contracts/` (node test for `test/js`, vitest for stratagems' own), rather than porting stratagems' suites to node test now?
7. P7: keep the indexer's simulation, checked by the scenario harness and the replay, rather than having the contract emit cell state?
8. P8: remove `web/verify`, the oracle and the alpha1 scripts, keep every deployment folder as history, and tag `alpha1-final` after step 1's checks pass, the tag's push asked for?
9. The "not questions" bullet: any objection to the identity, the policy, the bond of one whole chunk, the burn on forfeit, the `MovesRevealed` event, or keeping solc 0.8.24 with viaIR?

Surprises: that alpha1's own-cell exemption from `epochWhenTokenIsAdded` is a second, independent order dependence, invisible in `common/`'s measurement because the client's removal and another player's placement were never combined there (the client never builds the second); and that ownership inside a cycle reaches the payouts, which `common/`'s model has no tokens to show.

**Answered, 2026-10-25**: the user accepted every recommendation ("1. P1: yes agree" through "9: ok"), so P1 to P8 and the defaults are decided as written, and the specification above says "Decided" throughout. Landed: the integration branch fast-forwarded to `specify-10b` and pushed. Step 10b is next, unticked.

### 2026-10-25, step 10b: stratagems' contracts on the base

**Step 1, the alpha1 checks, at d70e4a91.** Baselines exactly as specified: contracts 69 passing (67 skipped), node verify check 0 errors and 2 warnings, unit 1427 + 72. `pnpm indexer:index alpha1` through the caching proxy, then `compare:alpha1`: **every question answered the same** (Q1 to Q7, D1, D2, Q3a for 36 accounts; rows-examined bound 1,956). `MODE=alpha1 pnpm --filter ./web build`: passes. `verify:alpha1`: the two-tab election test **passes**; the publication-versus-self-indexed test **fails three times, by its own 40-minute bound**: the tab started from the publication is at the tip in 13 to 15 s every time, and the self-indexed tab was still catching up when the bound expired, at blocks 50,882,360, 51,182,360 and 51,382,360 of about 53,080,000 (the last run on an idle machine, with every stable RPC answer served locally). It needs about 41 M blocks from alpha1's start block; this file recorded it as "about 40 min" when the tip was near 51.95 M, so the chain's growth since has carried it past its bound. Nothing was compared wrong: the comparison was never reached. Through `https://base.etherplay.io/` (the user's node, which serves the genesis block and 200,000-block `getLogs`), with nothing else running: the full suite again missed by about 1,700 blocks (53,082,360 of 53,084,047 at the bound) while the election test passed, and a rerun of the one test then **passed**: the self-indexed tab at the tip after 2,320 s (38.7 minutes), its state equal to the publication's, both as of block 23,303,136. **So every alpha1 check passed at d70e4a91, and `alpha1-final` is an annotated tag on it**, its message listing them. The check now runs within about a minute of its own 40-minute bound and will not, at this chain height, on a slower node; nothing will run it again, since it leaves with alpha1's support (step 8).

Environment, worth knowing for any rerun: `https://mainnet.base.org` refuses `eth_getLogs` from this host ("request limit reached", even for one block) since the first fold of this session went through the proxy to it, and had not recovered four hours later. `https://base-rpc.publicnode.com` serves 2,000-block `getLogs` but refuses the genesis block as an archive request, which the app reads at boot (its account key carries the genesis hash), so a tab pointed at it stays in `loading` for ever. What worked: the proxy (`rpc-cache-proxy.ts`, its cache copied from the main checkout, 20,493 chunks) on publicnode, and a small split in front of it sending `eth_getLogs` to the proxy and everything else to `mainnet.base.org`. The split was a throwaway under `/tmp` and is gone; nothing of it is in the tree.

**Step 2** (e954decf): the base and its suite in from `stem/main` unchanged; `.offshoot-omissions` names only the reference game's suites under `contracts/test/js`; `@etherplay/delegation` ^0.1.1, `solidity-kit` ^0.5.7, `@nomicfoundation/hardhat-node-test-runner` ^3.1.0 and `earl` ^2.0.0 join; a `test` network and `paths.tests.nodejs` as the stem has them; vitest excludes `test/js`; the contracts `test` script runs both. The base's **8 tests pass** before any game code moved.

**Step 3** (a82eccd5), `contracts/test/Stratagems.order.test.ts`: every order of one cycle's calls (reveals, and now and then a poke) is sent in ONE block from one sender with consecutive nonces, so every order runs at the same timestamp and the generator's accrued rewards compare exactly as well as its points; compared: every cell of the board and its ring (all fields, owner included), each player's wallet and reserve, the game's and the burn address's balances, every account's points and rewards, and which calls reverted. A revert is the same in every order and could hide a case, so the hand cases and the replay both require that no call reverts. It runs against either ABI, so it measured alpha1 first. **On alpha1**: the collision passes; the overwrite of a live cell (one order makes it Evil, stake 2, its points moved to the Evil owner), an owner's removal against another's placement (the owner's wallet and points differ by one gem), the dying cell's share, and a poke before or after a reveal all depend on the order; the randomized replay finds **240 of 1,000** trials (seed 1) and **2,274 of 10,000** (seed 7, 0 of 181,210 calls reverted).

**Step 4** (dbe063cd, then f574bc80): the game on the base, as specified, with these measured or read in Solidity:

- the rule (P1) as decided, through a per-cell record `_cellsAtCycleStart` (owner, cycle, life before the first move on the cell in a cycle), written by that first move; until then the current record IS the cycle's start, since a neighbour's move changes neither a cell's life at the current cycle nor its owner;
- payouts (P2) go to the owners at the cycle's start, and a share to the zero address is not made (it stays in the contract; alpha1 would have sent tokens to address zero);
- **three more alpha1 order dependences, none visible to `common/`'s model**: `_poke` zeroed a just-died cell's `lastEpochUpdate`, which stopped neighbours updating it, so its `delta` and `enemyMap` (which feed points later) followed whether a poke came first; a dying cell's shares were summed in twelfths per move and divided once while a poke divided per share, and which path pays a share follows the order (a wei moved between players: 54 of 10,000 trials on the first rebuild); `_getUpdatedCell` accrued producing time for a dead cell without moving its `lastEpochUpdate`, so a poke and a neighbour's update counted one span twice, and a removed cell accrued the whole cycle number (11 of 10,000). Each is fixed and said so at the line;
- points as a HOLDING (`_setPoints`): an owner holds `NUM_TOKENS_PER_GEMS * stake` exactly while the cell is alive with a positive effective delta, and each change removes the old holding and adds the new; alpha1's case-by-case arithmetic missed a third placement on an Evil cell whose delta changed in between (found by reading);
- `useReserve` and `permit` gone, `makeCommitmentWithExtraReserve(player, hash, bond, tokensToAdd, payee)` one transaction, `withdrawFromReserve(type(uint256).max)` withdrawing what no commitment bonds, `StratagemsDelegation` (`UsingDelegation`), `MovesRevealed(player, cycleNumber, moves)` between open and close, `_forfeit` burning, `acknowledgeMissedReveal` at one gem per committed move and `forfeitMissedReveal` (player or delegate) for the whole bond, `advanceCycle` present and refused under `Timed`; Evil moves by a player are accepted as alpha1 did (not a decided rule change; the replay draws them one move in twenty and finds nothing order-dependent); `UsingVirtualTime.sol` deleted with an omission entry; alpha1's four copies of the neighbour update are one `_updateNeighbour`.

**Measured on the rebuild**: the randomized replay **0 of 10,000 trials on seed 7 and 0 of 10,000 on seed 1**, with 0 of 181,210 and 0 of 182,898 calls reverted; every hand case passes. Contracts: **83 = the base's 8 (node runner) + 75 (vitest: alpha1's 69, every one kept, plus the order test's 6)**, 67 skipped as before; no test of a removed function existed to remove. All 10 scenarios under `contracts/test/scenarios/` pass **with no expected grid changed**, their indexer comparison (`expectIndexedGridToMatch`) included.

**Step 5**: `scripts/stratagems-generated.mjs` exports the four files from a throwaway deploy of the current contracts (environment `ephemeral`, records deleted after) and builds `common/` and the indexer; `ensure-deployments.mjs` (the stem's, unchanged) then finds its file. The deploy script carries `actionsPerReveal: 32` and `cyclePolicy: 0` (Timed) and the Delegation route. No live environment was added to `rocketh/config.ts`: P4 leaves the chain to step 6, and naming one there would decide it.

**Step 6** (a130ad1a, 8ad35e88, f574bc80): `common/` and the indexer follow the contract rule for rule (the indexer has a `cellAtCycleStart` entity, handles `MovesRevealed`, the base's events with a `uint256` player written as a lowercase address, and a commitment that moves to the next chunk); `prepareCommitment` hashes `furtherActions` always. `grep -rni epoch` in `contracts/src`, `contracts/test`, `common/src`, `indexer/src`: **one comment, naming alpha1**.

**Step 7** (816d7807): the legacy client's call sites follow the ABI (the commit bonds one whole chunk capped at the reserve; `balance.ts`'s hand-written ABI too), the account key gains the game's address, the indexer's renamed `commitment.cycle` and `placements[].cycle` are read. **From a fresh worktree** at f23249f9 (`pnpm install`, whose `prepare` generated all six from the throwaway deploy): **check 0 errors and 2 warnings (step 3's), unit 1427 + 72**, and `CI=1 pnpm --filter ./contracts test` **8 passing, then 75 passed and 67 skipped**. `pnpm format:check` on every file this step touched: clean (the plan file's 10a-contract table was already unformatted and is left). `check-omissions` 140 paths covered, `check-dangling-imports` clean.

**Step 8** (55aad96d, f23249f9): `web/verify/` and `verify:alpha1`, `indexer/oracle/2026-09-29-alpha1/` and `compare:alpha1` (and the indexer tsconfig's `oracle` include) are gone, the comments citing them point at `alpha1-final`, and the stratagems-local AGENTS.md corrections are in (`cycle` in the contracts, `common/` and the indexer; the base kept; alpha1 dormant and unsupported, its records history; the exports from a throwaway deploy; the heavier checks without alpha1's). No script under `contracts/scripts/` calls a function the new contracts lack (they call `poke`, `getConfig`, and the tokens' and generator's own), so that part of P8 is empty. The other deployment folders are untouched.

**Worth knowing in step 6**: the indexer writes a player as a LOWERCASE address (it has no viem to checksum the base's `uint256`), so the composition compares owners case-insensitively; the account storage key now ends in the game's address; the indexer's `commitment` carries `cycle`, `hash` and `bond`, and `placements[]` carry `cycle`; the legacy client's six call sites compile against the new ABI and have never been run.

**Surprises, and findings**:

- **The plan's "13 framework suites" is 10.** The template has 13 suite files, three of which (`game`, `offline`, `out-of-gas`) are the reference game's; the "16" it was counted from does not exist.
- **For the template: `pending-operation.e2e.ts` assumes the contracts page opens on the game.** It does on the template because `Game` sorts first; here `Gems` does and the suite failed. Fixed locally with the fixture's own `selectContract` and a `STRATAGEMS:` note; the fix belongs upstream (the fixture already says why a suite that means the game must ask for it).
- **For the template: a game cannot import a `$env/static/public` name the framework does not know.** `test/lib/context/fatal.test.ts` mocks that module with the framework's names only, so the composition's `PUBLIC_SNAPSHOT_URI` broke its four tests; the composition reads `$env/dynamic/public` instead.
- **The stake is two wallet transactions, not the template's one.** `addToReserve` is not payable, so the rail forwards no stipend to the browser's key; on a real chain the key's self-registration then finds no gas and the setup gate's "Authorise and carry on" (the framework's top-up flow) does it in a second transaction. In the e2e the fixture funds the key, so it is one flow. A payable deposit route is a contract change: written into step D.
- **A chunk of 32 placements costs about 6 M gas**, 188 k a move (four neighbours and up to five owners' points), well under the 16,777,216 a transaction may carry since Osaka (EIP-7825, which Hardhat 3.18 enforces: the first measurement asked for 30 M and was refused). The path where neighbours die in the reveal's own cycle (token transfers) is not measured: written into step D.
- **The template's harness and the indexer met without a change to either**: the local chain mining every second is what lets the board stamp its cycle (the 404 for the embedded publication is the worker finding no snapshot on a local chain and folding from the start block, as intended).

**Left behind on purpose**: nothing running; the `step-6b` worktree.

### 2026-10-26, step S: the one-transaction stake

Asked for by the user after 6b ("do it now"), so a step of its own rather than a line in step D. Branch `step-stake` (worktree `~/dev/worktrees/stratagems/step-stake`) from the integration branch at b8214832; the main checkout clean, `main` = `origin/main`, the integration branch = its origin.

**What was done**:

- 4415c229, **the contract**: `addToReserveAndFund(uint256 player, uint256 tokensAmountToAdd, address payable stipendTo)`, payable, in `StratagemsCommit` beside `addToReserve` (which stays, nonpayable: the template's contracts e2e drives `addToReserve nonpayable` by name). It credits `player`'s reserve from the caller's gems and forwards all of `msg.value` to `stipendTo` with a `call`, refusing a value with no recipient (`StipendWithoutRecipient`) or one it cannot forward (`StipendNotSent`): the shape of the template's `StakeSale.purchase` without a price. `contracts/test/Stratagems.stake.test.ts`, 3 tests: reserve credited, gems moved, the value at the key and nothing left in the game; nothing sent with no key; a value with nowhere to go refused and nothing credited.
- d68f2f33, **the client**: `stake.ts`'s acquisition calls `addToReserveAndFund` with the rail's `stipendTo` and `stipend` (they move together, the rail's `stipendFor`), the stipend sized by the template's `signerStipend` from the chain's `expectedWorstGasPrice` (`game-config.ts`); the stake button states the gems and the gas it is about to ask for. `contracts/rocketh/config.ts` declares chain 31337's properties, as the template does: `expectedWorstGasPrice` 1 gwei and `creditsGasMultiplier` = `commitGas + revealGas` (so the key's balance reads as moves), which makes the local stipend 1 gwei x (150,000 + 7,000,000) x 100 turns = 0.715 ETH. The e2e asserts, on a fresh chain, that after the stake the key is registered with no "Authorise and carry on", the account sent exactly ONE transaction, and the key received the stipend; 9f1f43a3 annotates the run instead when the key was already registered (a retry on the same chain), since then no stipend is sent and nothing can be measured.
- 21b3e243, **a flake found on the way, and its cause measured**: 3 of about 20 full e2e runs failed the stratagems suite with a reveal that reverted at the start of the reveal phase (a missed reveal). Logging the chain's latest block against the wall clock showed the e2e chain **30 s ahead of the wall clock** before the first test: stratagems' `node` network lacked the template's `allowBlocksWithSameTimestamp: true`, so every automined transaction (the deploy's, the suites') needed a later timestamp than the last block and pushed block time ahead a second at a time, and the client's clock and the chain's cycle came apart. With it, the chain stayed within 1.1 s of the wall clock over 6 of 6 full runs, all passing. It was there in 6b too (6b's three full runs passed by luck); it is a dev-node setting and changes no test network.

**Measured**: e2e **48 of 48** (each run starts a fresh node, so the key is unregistered and the one-transaction assertion runs; a run that logged which branch it took confirmed it); node verify **from a fresh worktree** at 21b3e243: check 0 errors and 2 warnings, unit 1589 + 72 (unchanged); `CI=1 pnpm --filter ./contracts test` 8, then **79** passed (76 + the stake test's 3) and 67 skipped; `prettier --check` clean on what this step touched; nothing left running.

**Plan changes**: step S added to the Steps list (ticked) with a short section; step D loses the stake item and gains a note that a live chain needs its own chain properties (without them the stipend is zero and staking is two transactions again) and that `revealGas` sizes the stipend.

- **FINDING 2: an in-memory store cannot answer the board's GraphQL.** `MemoryStateStore` (`@etherfold/state-store`, the only in-memory store etherfold 0.12 ships) is a backend without the query seam (`accessor`, `tip`, `blockAt`, `blockOf`, `revertSequence`), so `graphqlQueryHandler` answers every query `internal-error`. **Baselines** (each `pnpm install`, check, unit): jolly-roger `main` check 0/0, unit **984** + 44; `integration` 0/0, **1289** + 60; template-commit-reveal `main` 0/0, **1784** + 78, its e2e **54 of 54** in 4.2 min (`E2E_RPC_PORT=8555 E2E_PORT=4183`), nothing left on its ports; stratagems as the merge above measured (0/0, 1694 + 72, e2e 50 of 50).

**libsql `:memory:` (fuzd's open question)**: with `@libsql/client` 0.14.0 through `remote-sql-libsql` 0.0.6, a table created in one `db.batch`, rows inserted by a second `batch` and by an `execute`, are all seen by a third `batch` and an `execute`: one database across calls. But `client.transaction('write')` hands the client's connection to the transaction and the client then sees a fresh, empty database ("no such table"). `RemoteLibSQL` calls only `execute` and `batch`, so fuzd on `:memory:` is safe today, and a future `transaction()` in fuzd's storage would silently lose the in-memory database. P12's `:memory:` default stands.

**P8's premise (the stop)**: `SubmissionStorage.load()` cannot be served by the account data before it is `ready`, and the submission as written does not wait. Measured in synqable 0.0.13 (`dist/main/createSyncableStore.js`, `dist/storage/LocalStorageAdapter.js`): every storage adapter's `load` is `async`, `createLocalStorageAdapter`'s included, and the store's `load()` awaits it before setting `ready`, so `ready` is at least one microtask after the per-account store exists, and again after every account switch; with a sync adapter (`with/sync`), `performSync` runs only after `ready`, so a record written on another device arrives later still, as a change. On the template's side (`game/core/submission.ts` at de924635): `restore()` runs once, synchronously, inside `start()`, and never again (an identity arriving later is not restored either); `reveal()` begins with `storage.load()` and returns silently when it finds nothing, so a reveal attempted before `ready` would be skipped without a word; and the account data's writes throw before `ready` (`addItem` on a store not yet loaded, the "accountData not ready" pattern). So an account-data-backed storage behind today's port would lose a restored submission at every page load, which is the loss P8 exists to prevent.

**Also found**: the secret is in `localStorage` twice, not once. `createSubmissionStorage` copies every committed turn aside under `committedPrefix` (`committedTurnKey`), and `loadCommittedTurn` serves it to the missed-reveal settlement (`placement/context.ts` line 317; stratagems' `submission-storage.ts` and `context.ts`). P8 as decided moves only the submission, which would leave the copy that pays back the bond in plain `localStorage`.

**Re-proposed, for the user** (the detail of each below):

1. Q8-1: `SubmissionStorage` stays synchronous (`load`, `save`, `clear`) and gains an optional readiness signal, `ready: Readable<boolean>`, absent meaning always ready, so the `localStorage` implementation and every game's own storage keep working untouched. The submission restores when the storage becomes ready (and again on each later transition to ready, which an account switch causes), not only in `start()`; it refuses `plan` and `commit` while not ready (the skeleton's `readyToPlay` folds it in), and a `reveal()` asked for before ready waits for it instead of returning. Rejected: an asynchronous `load()` returning a `Promise`, which makes every read in `restore`, `reveal` and the cycle subscriber asynchronous and opens a gap between reading the record and setting the state, a gap the state machine's guards (`Revealing`, `Committing`) assume does not exist.
2. Q8-2: a record that arrives AFTER ready (another device, by sync) is adopted through the existing `adopt()` when the submission is `Idle` for that cycle, the storage's readable telling the submission that the record changed. Rejected: ignoring late arrivals (the synced secret would then help only after a reload, which is the second device's whole case).
3. Q8-3: the committed-turn copy moves into the account data too, as its own field (`committedTurns`, one record per chain, contract and identity, overwritten by each commit, never cleared by the submission), so the settlement's chunks are synced and encrypted with the secret. Rejected: leaving it in `localStorage` (the secret would then be in plain storage after all), and folding it into the submission record (the submission is cleared exactly when the copy is needed).
4. Q8-4: this stays inside step 8 (item 4, where P8 (b) already sits), with item 4's unit tests extended to the readiness paths: a restore that waits, a reveal that waits, a plan refused before ready, a synced record adopted. Rejected: splitting P8 into a step of its own before fuzd, which would leave `with/fuzd`'s `extensions.fuzd` record (P8 c) nowhere to live in the meantime.

Not ticked, not landed. Next: the user's answer to Q8-1 to Q8-4, then step 8 continues at item 2.

**Answered, 2026-10-27** (the user, over several rounds; the questions grew from Q8-4 to Q8-11 as the answers raised new ones):

- **Q8-1 accepted**: `SubmissionStorage` stays synchronous and gains an optional readiness signal; the submission restores on every transition to ready, refuses `plan` and `commit` before it, and a `reveal()` asked for before it waits.
- **Q8-2 accepted, corrected**: a record that arrives after ready (another device, by sync) is adopted under `adopt()`'s own guard (not while committing or revealing, only for the current cycle), so a committed record replaces an uncommitted local plan, not only an `Idle` state. A committed record arriving after its cycle is no longer revealable, but still lets the settlement be shown the chunks (Q8-9), so only the penalty is lost, and the missed-reveal check tells the player.
- **Q8-3 replaced by Q8-9.**
- **Q8-4 accepted**: this stays inside step 8.
- **Q8-5 accepted**: the account data exposes its lifecycle as one readable (loading, ready, failed with its error), at jolly-roger; the submission storage's readiness is derived from it. synqable already has `state$` (status `idle`, `loading`, `ready` and `loadError`), so nothing changes in synqable for the signal itself; `idle` with a `loadError` maps to failed.
- **Q8-6 accepted**: the skeleton's `readyToPlay` folds the account data's lifecycle in: while loading, the board refuses plans, commit is disabled and the HUD says the saved turn is loading; on a load error the HUD shows it and play stays blocked, never an empty turn in its place. The rest of the app (the stake purchase, browsing) is not blocked.
- **Q8-7 accepted**: the transaction writer (`connectors.ts`) keeps its in-flight ledger fallback, which already catches a broadcast that cannot be filed; its comment gains not-ready as the second cause beside "the account went away". Sends are not blocked.
- **Q8-8 accepted**: item 4's tests cover the submission's cases (a read, a plan, a commit and a reveal before ready; a cycle that turns while loading; an account switch; an identity arriving after `start()`, a gap that exists today even with `localStorage`; a late synced record; two devices committing in one cycle, decided by the chain's commitment hash; a failed write; a load error). Two tabs on one account online are measured by hand. Item 2's tests cover the readable at jolly-roger.
- **Q8-9 accepted** (found by reading synqable's merge, `dist/main/merge.js`: a map item merges by timestamp, the later write winning, deletions included, on device clocks): a phone that saved a plan, or cleared its record, after a laptop committed would overwrite the laptop's committed record on both devices at sync. So the records are split by what they are worth: plans in `submissions` (one per chain, contract and identity, where the later write winning is harmless), committed turns in `committedTurns`, keyed by identity AND cycle, written once at commit and never overwritten or cleared by the submission, so nothing ever competes for a key and clock skew stops mattering. Restore and late sync look for this cycle's committed turn first. It replaces the `localStorage` committed-turn copy (`committedTurnKey`, `loadCommittedTurn`), which today holds the secret a second time, in plain storage, for the missed-reveal settlement. No automatic expiry (a settlement may come weeks later): a committed turn is pruned once its cycle is revealed or settled on chain, keeping the latest few. Rejected: a merge hook per field in synqable.
- **Q8-10, accepted in principle, its shape awaiting confirmation**: synqable's types stop letting a caller write to a store that has not loaded (the user: "nobody uses it except us"). Proposed shape: `get()`'s union carries the write methods (`addItem`, `patchItem`, `removeItem`, `set`) only on its `ready` arm, so a write needs the narrowing; a `ready()` promise resolving to the writable store (rejecting on a load error) for a writer that must wait (the submission's commit); and a distinct `error` status. Reads do not change (`get()` already narrows for `data`; `watchField` and `state$` stay). jolly-roger has 6 write sites (5 in `AccountData.ts`, 1 in `toastConnector.ts`), each already guarding by hand. Built on synqable 0.1.0 (2026-08-26, tag `synqable@0.1.0`, source cloned at `~/dev/github/wighawag/synqable`, `main` 2d4174f), which the tree has not taken (it is on 0.0.13) and which is breaking (`permanent` renamed `value`, a `record` field type added; maps merge as before, so Q8-9 holds). So the tree moves to the latest synqable as part of this, the user: "we need to move to latest". A new item before item 2: synqable's change and release, the tree's move at jolly-roger and the cascade, the account data's writers on the new shape. bleeps, conquest-v1 and mandalas also depend on synqable and are left to take it when they next update.
- **Q8-11 accepted** (measured, scripts under `/tmp/s8-libsql`): `@libsql/client` on `:memory:` loses the database after `client.transaction()` (the client opens a new connection, and each in-memory connection is its own empty database) in 0.14.0, 0.15.0, 0.15.15 and 0.17.0 to 0.17.4, and keeps it in **0.18.0** (2026-09-02); `file::memory:?cache=shared` keeps it in every version. Not found in a changelog; measured only. fuzd depends on ^0.14.0 (so never the fix) and calls no `transaction()` today, but P12 makes `:memory:` the CLI's default. So item 3 moves fuzd to ^0.18.0, with a smoke test that writes, runs a transaction and reads back on `:memory:`; stratagems' contracts (^0.17.4, `contracts/utils/indexer.ts` on `:memory:`, neither `@etherfold/state-store-sqlite` nor `remote-sql-libsql` calling `transaction()`) move to ^0.18.0 when this step touches them. Rejected: defaulting to `file::memory:?cache=shared`, a workaround that keeps the broken version.

**Q8-10 decided, 2026-10-27** (the user): the requirement (no write reachable before the store has loaded, checked by the compiler) is accepted as unavoidable: a loading screen cannot gate the writers no click drives (auto-commit, auto-reveal and the fallback on cycle ticks), a synchronous load is impossible under encryption, and a queue of early writes makes "written" mean "will be written", which a secret that must be stored before its commit is sent cannot accept. The shape is refined from the one proposed: the mutators move off the store onto a separate write handle, obtained synchronously when loaded (for a writer with a fallback, the transaction writer) or through an asynchronous `ready()` that rejects on a load error and rejects rather than hangs on a teardown or an account switch (for the submission's commit), plus a distinct error state; not mutators on `get()`'s readonly snapshot. The names are the synqable session's.

**How the work is split from here** (the user): one session in synqable (`~/dev/worktrees/synqable/typed-ready-writes`, the change and a release, each push and release asked for), then one session in the jolly-roger tree that moves the tree to the new synqable AND does item 2 (P8 a and Q8-5), since both touch `AccountData.ts` on jolly-roger `main` and so cascade once, through `offshoot-fanout` down to stratagems `main` on `s8/<branch>` preview branches. The user pastes both prompts and comes back here; this session then does the between-steps merge of stratagems `main` into the integration branch and continues at item 3 (fuzd).

### 2026-10-30, step 8: the contracts-page flake was Playwright's forced scroll meeting bits-ui's scroll button; fixed at the fixture's home; "Done when" holds (landed once the template tree's pushes are approved)

Re-derived first, all holding: the main checkout clean on `main` = origin 639967cc; the integration branch = origin d2456758; `step-8` = origin 382645cb; template-commit-reveal `main` ee68c542, `with/fuzd` 490b0e5b, `work` add0cd8d, `offshoot` d9fa9b14; stratagems' `offshoot` 95c7bdd9.

**The cause** (the decided measurement: the click reproduced under CPU throttling on both branches, against the harness's own builds, reading the DOM and the select's state at the click). A probe spec (untracked, run through `pnpm test:e2e` so the harness built, deployed and served as it always does, then deleted) recorded every pointer event with its target and `elementFromPoint`, every frame's geometry of the list (the viewport's `scrollTop`, the content's box, the option's box, the scroll-up button, running animations) and the trigger's `aria-expanded`. What it showed, in order:

1. **CPU throttling alone does not reproduce it**: 0 failures in 112 clicks through the fixture's own steps on `step-8` (rates 1, 2, 4, 6, 8 and 12). So it is not "a slow page ignores the click".
2. **Playwright scrolls the option itself.** The list opens with an animation; `DEBUG=pw:api` shows the option click finding it "not stable" twice, and the third attempt (Playwright cycles the scroll alignment on retries, the third is `block: 'start'`) scrolls the option to the top of the list, `scrollTop` 0 to 127, though the settled list shows it without scrolling. Then the move, press and release come within about a millisecond. In 5 of 6 unthrottled clicks the list was scrolled this way.
3. **That scroll's `scroll` event comes in the next frame**, and bits-ui answers it (`SelectScrollUpButtonState.handleScroll` sets `canScrollUp`): the scroll-up button mounts at the top of the list (306, 24 px) and every item moves 24 px down, the option from 311 to 335 while the pointer stays at 327, now on the button.
4. **bits-ui selects on the release of an item** (`SelectItemState.onpointerup`). It covers a release on ANOTHER item after the list moved, not a release on its scroll button, so the press is forgotten and the list stays open on `Gems`. Both failing screenshots of run k5 show exactly that state: the up button at 306, "Stratagems" highlighted at 335, the trigger "Gems", the listbox expanded.

**Reproduced every time** on both branches by doing what Playwright's third attempt does with the frame placed between the press and the release (the scroll, the move and the press pipelined on one CDP session, two frames, the release): **16 of 16 on `step-8` and 16 of 16 on `merge-main-8`**, at rates 1 and 4. The release landed on the scroll-up button (the list left open on `Gems`) in 12 and 5 of them, and in the rest the frame came before the press (the pointer on the up button, its auto-scroll, the press on `Gems`). So the defect is the base's as much as this branch's; `step-8`'s 10% more JavaScript on `/contracts` only makes a frame inside that window likelier (2 of 7 full runs against 0 of 4), which is why the earlier entry associated it with this branch.

**The fix, chosen by the cause**: the fixture's home, template-commit-reveal `main` (`web/e2e/fixtures/contracts-page.ts`, inherited by every game; reveal-or-die's page opens on `Avatars` and clicks too). `selectContract` now waits in the page until the option's box is the same for three frames and nothing in the list animates, brings the option into view itself (`scrollIntoView({block: 'nearest'})`), waits for the list to rest again, and only then clicks: no retry, no scroll by Playwright. Measured with the change on `step-8`: 160 clicks through the real fixture (rates 1 and 4, viewport heights 720 and 420), every one taken, every one on its first attempt (60 of 60 under `DEBUG=pw:api`), `scrollTop` 0 at every press. Rejected: (b) re-clicking an ignored option (it would hide whatever drops a click), (c) the page taking the contract from `?contract=` (a product change for a test's sake), (d) loading `$lib/fuzd` lazily (only the odds, and the cause is not this branch's), and patching bits-ui (a third-party gap a player does not meet: a wheel or touch scroll mounts the button the frame after its first tick, long before a human's press; recorded as a finding on template-commit-reveal's `work`, `a-release-on-the-select-scroll-button-drops-the-press.md`, not reported upstream).

**Upstream, built and verified on local preview branches, NOT pushed** (record `work:work/tasks/done/select-contract-settles-before-clicking.md` on template-commit-reveal, on its local `sel/work` 9a541b23): `main` `fix/select-contract-settles` e4d964ea (check 0/0, unit 1903 + 78, e2e **54/54**), cascaded on `sel/*`: `with/pixi-js` 07a4c5ef, `with/nft-identity` 936af967, `with/fuzd` d4ef7f42 (e2e **61/61**), `with/all` e5089f2d, reveal-or-die `main` 97fb53e7 (its contracts-page suites **23/23**), bomber-world `main` 6d056960, stratagems `main` 09f8cd63; every node's verify green, every merge a diff of the fixture alone (stratagems' a merge with no tree change). Then the between-steps merge above (`merge-main-9`, 731af093), and `step-8` replayed onto it the same way as before (67 headings, all of both copies, in date order; the old plan a byte prefix of the new; the only other difference the merged fixture; the old head tagged `step-8-pre-replay-3`).

**Measured against "Done when"**, on `step-8` after the replay (the generated pair regenerated): check **0 errors and 0 warnings**; unit **1851 + 72** (as the last entry: nothing in the replay is unit-tested); `CI=1 pnpm --filter ./contracts test` **8**, then **83 passed and 67 skipped**; `check-omissions` against d4ef7f42 covers all 70 paths; `check-dangling-imports` clean; the byte-identical `git diff --stat` against d4ef7f42 **empty**; prettier clean on every file the step touched but the plan (as recorded before). **The e2e, five consecutive full runs, every one reported** (`E2E_RPC_PORT=8555 E2E_PORT=4183`, with `E2E_GATEWAY_PLAIN_PORT=4573 E2E_GATEWAY_SW_PORT=4574` because another project's e2e held 4273 and 4274): **54 of 54** in r1 (8.2 min), r2 (8.8), r3 (8.8), r4 (8.2), r5 (9.0), the closed-page fuzd case (1.4 min) and the fallback case (1.2 min) passing in each; nothing left on the ports and no fuzd process after any run. The by-hand check of the items 6 and 7 entry stands: neither the composition nor the submission changed since. The other repos' parts of "Done when" are as that entry records.

**The harness** (throwaway, not committed): the real `PixiCanvas.svelte` from the scratch node, mounted by a small Vite page with the template's `createCamera` (stratagems' limits, 10 to 100 cells, `cellSize` 10), a renderer that builds a generated board shaped like the chosen look, pessimistically (every cell a `Container` with a stone sprite from 48 code-drawn 128-pixel textures, colour by life marks, a glow or a red rim, a pulsing pip at every zoom, a bezel on one cell in ten; one `Graphics` of bridges and notches; the ivory wash and optionally the faction wash as blurred containers; the wood as a tiling sprite and the lines), served by `vite preview` on 5299; driven by Playwright 1.62.1's headless Chromium at 1,400 by 900; 2.5 s of warm-up, then 5 s of frame intervals from `performance.now()` in `tick`, and the CPU time of pixi's `render` wrapped. Vsync and the frame-rate limit off (`--disable-gpu-vsync --disable-frame-rate-limit`), so an interval is the real cost, not 16.7 ms.

**Frame time, the harness's Chromium as the e2e runs it (WebGL on SwiftShader, software)**, median (p95), all visible at 100 cells across unless said: empty board 3.9 ms; **2,000 cells 40.3 ms (48.6)**; **10,000 cells 100.2 ms (115.2)**; 10,000 without the ivory wash 74.0; with the faction wash on 128.2; at device ratio 2, 221.9; zoomed in to 24 across (about 360 cells visible, nothing culled) 94.4. pixi's own `render` costs 0.9 ms of CPU at 2,000 and 3 to 4 ms at 10,000, and the renderer's `tick` (10,000 alphas) 0.3 ms: the rest is SwiftShader's fill rate. Zoomed in with everything else off (no wash, no links, no antialiasing, culled) 10,000 cells are still 33 ms, against 4.3 for an empty board.

**The same on this machine's GPU** (an AMD Radeon iGPU of a Ryzen 9 9955HX, `--use-gl=angle --use-angle=gl-egl`): 2,000 cells 2.7 ms (8.5); **10,000 cells 7.7 ms (20.5)**; without the wash 4.9; with the faction wash 11.0; at device ratio 2, **17.0 (34.7)**; zoomed in 4.1. With vsync on, 10,000 cells hold 16.7 ms (60 frames per second) at ratio 1.

**Culling**: pixi's `Culler` over 10,000 cullable cells costs 3.3 to 5.4 ms of JavaScript every frame and saves little: SwiftShader zoomed in 94.4 to 82.0 ms, the GPU zoomed in 4.1 to 4.5 (worse). The filters are what cost: the blurred washes are 25 to 30% of a frame on either renderer.

**pixi 8.22 without WebGL** (`--disable-webgl --disable-3d-apis`): the host as it is (no `preference`) falls back by itself to pixi's **Canvas renderer** (`renderer.name` "canvas", after a "No available adapters" warning from the WebGPU probe) and draws every sprite, the tiling wood, the lines and the bridges, with no page error. **Filters are skipped** with a warning each ("filter is not supported in Canvas2D and will be skipped"), so both washes come out as hard, opaque shapes. Frame time 19.7 ms at 2,000 cells and 71.4 at 10,000 (faster than WebGL on SwiftShader).

**For P9-2**: `autoDensity` together with the host's `resizeTo: canvas` locks the canvas at pixi's default 800 by 600 (the style it writes is what it then measures), so the smooth option is `resolution` (the device ratio) and `antialias` only, with `roundPixels` off and `image-rendering: pixelated` following the prop; the CSS keeps the size. Linear filtering per texture (`texture.source.scaleMode = 'linear'`) works under the global `nearest`, so `assets.ts` need not change: the smaller change P9-2 asked item 1 to find.

**Asked of the user before item 2** (each a recommendation, the alternative rejected):

1. 4. P9-2 (refined): the smooth prop sets `resolution`, `antialias`, `roundPixels` and the CSS `image-rendering`, never `autoDensity`; textures stay `nearest` by default, a smooth game sets `linear` per texture.
